<p> Ottawa Citizen - RSS Feedhttps : **28;2334;TOOLONG , 23 Apr 2020 13:32:29 +0000Saxophonist Hugh O'Connor , an inspiration to generations of Ottawa jazz musicians , has diedhttp : **36;2364;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;2402;TOOLONG ... title= " OTTAWA , ONT.:MARCH 3 , 2009 . -saxophonist Hugh O'Connor , recording his first CD after 60 years playing , at the Almonte Old Town Hall , 14 Bridge St . ( Bruno Schlumberger / Ottawa Citizen ) . Assign# 93999 " alt= " OTTAWA , ONT.:MARCH 3 , 2009 . -saxophonist Hugh O'Connor , recording his first CD after 60 years playing , at the Almonte Old Town Hall , 14 Bridge St . ( Bruno Schlumberger / Ottawa Citizen ) . Assign# 93999 " data-portal-copyright= " Bruno Schlumberger " **28;2438;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1610000 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Ottawa saxophonist Hugh O&amp;rsquo ; Connor was in no rush to document his deep and lyrical grasp of jazz . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A quintessential gigging musician , O&amp;rsquo ; Connor only released his debut album a decade ago , after he had turned 80 , and after benefactor Harvey Glatt subsidized the project out of his love for O&amp;rsquo ; Connor&amp;rsquo ; s creamy , fluent playing . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; At the recording session in Almonte&amp;rsquo ; s town hall , O&amp;rsquo ; Connor was characteristically self-effacing when he told this newspaper : &amp;ldquo ; &lt;span id= " hiquote16 " &gt;I 'm not really nuts about what I did . &lt;/span&gt; &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But O&amp;rsquo ; Connor&amp;rsquo ; s peers and fans , who have enjoyed what he did on Ottawa&amp;rsquo ; s bandstands since the late 1940s , were in awe of his abilities . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Now , they&amp;rsquo ; re in mourning , as O&amp;rsquo ; Connor died Tuesday at the Foyer du bonheur long-term care home in Gatineau . O&amp;rsquo ; Connor was 92 and his death was not pandemic-related , said his daughter Sharon . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Born and raised in Ottawa , O&amp;rsquo ; Connor grew up in a musical family . He picked up the saxophone before he graduated from Ottawa Technical High School and was essentially self-taught musically . After a few years of playing for dancers and accompanying floor shows , O&amp;rsquo ; Connor joined the Central Band of the Canadian Forces in 1953 and was based at CFB Rockcliffe until he retired in 1978. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; He played countless gigs after his retirement , including stints at Caf&amp;eacute ; aux Quatre Jeudis in Hull , Zo&amp;eacute ; &amp;rsquo ; s in the Ch&amp;acirc ; teau Laurier , Woody&amp;rsquo ; s on Elgin Street and Clair de Lune and Chez Lucien in the ByWard Market . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Ottawa pianist Mark Ferguson said he played thousands of gigs with O&amp;rsquo ; Connor , including CBC recordings and Ottawa jazz festival concerts . Ferguson , who is 30 years O&amp;rsquo ; Connor&amp;rsquo ; s junior , says the saxman taught him hundreds of songs simply by playing them to him and allowing Ferguson&amp;rsquo ; s ear to catch up . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Whenever a young musician asked for a lesson , which happened a lot , Hughie would say he didn&amp;rsquo ; t teach but would invite them over to play together . He taught by example and never charged a penny , &amp;rdquo ; Ferguson said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Hughie was a philosopher , &amp;rdquo ; Ferguson continued . &amp;ldquo ; He was also a rebel and had little respect for authority figures , which is amazing because he somehow lasted for 30 years in the Canadian Armed Forces band . I think they made a lot of allowances for his personality because he was such a great musician . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Ottawa guitarist Roddy Ellias recalled that O&amp;rsquo ; Connor could sing improvised jazz solos that sounded exactly like what he would play on his horn . &amp;ldquo ; He ingrained that in me , so that was my ideal &amp;mdash ; try to play what you&amp;rsquo ; re singing inside , &amp;rdquo ; Ellias said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; About a dozen years ago , when Ottawa pianist Steve Boudreau was in his late 20s and O&amp;rsquo ; Connor was in his late 70s , the two musicians played every Sunday afternoon at Chez Lucien for a year . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; He would have a genuine conversation on any topic with anybody who cared to sit down and say hi , &amp;rdquo ; Boudreau remembered . &amp;ldquo ; His interest in other people and the world in general made a lasting impression on me. &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;div&gt;Boudreau , who described O'Connor 's playing as " effortless , playful and exciting , " added : " Every time I saw Hugh he was having a good time , whether he was on the clock or not . I aspire to that kind of life . " &lt;/div&gt; &lt;p&gt; O&amp;rsquo ; Connor was predeceased by his wife , Marion , and three of his five children , said Sharon O&amp;rsquo ; Connor . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " **25;2468;TOOLONG " **32;2495;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; Peter HumThu , 23 Apr 2020 13:32:29 **45;2529;TOOLONG ... Arts'What other jazz orchestra does that ? ' : Adonis Rose comes to Ottawa to champion the music of New Orleanshttp : **33;2576;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;2611;TOOLONG ... title= " Adonis Rose , Artistic Director of New Orleans Jazz Orchestra . " alt= " Adonis Rose , Artistic Director of New Orleans Jazz Orchestra . " data-portal-copyright= " " **28;2647;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1563989 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; The rhythms of New Orleans flow through drummer Adonis Rose&amp;rsquo ; s veins. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The sashaying grooves of the birthplace of jazz are in Rose&amp;rsquo ; s blood , as surely as they were passed down from his father , drummer Vernon Severin , who in turn had a drummer , Wilfred Severin , as his father . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Rose , who is in his mid-40s , took up a prime role as a musical leader in his hometown a few years ago . After playing in the New Orleans Jazz Orchestra for 15 years , Rose in 2017 became the 18-piece big band&amp;rsquo ; s artistic director . He&amp;rsquo ; ll be at the helm ( and behind his drum kit ) when NOJO plays the Shenkman Arts Centre in Orl&amp;eacute ; ans on Tuesday , presenting a concert focused on the music of the great New Orleans composer Allen Toussaint. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Below , Rose discusses the cultural mission of NOJO and what makes it unique . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Q : When and why did you start playing with NOJO ? &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A : I started playing with NOJO in 2002 when the organization was formed . It was important to me to be a member of NOJO because of what it represents . NOJO is a non-profit organization that preserves , educates and exposes the world to New Orleans music and culture by way of an 18-piece jazz orchestra . When I was a kid growing up in the city , it was rare to see a big band perform , and we definitely did not have an institute dedicated to preserving big band culture . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Q : What kind of satisfaction do you take in playing with a big band , compared to playing in a small group ? &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A : Performing in a big band is much different than playing in a small group . Roles are more defined . Navigating through a piece of music with 18 musicians requires a different level of concentration and skill . As a drummer in a big band , you must be fundamentally sound to properly lead and direct the band musically . If you have bad time , are unable to set up rhythmic figures , or not able influence the music dynamically , you will be exposed . In a smaller configuration , a lot of times drummers can mask their shortcomings by overplaying. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Q : Now that you 're NOJO 's artistic director , what has that meant for you and the band ? &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A : This position has taught me how to be a good administrator , leader and musician . I now have to work with a staff and board , create budgets , curate concert seasons , develop educational programming , create budgets , develop recording concepts , work with booking agents and managers , rehearse and tour the band and whatever is required for us to be successful as an organization . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; For the band , it has obviously meant a change in leadership style , musical content and an overall different approach . I&amp;rsquo ; ve always been a team player and I look to the musicians in the orchestra for guidance and I value their input musically . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Q : What makes NOJO stand out , compared to other well-known U.S. big bands ? &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A : I guess we can ask what makes New Orleans different than any other city in the U.S. ? &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; NOJO is comprised of 18 musicians that live and work in New Orleans , and contribute to the local music scene and the advancement of our culture in the States and around the world . We focus on preserving the music of New Orleans artists and we embody what New Orleans is every time we hit the stage . What other jazz orchestra does that ? &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Q : What music will you play in Ottawa , and why ? &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A : I&amp;rsquo ; m really looking forward to our show in Ottawa . We will be performing the music from our new CD Songs : The Music of Allen Toussaint . Allen Toussaint fans will hear some of his biggest hits , Working in the Coal Mine , Southern Nights , Ruler of My Heart , It&amp;rsquo ; s Raining and others . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Q : I 'm interested in the New Orleans perspective on jazz as it 's played outside of where it was born . What do you think of the strands of contemporary jazz that comes out of New York , or Brooklyn , or for that matter , Europe ? &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A : I think the music has changed over the years and I think evolution is inevitable . I believe that learning the fundamentals and history of the music is imperative to understanding how to play it . But that shouldn&amp;rsquo ; t impede advancement . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; We have many leaders that make great contributions to jazz globally through performances , leading cultural institutions and presenting jazz artists around the world . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; This responsibility falls on all that are committed to providing opportunities for audiences to hear and appreciate the music and the artists that create it . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; New Orleans Jazz Orchestra &lt;br&gt; When : Tuesday , Feb. 25 , 8 p.m. &lt;br&gt; Where : Shenkman Arts Centre , 245 Centrum Blvd , Orl&amp;eacute ; ans &lt;br&gt; Tickets : $58 , ( premium $65 , student $20 ) at shenkmanarts.ca &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; phum@postmedia.com &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;ALSO IN ARTS&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-family:Calibri ; " &gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;2677;TOOLONG ... and hope to fill NAC when Nova Scotia 's Jenn Grant **31;2712;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;2745;TOOLONG ... style= " font-family:Calibri ; " &gt;NAC preview : Play explores what happens when four people with opposing political views sit down for **29;2780;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;2811;TOOLONG ... style= " font-size:12pt ; font-family:Calibri ; " &g; ... Soundclash is back in Ottawa with their first new music in almost a **29;2846;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; Peter HumFri , 21 Feb 2020 17:17:38 **45;2877;TOOLONG ... ArtsPianist Rafael Zaldivar spins musical innovations from of his Afro-Cuban rootshttp : **35;2924;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;2961;TOOLONG ... title= " Montreal pianist Rafael Zaldivar " alt= " Montreal pianist Rafael Zaldivar " data-portal-copyright= " Mathieu Rivard " **28;2997;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1560016 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; When Rafael Zaldivar was five years old and growing up in Cuba , he took his first music lessons from his grandparents . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Now based in Montreal , Zaldivar then was decades away from becoming a Juno-nominated pianist who has worked and rubbed shoulders with some of contemporary jazz&amp;rsquo ; s biggest names . But the roots of the music that he makes today , and will perform Saturday at the National Arts Centre&amp;rsquo ; s Fourth Stage , are nonetheless connected to his earliest experiences in music . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Music offered me a unique opportunity to progressively increase my cultural introspection , &amp;rdquo ; says Zaldivar , who is in his mid-30s. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; At the Fourth Stage , Zaldivar will present his Afro-Cuban Revival band , a sinewy , earthy project that melds the rhythms of his homeland and Africa with the sophistication of jazz . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **34;3027;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;3063;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; Before Zaldivar took up the piano , he got a grounding in rhythm through percussion instruments . But eventually the piano won out . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Zaldivar says that the piano , too , is a percussion instrument , but it also offers him &amp;ldquo ; the full range of orchestration&amp;rdquo ; of rich harmony and melodies. &amp;ldquo ; It strongly appealed to me and I never gave the piano up since then . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; At 13 , Zaldivar fell under the sway of some truly masterful Cuban pianists : Chucho Vald&amp;eacute ; s , Gonzalo Rubalcaba , and Gabriel Hernandez. &amp;ldquo ; They had all developed a strong voice through an Afro-Cuban rhythmic ideal in jazz composition and improvisation , &amp;rdquo ; Zaldivar says . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Then , when U.S. jazz great Herbie Hancock visited Havana in 1998 , Zaldivar got a chance to speak to him . Hancock , Zaldivar says , spoke of the importance of jazz&amp;rsquo ; s bebop-based piano lineage. &amp;ldquo ; Since then , I have been involved with black-work-songs , blues , gospel , swing , and bebop songs . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Almost 20 years later , Zaldivar dug deeper into bebop through the veteran New York-based pianist and teacher . &amp;ldquo ; He completely illuminated my mind , &amp;rdquo ; Zaldivar says . &amp;ldquo ; He was a rich bible of knowledge in developing jazz roots through bebop. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; These models inspired me to understand the meaning of jazz and taught me how to try to stretch innovation out from roots , &amp;rdquo ; Zaldivar says . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; By then , Zaldivar had come to Canada . He immigrated to Sherbrooke , Que. in 2005 , at the age of 22 . He completed a B.A. from the Universit&amp;eacute ; de Montr&amp;eacute ; al in 2009 , and an M.A. from McGill University in 2011. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; That year , Zaldivar&amp;rsquo ; s debut disc Life Directions won two prestigious Quebec music awards &amp;mdash ; the Prix Opus and the Grand Prix Musical Archambault . In 2013 , his sophomore album , Drawing , was nominated for a Juno Award , vying for the best contemporary jazz album . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; His musical profundity and virtuosity has also afforded him opportunities to play with some cutting-edge American jazz artists , including saxophonists Steve Coleman and Greg Osby , guitarist Nir Felder , and drummers Ari Hoenig and Dan Weiss. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Zaldivar speaks philosophically about the place of music in his life . &amp;ldquo ; Music reflects the health of my soul and brings the colours of my emotional scale out of my life experiences , &amp;rdquo ; he says . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; In Canada , music reinforced my human values and helped me to find my place , &amp;rdquo ; he continues . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Now , I have accomplished most of my professional dreams . Music is the highlighted line that drives my healthy and successful life . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; For Zaldivar , his Afro-Cuban Revival project signifies &amp;ldquo ; being conscious of what cultural background and genetic line mean to our lives . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; The musical and cultural virtues that I want to share with listeners are the circularity of rhythm and the spirituality of human values through Afro-Cuban songs or prayer , &amp;rdquo ; &amp;nbsp ; he says . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Rafael Zaldivar -- Afro-Cuban Revival&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;br&gt; &lt;strong&gt;When : &lt;/strong&gt; Saturday , Feb. 15 , 8:30 p.m. &lt;br&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Where : &lt;/strong&gt; NAC Fourth Stage &lt;br&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Tickets : &lt;/strong&gt; nac-cna.ca or the NAC box office &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " **25;3099;TOOLONG " **32;3126;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;nbsp ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;ALSO IN ARTS AND LIFE&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " http : **34;3160;TOOLONG ... preview : Fronteras lights expose the darkness of borders , barriers , surveillance culture&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;3196;TOOLONG ... crap ' : Ottawa woman who went viral for pizza box video makes cameo in Oscar-winning movie Parasite&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;3231;TOOLONG ... is getting a new food and wine festival at the Shaw Centre&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; Peter HumThu , 13 Feb 2020 23:24:15 **45;3266;TOOLONG ... ArtsOttawa winter jazz preview : Saxophonist Allison Au found musical inspiration in her biracial ancestryhttp : **32;3313;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;3347;TOOLONG ... title= " Juno Award-winning saxophonist Allison Au " alt= " Juno Award-winning saxophonist Allison Au " data-portal-copyright= " " **28;3383;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 309835 " /&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 100% " height= " 100% " frameborder= " 0 " allowfullscreen= " true " src= " https : **34;3413;TOOLONG ... &gt; &lt;/iframe&gt; &lt;p&gt; Allison Au says she cried a lot while writing her most ambitious work to date , which she calls Migrations Suite . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Juno-winning Toronto-based saxophonist drew upon the inspirations of her ancestry , poring over her grandparents&amp;rsquo ; photos before setting notes to paper . On her father&amp;rsquo ; s side , Au , who is 33 , is Chinese , while on her mother&amp;rsquo ; s side , she is Jewish . The result is a work that teems with lyrics telling of migration , set to the accompaniment of an expanded ensemble that combines jazz and classical elements . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Au will be playing with her quartet Saturday night at the TD Ottawa Winter Jazz Festival . While Au&amp;rsquo ; s core band doesn&amp;rsquo ; t yet have arrangements of this new work in its repertoire , the group can be expected to deliver stirring and original contemporary jazz nonetheless . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Below , Au discusses her most personal music efforts yet . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Q : When did you come up with the idea to write your Migrations Suite ? What was your goal with this work ? &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A : When I was commissioned by the Royal Conservatory of Music and the 21C Music Festival , I was informed that my band would be premiering the music in an opening slot for Danilo P&amp;eacute ; rez&amp;rsquo ; s Global Messengers Ensemble . I intentionally tried to find a way to complement Danilo&amp;rsquo ; s Global Messengers , an ensemble that draws from various musical traditions and showcases a unique instrumentation . So I set out to find a musical theme that would be global in scope . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; As I was brainstorming , I knew I wanted to address subject matter that was unique to me , something that I could share that would feel authentic and genuine . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; I didn&amp;rsquo ; t anticipate the music would become so personal . But it wasn&amp;rsquo ; t long before I realized that I could speak to my own family history , because my ancestry is international in breadth and dimension . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; By simply tracking the movements of my parents and grandparents over the last century , I could address the larger issues of displacement , relocation , lament for the loss of home , the feeling of &amp;ldquo ; foreign-ness , &amp;rdquo ; and legacy . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; By exploring my family history through music , my goal is to show how these stories are not unique . The human act of migrating is complex , but universal . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Q : How does the suite fit into your body of work ? &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A : This suite marks a new phase in my growth as a composer . I sought to create canvasses of new textures with an expanded instrumentation that included vibraphone , violin , cello and vocals , in addition to the quartet . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Because of the limited time frame I was allocated for the performance , I forced myself to keep the pieces relatively short . So the songs , as a result , have a more through-composed feel , even though there are improvised sections within them . This is an element that varies from my other work . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Throughout the writing process , I also imagined I was writing for a classical chamber ensemble , and that really pushed me further in a new compositional direction . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Throughout , the voices of my bandmates , Todd Pentney , Jon Maharaj and Fabio Ragnelli , remained incredibly strong in my mind while composing . I could hear each of them playing the parts as I was writing them , and this aspect of my writing process has always been consistent in the creation of all my music . I really try to envision the musicians performing the work as I write it . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Q : Tell me more about your compositional process . How did it draw on your family 's story ? &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A : The writing process for this music was a very emotional one for me . I scoured my parents&amp;rsquo ; photo cabinet at home and reflected on almost every faded photograph I could find . I wanted to know who everyone was in each photo . I spent two weeks bombarding my parents and sister with questions about my grandparents , clarifying and verifying the stories I had heard as a child . Were they how I remembered them ? What changed , if anything ? The stories ? Or hearing the stories through adult ears ? &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Reflecting and meditating on the trauma experienced by my grandparents was painful . I cried a lot while writing this music . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Also , Migrations Suite is my first original work that prominently features a vocalist . And what&amp;rsquo ; s more , I&amp;rsquo ; m not a lyricist . So for this project , I decided to borrow the words of poets and set their words to my music . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; I made a trip to the Toronto Reference Library and spent one afternoon poring over the poetry of Indigenous Canadian , African American and other immigrant North American poets &amp;mdash ; writers who are all too familiar with the experience of &amp;ldquo ; other-ness , &amp;rdquo ; of &amp;ldquo ; foreign-ness. &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; I made an effort not to use writers who speak to the exact experience of my Chinese or Jewish ancestry to show that other ethnic and religious communities have shared in the same experience as my family . I intentionally wanted to show the universality of the experience of these complex issues . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Q : Have your parents heard this music ? If so , what do they think ? &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A : Yes ! My parents were able to attend the premiere last Saturday at Koerner Hall . It was so special to me to have them there . They told me they both loved it . I&amp;rsquo ; m not exactly sure if they hear their lives in the music per se , but they told me they really enjoyed the music as a sonic journey . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Overall , I think they were thrilled I would be inspired to write something inspired by our family story . They were really happy that I wanted to honour my ancestry in this way . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Q : How do you think this work resonates for others who have different family stories ? &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A : If not us or our own family , we all know someone who is from somewhere else . Especially in Canada . Living in a country that prides itself on opening its doors to newcomers , the stories of migration are so prevalent . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; I think the work resonates with anyone who has either endured the immigrant experience firsthand or deeply understands what it means to leave your home , leave behind family , learn a new language , feel alienated and alone , endure prejudice and discrimination and forge a new home in a new land or community . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It is joy and pain , fear and excitement , regret and optimism . This is the human experience . This is universal . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Allison Au Quartet &lt;br&gt; TD Ottawa Winter Jazz Festival &lt;br&gt; When : Saturday , Feb. 1 , 10:30 p.m. &lt;br&gt; Where : Arts Court Theatre &lt;br&gt; Tickets : ottawajazzfestival.com &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; phum@postmedia.com &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; **33;3449;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;nbsp ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;3484;TOOLONG ... things you need to know about the 2020 TD Ottawa Winter Jazz Festival&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;3519;TOOLONG ... Lovett , John Hiatt return to NAC in April&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;3554;TOOLONG ... Out : There 's celebrity-chef sizzle at Grill 41 , but also disappointing , overpriced food&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; Peter HumFri , 31 Jan 2020 20:45:14 **45;3589;TOOLONG ... Arts5 things you need to know about the 2020 TD Ottawa Winter Jazz Festivalhttp : **32;3636;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;3670;TOOLONG ... title= " Becca Stevens . " alt= " Becca Stevens . " data-portal-copyright= " Matthew Murphy " **28;3706;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1549049 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span class= " s1 " &gt;The breadth of offerings at this weekend 's TD Ottawa Winter Jazz Festival is especially striking , ranging from funk to free-jazz to demonstrations of cutting-edge tech deployed in the service of music . Peter Hum sifts through the three-night event 's 17 concerts for highlights . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; 1 ) Joel Frahm will be playing &amp;mdash ; not once , but twice . Simply put , the Brooklyn-based saxophonist is a world-class musician who always deploys his virtuosic technique in the service of unfettered , hyper-melodic improvising . But you knew that already if you heard his majestic playing last summer on the TD Ottawa Jazz Festival&amp;rsquo ; s main stage in pianist Brad Mehldau&amp;rsquo ; s quintet , or later that night at the festival&amp;rsquo ; s jam session . On Thursday at 6:30 p.m. in the Arts Court Theatre , Frahm will play duets with Hamilton-based pianist Adrean Farrugia , recalling the pair&amp;rsquo ; s 2018 album Blue Dharma . On Friday at 9 p.m. , Frahm returns to the theatre with his freewheeling trio , which includes Brooklyn-based bassist Dan Loomis and Toronto-based drummer Ernesto Cervini. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **34;3736;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;3772;TOOLONG &lt;div class= " excerpt " &gt; &lt;p&gt;2 ) Saturday night 's big shows will feature two up-and-coming female musicians who make engrossing , distinctive music . U.S. singer-songwriter Becca Stevens smartly and movingly fuses folk , pop and jazz better than anyone else , and would be a star if the music world were more fair and less ruled by lowest common denominators . Stevens plays with her band at the Ottawa Art Gallery at 9 p.m . At 10:30 p.m. the young Juno-winning Toronto saxophonist Allison Au brings her quartet to the Arts Court Theatre . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **34;3808;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;3844;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **32;3880;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;3914;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt;3 ) Tech-assisted jazz will be big at the festival . Take U.S. pianist Dan Tepfer , a bonafide jazzer who also wrote a computer program to make his high-tech grand piano play along interactively with him , effectively allowing him to play mind-blowing improvised duets with software . Tepfer will give a free lecture on his " Natural Machines " project Saturday at 4:30 p.m. , preceding his 7:30 p.m. concert . An allied artist is Clarice Jensen , who on Thursday at 10:15 p.m. in Club Saw will create evocative , trance-y music with not just her cello , but also effects pedals , tape loops and multi-tracking . On Friday at 11 p.m. , Dutch band Tin Men and The Telephone is a piano trio that incorporates live electronics , projected visuals , and even audience participation and inspiration through a cellphone app. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **34;3950;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;3986;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **34;4022;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;4058;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **34;4094;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;4130;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt;4 ) Funk-lovers have two choices at the festival . The spectacular Toronto drummer Larnell Lewis and his band will play Friday at 6:30 p.m. in the Arts Court Theatre , and will likely deliver the same kind of intense , groovy satisfactions that made their NAC Fourth Stage concert last summer such a big deal . Once again , the festival will assemble a revue of Ottawa-area vocalists bolstered by a crack back-up band to pay tribute to a funky musical legend . While past years saw the 33Z project salute Aretha Franklin , Michael Jackson and Prince , in 2020 the program will consist of Stevie Wonder 's music when the group plays Arts Court Theatre Thursday at 9 p.m. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **34;4166;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;4202;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt;5 ) Local musicians will enjoy the festival 's limelight . In addition to the crowd-pleasing 33Z concert , Ottawa guitarist Roddy Ellias and Montreal-based piano wizard Jean-Michel Pilc will take a leap into the unknown with a concert of free improvisations on Friday at 8 p.m. at the ODD BOX in the Ottawa Dance Directive ( 2 Daly Ave . ) . On Thursday at 8 p.m. , the same venue will host the sturdy mainstream quartet co-led by Ottawa guitarist Garry Elliott and pianist Steve Boudreau . Also , Ottawa expat Pierre-Yves Martel will bring his provocative avant-garde chamber-jazz group Quartetski to the ODD BOX on Saturday at 9 p.m. to perform its re-imagining of experimental composer John Cage 's work Four6. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **34;4238;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;4274;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt;For more festival info : &lt;a href= " http : //ottawajazzfestival.com " &gt;ottaw ... **27;4310;TOOLONG IN **34;4339;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;4375;TOOLONG ... ( The Independent ) &amp;ldquo ; Her awe-inspiring voice and melancholic sensuality captivated the audience right from her very first note and did not let go over the full one and a half hours of an impressively spell-binding performance . &amp;ldquo ; ( S&amp;uuml ; ddeutsche Zeitung ) &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;KAMA Kollektiv ( NL ) &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;br&gt; Kirsi-Marja Harju ( tpt , voc , mbira ) , Jetse de Jong ( p ) , Jonathan Nagel ( db ) , Yoad Korach ( dr ) &lt;br&gt; ( Kirsi-Marja &amp;ldquo ; Kiki&amp;ldquo ; Harju ) tells stories &amp;ndash ; some in Finnish , some in English &amp;ndash ; about love and transformation , forests and animals , small everyday pleasures , and a mystical , romantic view of nature . The music is a mesmerizing mixture of jazz , Scandinavian folk and indie pop . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **34;6329;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;6365;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Kasia Pietrzko Trio ( PL ) &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;br&gt; Kasia Pietrzko ( p ) , Andrzej Swies ( db ) , Piotr Budniak ( dr ) &lt;br&gt; The artist has been nominated for a Fryderyk Award in 2018 , in the Debut of The Year category , and is ranked as the second New Hope in the monthly Jazz Forum . ( &amp;hellip ; ) In the opinion of distinguished Polish jazz musicians , she is characterized by her improviser&amp;rsquo ; s instinct , harmonic imagination and &lt;br&gt; tonal creativity . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **34;6401;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;6437;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Kristina Barta Event Horizon&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;br&gt; Kristina Barta ( p ) , Jan Feco ( db ) , Marek Urbanek ( dr ) , Nela Dusova ( sax ) , Krystof Tomecek ( g ) &lt;br&gt; ( The ) Project Event Horizon is an original trio enriched with guitar and saxophone . This band plays original contemporary creative jazz , reflecting on Kristina&amp;rsquo ; s earlier classical music studies with an emphasis on melodies , modern rhythms and electronic elements . &lt;/p&gt; With ist unorthodox orchestration ( amplified guitar , cello &amp;hellip ; ) , the ensemble shifted towards a new form of &amp;ldquo ; chamber jazz&amp;ldquo ; ( &amp;hellip ; ) while using all the expected attributes of &lt;br&gt; gypsy jazz ( rhythmic energy , melodic grandiosity ) amplifying its virtue and modernizing its codes . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **33;7071;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;7106;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;&lt;br&gt; Torun Eriksen ( NO ) &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;br&gt; Torun Eriksen ( voc , p ) , Kjetil Dalland ( el-b ) , Eivind Fossnes Dahl ( sound ) &lt;br&gt; Torun ( &amp;hellip ; ) has since ( her debut ) made her mark as a unique voice , with an ever-growing audience on the Norwegian and international music stages . Fall 2018 , Torun released her sixth solo album ( which ) received accolades in the Norwegian and German press , with critics describing it ( &amp;hellip ; ) &amp;ldquo ; A Musical &lt;br&gt; gem&amp;ldquo ; ( Jazz Podium ) . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **34;7142;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;7178;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " **25;7214;TOOLONG " **32;7241;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; Peter HumThu , 09 Jan 2020 18:03:25 **45;7275;TOOLONG ... jazz guitar master Vic Jurishttp : **35;7322;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;7359;TOOLONG ... title= " Guitarist Vic Juris at the NAC 's Fourth Stage in Ottawa , Ont. , Feb. 28 , 2009. " alt= " Guitarist Vic Juris at the NAC 's Fourth Stage in Ottawa , Ont. , Feb. 28 , 2009. " data-portal-copyright= " Christopher Pike " **28;7395;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1531890 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Vic Juris , a jazz guitarist of astounding abilities perhaps best known for his 23-year tenure in David Liebman&amp;rsquo ; s band , died Tuesday morning , according to a message on his Facebook page attributed to his friend and fellow guitarist Dave Stryker. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The New Jersey-based Juris , was 66 . He was diagnosed six months ago , after unexplainable fatigue on his gigs , with metastatic liver cancer . A GoFundMe campaign launched soon after raised more than $110,000 from fans and friends to help cover his treatment and living expenses . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Juris played in Ottawa in 2008 with Liebman&amp;rsquo ; s group at the now-shuttered Cafe Paradiso on Bank Street , and that appearance led to further gigs and concerts here with Ottawa musicians John Geggie and Roddy Ellias. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In 2009 , before his National Arts Centre Fourth Stage concert Juris , Geggie said : &amp;ldquo ; Lots of people are aware of guitarists like John Scofield , John Abercrombie , Pat Metheny , Bill Frisell and Kurt Rosenwinkel , &amp;rdquo ; Geggie says . &amp;ldquo ; I feel Vic should be added to that exclusive list . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Geggie said that Juris&amp;rsquo ; s playing at Paradiso with Liebman had completely knocked him out . &amp;ldquo ; The fluid lyricism , the subtle phrasing , the engaging harmonies , the way he used his pedals as well as the classical guitar . They were revelations for me. &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Juris , who was as humble and down-to-earth as he was talented , simply told this newspaper in 2009 : &amp;ldquo ; I&amp;rsquo ; ve played with just about everybody I wanted to play with , which is really a good thing . And , you know , there&amp;rsquo ; s not room for everybody at the forefront . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Juris&amp;rsquo ; s peers had the highest respect for him . The late John Abercrombie called him &amp;ldquo ; the greatest &amp;hellip ; there is none better anywhere . &amp;rdquo ; Metheny credited Juris for &amp;ldquo ; inventing new ways of thinking about jazz , harmonically and melodically. &amp;rdquo ; Frisell called him &amp;ldquo ; a total monster . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; On Juris&amp;rsquo ; s Facebook page , Stryker wrote : &amp;ldquo ; I am sorry to report that Vic Juris passed away this morning . Vic will live on through his music , as a beautiful person and through his many students . &amp;rdquo ; Here are Stryker , on the left , and Juris , on the right , playing a duet a few years ago : &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **34;7425;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;7461;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; Geggie says he decided to invite Juris to be part of his series after hearing the guitarist perform last April as part of saxophonist Dave Liebman&amp;rsquo ; s dynamic quartet at Caf&amp;eacute ; Paradiso. &amp;ldquo ; The whole band was stunning &amp;hellip ; a life-changing event for a lot of people , &amp;rdquo ; the bassist says . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; When my former colleague Doug Fischer interviewed Juris in 2009 , he put it to the guitarist that his stylistic versatility and range kept him from acquiring the profile of his more famous contemporaries . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Juris replied : &amp;ldquo ; There might be something to that theory . have always been interested in all kinds of music and styles . I like crossing boundaries . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; I had just a few very pleasant conversations with Juris , and this on-the-record exchange in early 2009 : &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;b&gt;Q : What 's the difference between good music and bad music ? &lt;/b&gt; &lt;br&gt; A : What I decide I like and what I decide I don&amp;rsquo ; t like . I am at a point in my life where either I like something or I don&amp;rsquo ; t like it . And that can happen for a variety of reasons , but there is a connection or there is not . Whether it&amp;rsquo ; s good or bad is really up to the individual . The listener determines that . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;b&gt;Q : If you could have dinner with any jazz figure , dead or alive , who would it be , and why ? &lt;/b&gt; &lt;br&gt; A : Wes Montgomery . He&amp;rsquo ; s always been my favourite guitarist . I think he&amp;rsquo ; d be an interesting guy to hang out with , to spend some time with , talking and maybe jamming a bit . Probably he&amp;rsquo ; d give me a lesson . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;b&gt;Q : What 's the main thing you 're working on these days ? &lt;/b&gt; &lt;br&gt; A : I am working with a teacher in Boston named &lt;a href= " http : **34;7497;TOOLONG ... title= " Charliebanacos.com " target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;Charlie Banacos&lt;/a&gt; . We&amp;rsquo ; re working on all kinds of different harmonic and rhythmic challenges . It&amp;rsquo ; s been going on for years . He&amp;rsquo ; s a well-known teacher up there . We do it by correspondence . He sends me the music . We send cassette tapes back and forth . I guess it&amp;rsquo ; s old fashioned but cassettes are such an easy format . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;b&gt;Q : What are your three desert island recordings ? &lt;/b&gt; &lt;br&gt; 1 ) The Beatles , &lt;i&gt;Rubber Soul&lt;/i&gt; . Decade after decade I keep going back to it . The songs on that record are just incredible . It was kind of a departure for the Beatles . They played a more acoustic thing and it&amp;rsquo ; s just all so fantastic . &lt;br&gt; 2 ) Jimmy Smith and Wes Montgomery , &lt;i&gt;Dynamic Duo&lt;/i&gt; . It&amp;rsquo ; s just a classic . Pure joy . &lt;br&gt; 3 ) John&amp;rsquo ; s Coltrane&amp;rsquo ; s **26;7533;TOOLONG . It&amp;rsquo ; s the Bible for ballad playing . I go there for inspiration all the time . It never sounds old . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;b&gt;Q : If you were n't a musician , what do you think you 'd be doing ? &lt;/b&gt; &lt;br&gt; A : Oh , probably photography . I don&amp;rsquo ; t do it as much as I like to , but it&amp;rsquo ; s always been something I want to pursue , to get better at . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Born Victor Jurusz in Jersey City , New Jersey , Juris took up the guitar in the early 1960s after listening to his father&amp;rsquo ; s Chuck Berry and Howlin&amp;rsquo ; Wolf records . Soon he was swept up in the rock explosion , especially the British invasion , and like many teenagers of the era he played in rock bands . He listed Beatles&amp;rsquo ; songs among his favourites . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In the early &amp;rsquo ; 70s , he landed a jazz gig with bop-rooted organist Don Patterson , an encounter that led to work with Jimmy Smith , Wild Bill Davis and eventually regular stints in the bands of Phil Woods , Dizzy Gillespie and Chico Hamilton . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; By the mid-&amp;rsquo ; 70s , he was juggling jazz with fusion , performing with Barry Miles and Eric Kloss , and first encountering Liebman , who was also heavy into fusion . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In the mid-&amp;rsquo ; 80s , Juris had a year-long stint in a guitar duo with Larry Coryell , and later teamed up with the young gypsy guitar improviser Bir&amp;eacute ; li Lagr&amp;egrave ; ne for a European tour and a recording . Among many other projects , he was a member of the Gary Peacock Quartet and musical director of a guitar quintet devoted to the music of Charles Mingus. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; He joined Liebman&amp;rsquo ; s band in 1991 , and taught at Rutgers and Lehigh universities . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; On Facebook , Liebman wrote Wednesday morning : &amp;ldquo ; There was no one more real then Vic both as a person and musician . His sense of humour , honesty , sweet nature and musical skills were legendary . Vic was a &amp;lsquo ; nice guy&amp;rsquo ; in the true sense of the cliche , always ready to give a helping hand . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I was best man at Kate and Vic&amp;rsquo ; s wedding while he spent twenty-three years with me going around the world to present our music . He was the hardest worker I have known , bringing a tape recorder to rehearsals , imploring me to play a voicing a few more times on the piano to be sure he understood it . Then taking it home to practice . Dedication beyond the norm . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **34;7561;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;7597;TOOLONG &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **33;7633;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;7668;TOOLONG &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **34;7704;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;7740;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; Juris continued to gig despite his cancer , and he performed a few days before Christmas at Zinc Bar in New York with his trio . On Christmas Day , he posted a photo of himself with the gift that his wife , vocalist Kate Baker , gave to him &amp;mdash ; a beautiful guitar , of course &amp;mdash ; and wrote : &amp;rdquo ; I have been blessed by overwhelming support by you as I continue to fight the evil giant . He ain&amp;rsquo ; t gon na get me ! &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Two recordings that Juris recently made have yet to be released , said publicist Lydia Liebman , who is Dave Liebman&amp;rsquo ; s daughter . They will be issued in 2020 , she said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Rest in peace , Vic Juris. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " **25;7776;TOOLONG " **32;7803;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; Peter HumTue , 31 Dec 2019 23:38:22 **45;7837;TOOLONG ... Dave Meder &amp;ndash ; Passage ( Outside In Music ) &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " **25;8130;TOOLONG " **32;8157;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;nbsp ; &lt;/p&gt; Peter HumTue , 24 Dec 2019 19:44:42 **45;8191;TOOLONG ... long list of 2019 's best Canadian jazz albums , plus even more honourable mentionshttp : **32;8238;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;8272;TOOLONG ... title= " Quinsin Nachoff " alt= " Quinsin Nachoff " data-portal-copyright= " Bo Huang " **28;8308;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1529212 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; List-reading fans of Canadian jazz , your patience has been rewarded . Feast your eyes and ears on the lists below , which take into account releases by Canadians living in Canada and elsewhere . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Best Canadian jazz of 2019&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; 1 . Quinsin Nachoff &amp;ndash ; Path of Totality ( Whirlwind Recordings ) &lt;br&gt; 2 . The North &amp;ndash ; Plays the Music of Kenny Wheeler ( AMM ) &lt;br&gt; 3. &amp;nbsp ; Anna Webber &amp;ndash ; Clockwise ( Pi Recordings ) &lt;br&gt; 4 . Kris Davis &amp;ndash ; Diatom Ribbons ( Pyroclastic Records ) &lt;br&gt; 5 . Pat LaBarbera / Kirk MacDonald Quintet &amp;ndash ; Trane of Thought , Live at the Rex ( Cellar Live ) &lt;br&gt; 6 . Mike Allen Trio &amp;ndash ; Just Like Magic ( Cellar Live ) &lt;br&gt; 7 . Fraser Hollins &amp;ndash ; Treehouse ( self-released ) &lt;br&gt; 8 . Florian Hoefner Trio &amp;ndash ; First Spring ( Alma Records ) &lt;br&gt; 9 . Steve Haines &amp;ndash ; and the Third Floor Orchestra ( Justin Time ) &lt;br&gt; 10 . Joel Miller &amp;ndash ; Unstoppable ( Multiple Chord Music ) &lt;br&gt; 11 . Four Visions Saxophone Quartet ( Sunnyside Records ) &lt;br&gt; &lt;span style= " font-family : -apple-system , BlinkMacSystemFont , ' Segoe UI ' , Roboto , Oxygen-Sans , Ubuntu , Cantarell , ' Helvetica Neue ' , sans-serif ; " &gt;12 . Seamus Blake -- Guardians of the Heart Machine ( Whirlwind Recordings ) &lt;br&gt; 13 . John Stetch &amp; Vulneraville -- Black Sea Suite ( self-released ) &lt;br&gt; 14 . Louis-Vincent Hamel -- Self-Inquiry ( self-released ) &lt;br&gt;&lt;/span&gt; &lt;span style= " font-family : -apple-system , BlinkMacSystemFont , ' Segoe UI ' , Roboto , Oxygen-Sans , Ubuntu , Cantarell , ' Helvetica Neue ' , sans-serif ; " &gt;15 . PJ Perry -- This Quiet Room ( Cellar Live ) &lt;br&gt; 16 . Yannick Rieu -- MachiNations ( Productions Yari ) &lt;br&gt;&lt;/span&gt; &lt;span style= " font-family : -apple-system , BlinkMacSystemFont , ' Segoe UI ' , Roboto , Oxygen-Sans , Ubuntu , Cantarell , ' Helvetica Neue ' , sans-serif ; " &gt;17 . Mike Murley -- Taking Flight ( Cornerstone ) &lt;br&gt; 18 . Don Thompson and John Abercrombie -- Yesterdays ( Cornerstone ) &lt;br&gt; 19 . Ted Quinlan -- Absolutely Dreaming ( self-released ) &lt;br&gt; 20 . Karin Plato -- This Could Be The One ( self-released ) &lt;br&gt;&lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; ( An even longer list of honourable mentions appears below after my descriptions and videos. ) &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; This year&amp;rsquo ; s crop of Canadian jazz seemed to me to be better and more bountiful , and I don&amp;rsquo ; t think that&amp;rsquo ; s simply because I resolved to take a deeper dive . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Canadian expats living in the U.S. did some of their most ambitious , bracing work this year . That goes not only for saxophonist and bracing composer Quinsin Nachoff , whose mind-expanding double album tops my list , thanks to its broad range of stimulations and the intense and creative contributions of saxophonist David Binney and Nachoff himself , keyboard wizard Matt Mitchell and drummers Kenny Wollesen and Nate Wood . Also thrumming with rigorous , innovative sounds from expanded ensembles were the highly stimulating albums by reeds player Anna Webber and pianist Kris Davis . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **33;8338;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;8373;TOOLONG &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **34;8409;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;8445;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; Moving in another direction , North Carolina bassist and educator Steve Haines , an Ottawa native , released a beautiful orchestral album . Haines&amp;rsquo ; project featured the impeccable vocalist Becca Stevens singing a program that leaned heavily into Canadiana ( think Gordon Lightfoot , Kim Mitchell , Leonard Cohen ) , her voice wafting over lush accompaniment and offset by soloists Chad Eby and Joey Calderazzo. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **33;8481;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;8516;TOOLONG &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **34;8552;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;8588;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; Montreal saxophonist Joel Miller went out on a limb with his own orchestral project that held multiple genres in its embrace&amp;hellip ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **33;8624;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;8659;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; &amp;hellip ; while he played a starring role on the album of his friend , Montreal bassist Fraser Hollins , whose quartet was completed by no less than pianist Jon Cowherd and drummer Brian Blade . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 100% " height= " 120 " style= " position : relative ; display : block ; width : 100% ; height : 120px ; " src= " **40;8695;TOOLONG ... allowtransparency= " true " frameborder= " 0 " &gt;&lt;/iframe&gt; &lt;p&gt; Perhaps I&amp;rsquo ; m betraying a bias here , but tenor saxophonists took the lion&amp;rsquo ; s share of honours in my list , from Toronto&amp;rsquo ; s Mike Murley , definitively interpreting Kenny Wheeler&amp;rsquo ; s music in the multi-national band The North , which also features David Braid&amp;rsquo ; s incisive piano playing , to Vancouver&amp;rsquo ; s Mike Allen , who paid a visit to New York and made a casual and effortlessly swinging masterpiece with bassist Peter Washington and drummer Lewis Nash . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 480 " src= " https : **34;8737;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;8773;TOOLONG &lt;iframe width= " 100% " height= " 120 " style= " position : relative ; display : block ; width : 100% ; height : 120px ; " src= " **40;8809;TOOLONG ... allowtransparency= " true " frameborder= " 0 " &gt;&lt;/iframe&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Toronto tenor tandem of Pat LaBarbera and Kirk MacDonald made clear why they&amp;rsquo ; re such musical treasures with a live album in which they roared through John Coltrane&amp;rsquo ; s music , from the conventionally bopping to the harmonically ornate to the momentously modal . Meanwhile , Montreal hornman Yannick Rieu&amp;rsquo ; s MachiNations was as eclectic and electric can be . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 100% " height= " 120 " style= " position : relative ; display : block ; width : 100% ; height : 120px ; " src= " **40;8851;TOOLONG ... allowtransparency= " true " frameborder= " 0 " &gt;&lt;/iframe&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **34;8893;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;8929;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; With a French rhythm section , Vancouver-raised saxophonist Seamus Blake presented more of his fine , ear-catching compositions , which can be overshadowed because he is such a ferocious and engrossing soloist . Blake&amp;rsquo ; s one-time roommate in New York , Vancouver-based pianist John Stetch , continued to plumb his own heady and captivating depths with his own new band . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **34;8965;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;9001;TOOLONG &lt;iframe width= " 100% " height= " 120 " style= " position : relative ; display : block ; width : 100% ; height : 120px ; " src= " **40;9037;TOOLONG ... allowtransparency= " true " frameborder= " 0 " &gt;&lt;/iframe&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 100% " height= " 120 " style= " position : relative ; display : block ; width : 100% ; height : 120px ; " src= " **40;9079;TOOLONG ... allowtransparency= " true " frameborder= " 0 " &gt;&lt;/iframe&gt; &lt;p&gt; For pure saxophony , no record could top the elaborate pleasures of the Four Visions Saxophone Quartet , a project masterminded by Montreal saxophonist Samuel Blais that enlisted the formidable Americans David Binney , Dave Liebman and Donny McCaslin. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 100% " height= " 120 " style= " position : relative ; display : block ; width : 100% ; height : 120px ; " src= " **40;9121;TOOLONG ... allowtransparency= " true " frameborder= " 0 " &gt;&lt;/iframe&gt; &lt;p&gt; More off-the-cuff were the nonetheless pristine duo albums from alto saxophonist veteran PJ Perry and pianist Bill Mays , and , from the vaults , an early 1990s gigging document from bassist Don Thompson and the late American guitar star John Abercrombie. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 100% " height= " 120 " style= " position : relative ; display : block ; width : 100% ; height : 120px ; " src= " **40;9163;TOOLONG ... allowtransparency= " true " frameborder= " 0 " &gt;&lt;/iframe&gt; &lt;p&gt; Saxophonist Murley also made the above list a second time for his chamber-y collaboration with stalwart collaborators Reg Schwager on guitar and bassist Steve Wallace , with pianist Renee Rosnes guesting and adding her special panache . It was also nice to hear Rosnes apply her talents to the album&amp;rsquo ; s two Kenny Wheeler tunes , as every Canadian jazz-person should . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Speaking of Canada&amp;rsquo ; s vital jazz pianists , the country can now claim German-born pianist Florian Hoefner , who resides in Newfoundland , as one of its own . Hoefner , who became a Canadian citizen , released a wonderful trio record this year with the Toronto rhythm team of bassist Andrew Downing and drummer Nick Fraser helping him to bring his music to life . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **34;9205;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;9241;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; Toronto guitarist Ted Quinlan marked his recent retirement from Humber College with a quartet album showing off his warm sound and sophisticated lyricism , bolstered by his long-time friends and elite musicians Brian Dickinson at the piano , bassist Kieran Overs and drummer Ted Warren . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **33;9277;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;9312;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; The young Montreal drummer Louis-Vincent Hamel released a dark-hued album that was the year&amp;rsquo ; s best debut by a Canadian jazz player , powered by performances by saxophonist Alex Francoeur , trumpeter Simon Millerd , pianist Rafael Zaldivar , bassist Mike De Masi and the drummer&amp;rsquo ; s father , guitarist Gabriel Hamel. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 100% " height= " 120 " style= " position : relative ; display : block ; width : 100% ; height : 120px ; " src= " **40;9348;TOOLONG ... allowtransparency= " true " frameborder= " 0 " &gt;&lt;/iframe&gt; &lt;p&gt; The best release from a Canadian jazz vocalist came from the other side of the country , in the form of Karin Plato&amp;rsquo ; s This Could Be The One , on which pianist Chris Gestrin and clarinetist James Danderfer distinguished themselves . &lt;/p&gt; ArtsFor her fifth album , jazz singer-songwriter Diane Nalini focuses on the futurehttp : **34;10417;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;10453;TOOLONG ... title= " Diane Nalini. " alt= " Diane Nalini. " data-portal-copyright= " Adrian Cho " **28;10489;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1525404 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Singer-songwriter Diane Nalini allowed a decade to pass between the release of her fourth and fifth albums &amp;mdash ; with good reason . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; First , there was the matter of her impressive career in the federal public service . In the last 10 years , Nalini , a Rhodes Scholar with a doctorate in applied nuclear physics , worked first on climate change policy at Environment Canada before moving most recently to Natural Resources Canada to turn her attention to nuclear energy policy . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It&amp;rsquo ; s not as if Nalini had just set music-making aside . She&amp;rsquo ; s been plenty active as the resident vocalist of the Ottawa Jazz Orchestra , and performed some of her songs in that setting . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Still , Nalini says : &amp;ldquo ; I don&amp;rsquo ; t think I expected to go so long between albums . I&amp;rsquo ; ve been doing a lot musically over the past few years , but never found the right time to make a new album until I finally took three months off to work on it full-time . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The result of Nalini&amp;rsquo ; s fall 2018 sabbatical is her album Future Perfect , a collection of 13 songs she and her band will launch Saturday during two concerts at the National Arts Centre&amp;rsquo ; s Fourth Stage . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; During the first month of her time away , Nalini took songs she had written in recent years and crafted arrangements for the band that would record them , paying particular attention to determining precise tempo markings for her material . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Especially for passages where the lyrics are dense , it can really kill a tune if you count it in too fast . It won&amp;rsquo ; t breathe right , &amp;rdquo ; Nalini says . &amp;ldquo ; Conversely , if you count a tune in too slowly , it can become a dirge . So I do that work ahead of time so that there won&amp;rsquo ; t be false starts in the studio because of a count-in that was too fast or slow . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A month into her hiatus , Nalini recorded in Montreal , where she grew up and lived before studying at Oxford University . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Joining her in the studio over three days were her long-time friends and collaborators Dany Roy on tenor saxophone , guitarist Mike Rud , bassist Alec Walkington and drummer Camil B&amp;eacute ; lisle . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; They made everything sound even better than it could in my head ! &amp;rdquo ; says Nalini. &amp;ldquo ; Every one of them is so talented and yet very humble and grounded . And so musical . I love the way they listen , and when we all listen to each other and have musical conversations with space to breathe , that&amp;rsquo ; s when the magic happens . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; All but Rud , who this year moved to B.C. , will be at the Fourth Stage . Ottawa guitarist Tim Bedner will step in. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; After the session , Nalini spent time reviewing rough mixes and choosing the best takes . Her mixing sessions were mostly finished last December . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Her new album is called Future Perfect , although there is no song on the recording with that title . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; As I put together the list of songs that I wanted to record , I realized that every one of them dealt with the future in some way , &amp;rdquo ; Nalini says . &amp;ldquo ; Either they explored my hopes and dreams for the future , or concerns about the future of the planet , or a sense of nostalgia for a love that might have been in an alternate future . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The new album reflects Nalini&amp;rsquo ; s growth as a vocalist and songwriter. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; As a vocalist , I&amp;rsquo ; ve become more disciplined about regular practice , including working more on technique and physical endurance . This is crucial when singing with a big band , &amp;rdquo ; she says . &amp;ldquo ; I&amp;rsquo ; ve also been doing more teaching , helping singers who want to develop good jazz phrasing and who want to start improvising . This definitely builds more awareness of one&amp;rsquo ; s own process for unpacking harmony . As a result , I&amp;rsquo ; m much more aware of my own instrument . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The album also includes Nalini&amp;rsquo ; s able scat-singing , which did not figure on her previous recordings . &amp;ldquo ; On my new album , I improvised on several tracks and I had lots of fun engaging in musical conversations with the musicians , throwing melodic themes back and forth at each other , &amp;rdquo ; she says . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; As a songwriter , Nalini recently found a new tool to help her &amp;mdash ; the ukulele. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I&amp;rsquo ; ve fallen in love with the tenor and baritone ukulele , &amp;rdquo ; she says . &amp;ldquo ; After some misadventures with acoustic guitar some years ago , I was left feeling it was just too big for my hands . I still used it for composing , but it never seemed to fit . Enter the ukulele . I can noodle on it while sitting on the sofa and it&amp;rsquo ; s small enough to travel with . I took it with me to an artist residency in Spain in 2017 to complete some of the songs for this album . It&amp;rsquo ; s a brilliant songwriting tool . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In true polymath fashion , Nalini writes and sings music in four languages &amp;mdash ; English , French , Spanish and Portuguese . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I love singing in different languages because they really bring out a completely different aspect of my personality , &amp;rdquo ; she says . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Each language has unique expressions that don&amp;rsquo ; t have their exact equivalent in the other . The metaphors and jokes are completely different . It&amp;rsquo ; s a different way of thinking . And therefore also a different way of singing . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; If I had to over-simplify , I&amp;rsquo ; d say Brazilian Portuguese has a soft , sensual and syncopated quality that is the perfect vehicle for bossa nova . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **34;10519;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;10555;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; Spanish is passionate and dramatic . French is romantic and sarcastic . English is the language of Shakespeare and of jazz and blues . So taken together , that&amp;rsquo ; s an emotional journey worth taking . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Nalini says that while Future Perfect is &amp;ldquo ; definitely a jazz album , &amp;rdquo ; it covers a lot of ground with the subjects of its songs . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; There are love songs , songs about wonder , about the joys of little things , songs about needing solace , facing an uncertain future but staying strong . Songs about the joys of getting away from it all by unplugging , and questions about where our obsession with technology will lead the planet , &amp;rdquo ; she says . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I wanted to explore many topics with this album . So I hope people will enjoy going on this journey with me. &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Diane Nalini album launch : Future Perfect&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;br&gt; &lt;strong&gt;When : &lt;/strong&gt; Saturday , Dec. 21 at 4:30 and 8:30 p.m. &lt;br&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Where : &lt;/strong&gt; NAC Fourth Stage &lt;br&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Tickets : &lt;/strong&gt; $35 for the matinee , $42 for the evening show , at the NAC box office or &lt;a href= " http : //nac-can.ca " &gt;nac-can.ca&lt;/a &; ... &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " **25;10591;TOOLONG " **32;10618;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;ALSO IN ARTS&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;10652;TOOLONG ... bets , Dec. 19 to 25 : Jann Arden teams up with the NAC Orchestra&lt;/a&gt; &lt;strong&gt; &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;10687;TOOLONG ... new live music hotspots on the Ottawa club scene&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;10722;TOOLONG ... Arts2020 TD Ottawa Winter Jazz Fest will bring 16 concerts to Arts Court , discuss merger of music and technologyhttp : **30;10804;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;10836;TOOLONG ... title= " Becca Stevens will play at the TD Winter Jazz Fest . " data-portal-copyright= " Matthew Murphy " **28;10872;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1509401 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; The 2020 TD Ottawa Winter Jazz Fest will present international stars including saxophonist Joel Frahm , pianists Dan Tepfer and Jean-Michel Pilc and vocalist Becca Stevens at its new location , Arts Court , when it takes place at the end of January . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The three-day event , which in recent years was staged at La Nouvelle Sc&amp;egrave ; ne Gilles Desjardins on King Edward Avenue , will also host panel discussions with musicians and music industry presenters concerning the impact of digital technology on jazz and creative music . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Each of the festival&amp;rsquo ; s three nights will be filled with a diverse and discriminating set of concerts . Frahm , a world-class mainstream saxophonist heard this summer in Ottawa with the quintet of pianist Brad Mehldau , opens the festival on Jan. 31 with a set of duets alongside his friend , Hamilton-based pianist Adrean Farrugia . Then , in another room at Arts Court , the Swedish saxophonist Mats Gustafsson , a juggernaut of free jazz , will play a solo set . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Two local groups also play that Thursday night . The quartet co-led by guitarist Garry Elliott and pianist Steve Boudreau will concentrate on originals documented on their 2018 album Opus 2 , while the ad hoc vocalist-revue project 33z , a crowd-pleaser at past Winter Jazz Fests , will cover Stevie Wonder&amp;rsquo ; s music . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **34;10902;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;10938;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; The night&amp;rsquo ; s last concert will feature the patient and mind-bending music of Clarice Jensen , an American cellist and the artistic director of ACME , the American Contemporary Music Ensemble , who processes her instrument with electronic effects . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Friday&amp;rsquo ; s program begins with the return of the Larnell Lewis Band . The fantastically funky Toronto-based drummer gave an uplifting late-night show this summer at the TD Ottawa Jazz Festival . Then , Gatineau experimentalists Fet.Nat , darlings of the Polaris Prize set , play . Wide-roaming and virtuosic pianist Pilc , a native of France who now teaches at McGill University , will reunite with Ottawa guitarist Roddy Ellias for a highly improvised set before Frahm will play Arts Court&amp;rsquo ; s main room with a trio . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **34;10974;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;11010;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; Then , Gustafsson returns to play duets with Canadian saxophone iconoclast Colin Stetson . The night&amp;rsquo ; s final concert , at 11 p.m. will be by the provocative and engaging Dutch trio Tin Men and the Telephone . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **34;11046;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;11082;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; The festival&amp;rsquo ; s last night , Saturday , Feb. 1 , kicks off with a solo set by Stetson followed by the Natural Machines project of pianist and polymath Tepfer , which explores the multi-layered intersection of improvisation and interactive computer programs Tepfer has created . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **34;11118;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;11154;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; Then , the Montreal-based group Quartetski , which takes an avant-garde jazz approach to classical material , will tackle music by John Cage . Concerts by Stevens , a potent artist who compellingly blends folk and jazz , and then Toronto saxophonist Allison Au , round out the night . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **34;11190;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;11226;TOOLONG &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **32;11262;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;11296;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; There will also be panel discussions , most likely on the Saturday afternoon , regarding music and technology . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Tickets for concerts ( $28 plus taxes and fees ) , day passes ( up to $75 plus taxes and fees ) and festival passes ( $150 plus taxes and fees ) are available at &lt;a href= " http : //ottawajazzfestival.com " &gt;ottaw ... . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " **25;11332;TOOLONG " **32;11359;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;hr&gt; &lt;p&gt; **33;11393;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;11428;TOOLONG ...
