Out : Fauna a contender for best restaurant dinner in Ottawa this year&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; Peter HumThu , 21 Nov 2019 15:31:49 **45;11533;TOOLONG ... ArtsMeet Peter Woods , the Ottawa minister who marries jazz and spiritualityhttp : **28;11580;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;11610;TOOLONG ... title= " Minister Peter Woods , who is also a saxophonist , and is combining music , spirituality and community building at several events next weekend . " alt= " Minister Peter Woods , who is also a saxophonist , and is combining music , spirituality and community building at several events next weekend . " data-portal-copyright= " Jean Levac " **28;11646;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1504219 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; If you ask Peter Woods to name some of his heroes , the minister of MacKay United Church in New Edinburgh offers an eclectic response . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; For example , Woods looks up to Pastor Miguel Tomas Castro Garcia , a Salvadoran activist and baptist preacher who mentored him in the 1980s when Woods was living in Central America and doing human rights work . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Woods , who is 56 and was ordained almost three decades ago , also counts as a major influence the Canadian poet and philosopher Jan Zwicky , whose work helps him ponder the connection between faith and art . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; And because Woods is a busy saxophonist around Ottawa , he reveres some of jazz history&amp;rsquo ; s most swinging and big-toned horn players . He confesses to a particular obsession with the late , gruff-toned saxman Gene Ammons &amp;ldquo ; as a player and as a spiritual force . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Indeed , Woods says that for him , music and spirituality are deeply intertwined . Throughout his life as a minister , &amp;ldquo ; music has been the governing image of the divine &amp;hellip ; and gigging has truly been a sort of spiritual practice , &amp;rdquo ; Woods says . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Later this week , Woods will play a run of especially spiritual gigs involving some of his Ottawa-based collaborators plus a special guest from Toronto , Tom Reynolds , who is both a jazz pianist and a professor of theology at the University of Toronto&amp;rsquo ; s Emmanuel College . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Woods grew up in Ottawa and began his musical exploits as a trombonist at Broadview Public School and Nepean High School . He switched to saxophone after high school and took some private lessons . He also took degrees in literature and theology , eventually leading to a master of divinity degree . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Over the last decade and a half , Woods played extensively and recorded with the late Ottawa jazz pianist Brian Browne , enjoying &amp;ldquo ; a wonderful and ambitious gig-based educational odyssey , &amp;rdquo ; Woods says . He and Browne were involved in a duo project that Woods called Jazz in Sacred Spaces , which allowed them to play concerts from Alberta to Nova Scotia . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **33;11676;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;11711;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; Woods says theology and music have been involved in a &amp;ldquo ; beautifully endless conversation&amp;rdquo ; for centuries , from the songs of King David to medieval chants to Bach to gospel music to Duke Ellington . Ammons , the earthy , bluesy saxophonist , recorded an album called Preachin&amp;rsquo ; , which Woods says &amp;ldquo ; speaks straight to me for its frank simplicity as an inspired testimony . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Woods also finds a seam of contemplative spirituality in the much more European and meditative music exemplified by the ECM Records label catalogue . The minister and pianist Mitchell Wright , MacKay&amp;rsquo ; s musical director , will improvise music for meditation every Tuesday this month at 7 p.m. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The spiritual force of music &amp;ldquo ; has the potential to be evoked at almost any gig being played in Ottawa tonight , &amp;rdquo ; adds Woods . &amp;ldquo ; I think my appetite for life as a working musician is borne out of a curiosity to see whether that surprising and magical quality will emerge on the gig tonight . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; From Ottawa&amp;rsquo ; s Roddy Ellias to Tim Bedner and Miguel De Armas , there are so many great players at all levels of jazz who work a deeply creative vein of spirituality , &amp;rdquo ; Woods says . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In the summer of 2015 , Woods began at MacKay , where the leadership team , he says , &amp;ldquo ; encouraged a deep pursuit of artistic expression , theological reflection and community engagement . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The latest expression of Woods&amp;rsquo ; jazz-based work at MacKay is what he calls the Kindness of Jazz project . Woods says he&amp;rsquo ; s addressing such questions as &amp;ldquo ; How do faith communities engage in social and spiritual improvisation ? &amp;rdquo ; and &amp;ldquo ; How do I best integrate the depth of wisdom and reflection that jazz musicians bring to bear on these contemporary and ancient themes ? &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; It&amp;rsquo ; s partly about finding opportunities to improvise music in sacred and communal settings , and partly about how that improvisation reflects on communal values , &amp;rdquo ; Woods says . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; One of Woods&amp;rsquo ; gigs this weekend with jazz pianist Reynolds will be a quartet performance at the first Beechwood Night Market , which will be hosted by MacKay on Friday from 6 to 10 p.m . The group will play accompaniment for a silent movie , Woods says , while food and beverage vendors will have wares for sale . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; It&amp;rsquo ; s a chance to throw our doors open , host the neighbourhood , and share some music and fun together , &amp;rdquo ; Woods says of the night market , for which admission is free . The quartet with Woods and Reynolds will also play Saturday night at the Royal Oak on Beechwood Avenue . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; MacKay marks its 144th anniversary on Sunday . That morning , at 10:30 , Reynolds will be speaking and performing with a full quintet and choir . A smaller ensemble will hit the road to play a Sunday afternoon concert at Holy Trinity Anglican Church in North &lt;span class= " marky9d3zu0ws " &gt;Gower. &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; A church that plays jazz together &amp;mdash ; both in musical terms , and also metaphorically &amp;mdash ; is a community in which diversity and paradoxes can co-exist and thrive , &amp;rdquo ; Woods says . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Kindness of Jazz at MacKay United Church&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;br&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Where : &lt;/strong&gt; 39 Dufferin Rd. , New Edinburgh &lt;br&gt; &lt;strong&gt;More info : &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;a href= " http : //mackayunitedchurch.org " &gt;macka ... &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " **25;11747;TOOLONG " **32;11774;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; Peter HumWed , 13 Nov 2019 19:36:12 **45;11808;TOOLONG ... ArtsGentiane M-G Trio album reviewedhttp : **32;11855;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;11889;TOOLONG ... title= " Montreal jazz pianist Gentiane MG , who plays the NAC Fourth Stage on March 28 , 2019. " alt= " Montreal jazz pianist Gentiane MG , who plays the NAC Fourth Stage on March 28 , 2019. " data-portal-copyright= " " **28;11925;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1369967 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Wonderland ( Effendi Records ) &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;br&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Gentiane MG Trio&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Pianist Gentiane Michaud-Gagnon ( or Gentiane M-G , as she more succinctly calls herself ) is currently more than 4,500 kilometres removed from her home base in Montreal , where is one of the city&amp;rsquo ; s brightest jazz up-and-comers. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The award-winning pianist , who is not yet 30 , is on the road with her trio , which also includes bassist Levi Dover and drummer Louis-Vincent Hamel . They all graduated from McGill University&amp;rsquo ; s jazz program not that long ago , but under Michaud-Gagnon&amp;rsquo ; s leadership , they make up a formidably promising trio that is developing its own language . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Tonight , Nov. 6 , the trio plays Tyrant Studios in Vancouver , before hitting the Yardbird Suite in Edmonton on Thursday and then the JazzYYC Canadian Festival in Calgary on Saturday . Next week , stops closer to the trio&amp;rsquo ; s home beckon , including gigs at Ottawa&amp;rsquo ; s Brookstreet Hotel Nov. 13 , the Jazz Bistro in Toronto Nov. 14 and 15 , and the Jazz Room in Kitchener Nov. 16. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; This review , then , is a crash course is the trio&amp;rsquo ; s music , for not only Western Canadian jazz fans but also for anyone else not yet familiar with its rarefied stimulations. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Expect Michaud-Gagnon to draw from her sophomore album , Wonderland , which was released in the spring . It consists entirely of the pianist&amp;rsquo ; s originals , which are complex musically and emotionally but always rendered with great lucidity. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Tune titles such as Forgiveness , Repentance , Erased Self , Empty Canvas and Sanctuary hint at Michaud-Gagnon&amp;rsquo ; s thoughtful orientation and desire to expose an inner life through music . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In more concrete terms , tunes frequently raise the bar rhythmically . After the album&amp;rsquo ; s short , serious , through-composed Opening , Erased Self is a knotty tune that seeks liberation against the background of its 5/4 ostinato . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Meanwhile , Road to Nowhere is a moody bossa nova that sways in 7/4 : &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **34;11955;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;11991;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; The group fills Empty Canvas with jauntily swinging , freebopping content : &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **34;12027;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;12063;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; And Blue &amp;ndash ; Noir &amp;mdash ; Sol grows more open after its rugged but precise theme , and features one of Hamel&amp;rsquo ; s typically enthralling drum solos . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; There&amp;rsquo ; s much gravitas to Michaud-Gagnon&amp;rsquo ; s music , perhaps most of all on tracks such as Heavy Weight , the titular suite , and the slow , dour album-closer Repentance , which brings down the curtain on the album with a chord filled with implications . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Wonderland offers a generous serving of heady music from a blossoming artist with wonders to share . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Gentiane M-G Trio plays : &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Nov. 6 at Tyrant Studios in Vancouver &lt;br&gt; Nov. 16 at the Jazz Room in Kitchener &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " **25;12099;TOOLONG " **32;12126;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; Peter HumWed , 06 Nov 2019 19:15:49 **45;12160;TOOLONG ... fascination with drums led Mareike Wiening to become a transcontinental jazz playerhttp : **34;12207;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;12243;TOOLONG ... title= " German ( NY-based ) jazz drummer Mareike Wiening. " alt= " German ( NY-based ) jazz drummer Mareike Wiening. " data-portal-copyright= " Lukas Diller " **28;12279;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1495578 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; As a child , Mareike Wiening quickly took to several instruments and music in general . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; She began piano studies at the age of five , and flute lessons five years later . She sang in a choir for 10 years . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But it was only at 15 that she came to the instrument at the centre of the music she makes today &amp;mdash ; the drums . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I taught myself when I was 15 and started to take lessons when I was 16 , &amp;rdquo ; recalls the German musician who is in her early 30s. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I was fascinated by the independence of a drummer and by the power of the instrument , &amp;rdquo ; she continues . &amp;ldquo ; Especially in jazz music , I always loved the freedom , and that one song can sound completely different each time you play it . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; These days , Wiening pursues jazz on two continents , splitting her time between New York City and Cologne . She and her New York-based quintet play GigSpace in Ottawa on Sunday night as part of a short Ontario tour . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The concert in the cosy 46-seat space on Gladstone Avenue will consist of Wiening&amp;rsquo ; s elaborate but lyrical compositions , which are documented on her just-released album Metropolis Paradise on the esteemed Greenleaf Music label . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **34;12309;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;12345;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; Wiening is steeped in musical education . Her first degree is jointly from the Mannheim University&amp;nbsp ; of Music and&amp;nbsp ; Performing Arts and the Rhythmic Music Conservatory in Copenhagen . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Copenhagen was great , because they emphasize on finding your own artistic voice , &amp;rdquo ; Wiening says . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; About six years ago , she moved to New York to attend New York University on a full scholarship and got her master&amp;rsquo ; s degree . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In New York , she also formed her band and began composing . &amp;ldquo ; I got to know myself as a drummer , composer and as a person . I feel like I grew artistically and that would not have been possible in Germany , &amp;rdquo ; she says . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; One of her most influential teachers was vibraphonist Stefon Harris , with whom she studied composition and harmony . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; His approach to improvising , composing , harmony , ear training and communicating within the band had a huge impact on my playing and my relationship to music , &amp;rdquo ; Wiening says . &amp;ldquo ; It opened up a whole other world to me. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; With the drums , I was always missing the melodic part that I&amp;rsquo ; m used to from the piano . With Stefon&amp;rsquo ; s composing technique , I was really able to express my feelings into harmony . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Wiening continues to study with composer and bandleader Guillermo Klein. &amp;ldquo ; He had a huge impact , for instance , on the title track to Metropolis Paradise , &amp;rdquo ; she says . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **34;12381;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;12417;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I admire Guillermo&amp;rsquo ; s music . It&amp;rsquo ; s so pure , honest and emotional . That&amp;rsquo ; s what I&amp;rsquo ; m looking for in my music as well . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Wiening&amp;rsquo ; s potent band includes bassist Johannes Felscher , a fellow German , the accomplished young pianist Glenn Zaleski , Canadian ex-pat guitarist Alex Goodman and veteran tenor saxophonist Rich Perry . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; While she lauds the specific talents of her bandmates , she also says that if Goodman and Perry happened to play different instruments , she would still have them in her band . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; It&amp;rsquo ; s really about the musicians themselves . If Rich and Alex would play a different instrument , my band would have a different instrumentation . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **33;12453;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;12488;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; After her Ontario concerts and one show in New York , Wiening will head to Germany for the rest of November for a batch of concerts with German musicians . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Although I love New York City , I&amp;rsquo ; m still German and I love Germany and miss my family , &amp;rdquo ; she says . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Also there&amp;rsquo ; s more funding for arts and music in Europe and the quality of life is much better , in my opinion . I don&amp;rsquo ; t want to give that up . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Of course , the music scene and the artistic value of New York is not comparable to any other place in the world . I&amp;rsquo ; d like to have both , if possible . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; When asked if it&amp;rsquo ; s hard to make a living as a jazz musician in an age of streaming and declining album sales , Wiening responds that the jazz life has always been challenging . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Jazz musicians don&amp;rsquo ; t do it for money , &amp;rdquo ; she adds . &amp;ldquo ; If I&amp;rsquo ; d like to be famous and rich , I can go ahead and play pop music . But I don&amp;rsquo ; t want to do that . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Jazz is a decision , and if you really work hard , it&amp;rsquo ; s possible to make a living , &amp;rdquo ; she says . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Mareike Wiening Quintet&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;br&gt; &lt;strong&gt;When : &lt;/strong&gt; Sunday , Nov. 3 at 7:30 p.m. &lt;br&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Where : &lt;/strong&gt; GigSpace ( 953 Gladstone Ave. ) &lt;br&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Tickets : &lt;/strong&gt; $25 at &lt;a href= " http : //gigspace.ca " &gt;gigspace.ca&lt;/ ... , or by calling 613-729-0693 &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;The Mareike Wiening Quintet also plays : &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;br&gt; Thursday , Oct. 31 at the Rex Jazz and Blues Bar in Toronto &lt;br&gt; Friday , Nov. 1 at PUASA art house in Buffalo , NY &lt;br&gt; Saturday , Nov. 2 at the Jazz Room in Kitchener &lt;br&gt; Tuesday , Nov. 5 at the Jazz Gallery in New York &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " **25;12524;TOOLONG " **32;12551;TOOLONG &lt;br&gt; &lt;a href= " http : //twitter.com/peterhum " &gt;twitter ... &lt;br&gt; &lt;a href= " http : **28;12585;TOOLONG " &gt;o ... &lt;/p&gt; Peter HumWed , 30 Oct 2019 19:18:22 **45;12615;TOOLONG ... ArtsFlorian Hoefner Trio CD reviewedhttp : **32;12662;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;12696;TOOLONG ... title= " Florian Hoefner Trio , with drummer Nick Fraser and bassist Andrew Downing . " alt= " Florian Hoefner Trio , with drummer Nick Fraser and bassist Andrew Downing . " data-portal-copyright= " BO HUANG " **28;12732;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1473003 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;First Spring ( Alma Records ) &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;br&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Florian Hoefner Trio&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; If I recall correctly , Florian Hoefner , the exceptional German-born jazz pianist now based in St. John&amp;rsquo ; s , Nfld. , became a Canadian citizen this year . Given that his new album , First Spring , includes an original track entitled Winter in June , I&amp;rsquo ; d say Hoefner gets Canada as well as anyone born here . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; You might even call his new album , First Spring , his most Canadian album yet . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Yes , the influence of living on the Rock for the last few years &amp;mdash ; Hoefner teaches at Memorial University , as does his Canadian-born wife , clarinetist Christine Carter &amp;mdash ; can clearly be discerned on selected tracks on his most recent albums , the solo album Coldwater Stories and the quartet album Luminosity . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But First Spring , which was released in late September and which is being feted during the next few nights at trio gigs in Toronto , Hamilton and Montreal , marks Hoefner&amp;rsquo ; s first work with an all-Canadian group . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; On his first trio album , he&amp;rsquo ; s joined by the wonderfully in-sync bass-and-drums pair from Toronto , bassist Andrew Downing and drummer Nick Fraser . The trio breathes life beautifully into nine tracks that in the main brings Hoefner&amp;rsquo ; s jazz-based musical sensibility to bear on a diverse collection of folk-music material . This strategy strikes me as a bit of a Canadian jazz predilection , if not exclusively so . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Opening the album is Hound&amp;rsquo ; s Tune . With it , Hoefner puts his trio&amp;rsquo ; s bold stamp on a piece by the late Newfoundland fiddle master Rufus Guinchard . A crucial component on this effervescent opener is Downing&amp;rsquo ; s dazzling arco playing , which reappears elsewhere on First Spring with the impact of a separate instrument . Here&amp;rsquo ; s exactly how that tune went down : &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **34;12762;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;12798;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; The album takes a sombre , bluesy turn with the trio&amp;rsquo ; s run through Calvary , Levon Helm&amp;rsquo ; s rumbling , twanging epic . This patient track transforms Helm&amp;rsquo ; s gravelly version without losing its essence , and is spacious but also rich with the kinds of arranged intricacies that grace Hoefner&amp;rsquo ; s work with the German co-op quintet Subtone. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Hoefner returns to the Americana well for a rendition of Sam Amidon&amp;rsquo ; s simple lament Short Life . On this track , Downing offers another soulful star turn with his bowing and Hoefner&amp;rsquo ; s playing is at its most bell-like before he ratchets up the tension during his improvisation , well shadowed by Fraser . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-family : -apple-system , BlinkMacSystemFont , ' Segoe UI ' , Roboto , Oxygen-Sans , Ubuntu , Cantarell , ' Helvetica Neue ' , sans-serif ; " &gt;Hoefner roams further afield when the trio addresses the sad strains of the Armenian folk song Loosin Yelav . With its glowing expressiveness and mature way , the trio brings to mind a Keith Jarrett Trio performance . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Then , the trio launches into its take on the waltzing Scottish folk song The Maid on the Shore , a minor-key romp that eventually charges ahead with an unabashedly swinging vibe. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Three Hoefner originals are woven into the flow of the album , and they mesh well with the other material and broaden the thrust of the disc . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The captivating title track , written for Hoefner&amp;rsquo ; s young son Max , and Hoefner&amp;rsquo ; s piece Solstice are lighter and more buoyant than the album&amp;rsquo ; s more plaintive tracks . Solstice in particular is a surging , charging tune . Both tracks benefit from Fraser&amp;rsquo ; s impeccable musicality , which allows him to propel music and make it vividly colourful at the same time . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Hoefner&amp;rsquo ; s piece Winter in June is slower and more meditative before it thaws out and becomes more spirited . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **34;12834;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;12870;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; Closing the album is an austere rendition of the traditional piece Rain and Snow &amp;mdash ; a companion piece to Winter in June ? &amp;mdash ; that brings an exquisite and beautifully recorded album to a fine point of rest . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Having heard Hoefner now on record and in person performing solo and with a quartet , a quintet and now a trio , I&amp;rsquo ; m stumped as to which context I prefer him in . In every situation , the music is so substantial and at times even breath-taking . I do know that I&amp;rsquo ; ll be coming back to First Spring for more enjoyable listens , to appreciate its forthright beauty and this new expansiveness of Hoefner&amp;rsquo ; s musical world . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt;The Florian Hoefner Trio plays : &lt;/em&gt; &lt;br&gt; &lt;em&gt;Thursday , Oct. 24 at 9:30 p.m. at the Rex Jazz and Blues Bar in Toronto ( with Andrew Downing and Jim Doxas ) &lt;/em&gt; &lt;br&gt; &lt;em&gt;Friday , Oct. 25 at 7 p.m. at the Conservatory in Hamilton ( with Andrew Downing and Nick Fraser ) &lt;/em&gt; &lt;br&gt; &lt;em&gt;Saturday , Oct. 26 at 6:30 p.m. at Diese Onze in Montreal ( with Adrian Vedady and Jim Doxas ) &lt;/em&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " **25;12906;TOOLONG " **32;12933;TOOLONG &lt;br&gt; &lt;a href= " http : //twitter.com/peterhum " &gt;twitter ... &lt;br&gt; &lt;a href= " http : **28;12967;TOOLONG " &gt;o ... &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;nbsp ; &lt;/p&gt; Peter HumThu , 24 Oct 2019 19:32:52 **45;12997;TOOLONG ... think of J.J. Johnson every day , " says Montreal saxophonist Dan Faulkhttp : **35;13044;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;13081;TOOLONG ... title= " " alt= " " data-portal-copyright= " " **28;13117;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1490467 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Before saxophonist Dan Faulk moved to Montreal a few years ago , he spent more than two decades in the fertile artistic soil of New York&amp;rsquo ; s jazz scene . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; On his r&amp;eacute ; sum&amp;eacute ; is a stint in the 1990s with the band of the late trombone master J.J. Johnson , and Faulk appears on three of Johnson&amp;rsquo ; s albums , including the Grammy-winning 1999 album Heroes . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Faulk will be making his Ottawa debut on Saturday night when his quartet plays GigSpace , the intimate 46-seat listening room on Gladstone Avenue . Below , Faulk discusses his musical and geographic travels . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Q : When and why did you move to Montreal ? &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A : I moved to Montreal in early 2017 . The end of December 2019 will mark my third year living in Canada . I moved here to join the music scene and fulfill immigration efforts I began a while ago . I hope to make Canada my long-term primary residence . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Q : You were part of a high-powered peer group in New York . Do you miss the scene there ? &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A : I think it would be hard for anyone to not miss the New York jazz scene ! I&amp;rsquo ; m looking forward to increasing activities in New York and the States again soon . I would like to increase traveling abroad , with Canada as my base . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **34;13147;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;13183;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Q : Tell me about your early attraction to jazz and the saxophone . &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A : At a very early age , the sound of the saxophone stood out to me on recordings from all music genres . I heard Sonny Rollins&amp;rsquo ; s original St. Thomas recording when I was about 14 years old . I turned to my younger brother as it finished and said , &amp;ldquo ; I&amp;rsquo ; m going to be a professional saxophonist ! &amp;rdquo ; He looked at me like I had lost my mind . But I was fully hooked and set from that point . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; I was fortunate to have a very large collection of classic jazz recordings from a young age , by way of my sax instructor . And early on , I also liked a lot of fusion players , especially Wilton Felder and Cannonball Adderley&amp;rsquo ; s crossover material . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; As I progressed as a player and musician , I delved deeply into bebop , especially while studying and becoming good friends with pianist Barry Harris . I also became more open to and interested in free jazz . Now I listen to all styles of music , and still have a deep love for straight-ahead jazz . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Q : What was it like working with J.J. Johnson ? &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A : I have only positive memories of my time with J.J.&amp;rsquo ; s band . I learned so many quality lifetime lessons , musically and otherwise , working for him . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; I was in my early 20s and he was maybe 50 years older than me and playing at top form every night ! It was just us in the front line of his quintet . He gave me so much opportunity to grow . I think of him every day . I hope to add in some way to his legacy of bringing art of the highest integrity that moves and inspires people . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Q : You 're a prolific composer . What drives you to compose ? What are you striving for as a composer ? &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A : I love writing as much as playing the horn . Sometimes playing and writing are competing for my creative time and energies . For me , a composition can be a snapshot of a moment in time or a vehicle to work out something on my horn or for specific musicians I&amp;rsquo ; m playing with . Other times , writing is something that just comes to me &amp;mdash ; that , as I&amp;rsquo ; ve heard many other writers say , is written through me. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Q : What musical ambitions do you have ? &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A : I would like to spend some time writing larger suites for my small groups and writing more music for my new Electric band . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Q : Tell me a bit about the musicians coming with you to Ottawa , and the material you 'll play . &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A : I like playing with musicians from diverse backgrounds , experiences , and age groups . It brings a great energy to the bandstand , I feel . I&amp;rsquo ; d like to develop this further , and make more music with Montreal musicians playing styles outside of jazz . Montreal is very rich in artistic and cultural diversity , and I feel I&amp;rsquo ; m still scratching the surface . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **33;13219;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;13254;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; Our pianist is Zach Frampton . Martin Heslop is an in-demand Montreal bassist who also co-owns the R&amp;eacute ; sonance Cafe , and Wali Muhammad is a leading Montreal drummer . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The music will be a collection of standards and my originals , a combination of modern straight-ahead and Latin tunes , with some odd-meter tunes and some ballads . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Dan Faulk Quartet&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;br&gt; &lt;strong&gt;When : &lt;/strong&gt; Saturday , Oct. 26 , 7:30 p.m. &lt;br&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Where : &lt;/strong&gt; Gigspace ( 953 Gladstone Ave. ) &lt;br&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Tickets : &lt;/strong&gt; $25 at gigspace.ca or at the door &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " **25;13290;TOOLONG " **32;13317;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; Peter HumWed , 23 Oct 2019 13:52:26 **45;13351;TOOLONG ... ArtsConcert review : Ogresse , C ? cile McLorin Salvant 's collaboration with Darcy James Argue , was flawlesshttp : **32;13398;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;13432;TOOLONG ... title= " C ? cile McLorin Salvant " alt= " C ? cile McLorin Salvant " data-portal-copyright= " John Abbott " **28;13468;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1481435 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Vancouver-based jazz broadcaster Nou Dadoun was in New York late last month for the most recent performance of Ogresse , vocalist C&amp;eacute ; cile McLorin Salvant&amp;rsquo ; s ambitious and spellbinding song cycle . He passes on this detailed review : &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; **36;13498;TOOLONG &lt;br&gt; &lt;strong&gt;The Rose Hall , Jazz At Lincoln Center&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;br&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Reviewed Friday , Sept. 28&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt;There 's a woman&lt;/em&gt; &lt;br&gt; &lt;em&gt;Lived in the woods&lt;/em&gt; &lt;br&gt; &lt;em&gt;On the outskirts of town&lt;/em&gt; &lt;br&gt; &lt;em&gt;Her skin was chocolate brown&lt;/em&gt; &lt;br&gt; &lt;em&gt;Upon her head she wore a crown&lt;/em&gt; &lt;br&gt; &lt;em&gt;of bones , human bones&lt;/em&gt; &lt;br&gt; &lt;em&gt;She lived alone&lt;/em&gt; &lt;br&gt; &lt;em&gt;With the birds and the trees&lt;/em&gt; &lt;br&gt; &lt;em&gt;With her memories&lt;/em&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Thus begins Ogresse , a fairy tale worthy of the Brothers Grimm that tells the story of a creature whose loneliness makes her ravenous . Her story is a song cycle filled with longing , love , betrayal , and drama . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Ogresse is a unique collaboration between its composer and vocalist C&amp;eacute ; cile McLorin Salvant and bandleader/composer Darcy James Argue , who arranged it . In performance , Argue conducted the nine-piece L&amp;rsquo ; Orchestre Ogresse in combination with the Mivos String Quartet , an ensemble that has built its own reputation for collaborations with creative artists like experimental vocalist Jen Shyu. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; McLorin Salvant and Argue share a stylistic flair that leans toward large statements in fashion , art , music and storytelling and Ogresse is operatic in scope . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; McLorin Salvant has a voice that comes along only once or twice in a generation . It has passed through training in classical and formal technique but also takes on a remarkable expressiveness . A deserved darling of dozens of critics&amp;rsquo ; and popular polls , she won the Thelonious Monk International Jazz Vocals Competition in 2010 , when she was just 21. followed by two vocal jazz Grammy Awards before she turned 30. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Argue&amp;rsquo ; s compositions are expansive and most often realized through his own large ensemble the Secret Society . They are complex but with strong melodic lines that coalesce around the steampunk imagery of Infernal Machines , the overblown attempt to build the world&amp;rsquo ; s largest carousel in Brooklyn Babylon ( a multi-media production in collaboration with visual artist Danijel Zezelj ) or the modern political paranoia of Real Enemies . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; There&amp;rsquo ; s a direct line from Gil Evans through Bob Brookmeyer and Maria Schneider in Argue&amp;rsquo ; s arrangements . The quality of his work has also been recognized in a series of Grammy and Juno Award nominations , in appearances of the Secret Society at major festivals like the Newport Jazz Festival and commissions from other adventurous ensembles like the Hard Rubber Orchestra . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; We attended the Friday performance , the first of two on the last weekend in September at Rose Theater at Jazz at Lincoln Center In New York . The Theater is a beautiful wood-paneled space in an oval shape with about 1,200 seats that fan away from the stage in semi-circles and surround it with balconies . The wood and closeness of the performers enhanced the intimacy and closeness of the story &amp;mdash ; like watching a fairy tale play out in a field under the stars . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; L&amp;rsquo ; Orchestre Ogresse was assembled on stage when we entered . McLorin Salvant arrived in a luminous gown and commanded the stage with the ensemble in a semi-circle behind her and the story began . The song cycle was episodic in form and many of the episodes began with a country-twanged claw-hammer banjo playing a down-home introduction . Several of the later episodes had that same theme picked up by different parts of the Orchestra , here the string quartet and there the marimba. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The fairy tale was told by McLorin Salvant in alternating voices &amp;mdash ; an omniscient narrator , the Ogresse herself , her deceptive suitor and the birds who warned all of betrayal and disaster . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Ogresse lives in the woods outside a town of wary and suspicious folk who can not believe how vast she is &amp;mdash ; her mouth is the size of a planet . And when the townspeople become too curious or try to catch her , she simply pops them inside that planet-sized mouth . She revels in her enormity but still she is lonely with only the trees and birds to keep her company . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; One of the townsmen conspires against her and tries to subdue her with sweet words and love . She is resistant &amp;mdash ; &amp;ldquo ; I do not believe you , why should I believe you ? &amp;rdquo ; &amp;mdash ; but he persists and eventually wears her down through her longing and loneliness . She dreams of dancing with him , hand in hand under clouds of violet , in a graveyard with bones underfoot . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In a heart-stopping moment , the birds reveal his treachery with a damning accusation : &amp;ldquo ; The Man is Lying ! &amp;rdquo ; In her despair and betrayal , the Ogresse poisons and consumes him &amp;ndash ; poisoning herself in a rage that consumes them both in an ultimate conflagration. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; McLorin Salvant was the only vocalist but her power and expressiveness didn&amp;rsquo ; t not flag at all during the uninterrupted 90-minute performance . At the end , her hand holding the microphone gradually dropped to her side and her clear , un-amplified voice still filled the theatre completely . The perfect acoustics of Rose Theater supported the way in which she inhabited and acted out the different parts . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; As a bonus , the libretto was handed out at the end of the performance in a small hand-printed booklet that was illustrated with Salvant&amp;rsquo ; s evocative sketches , revealing levels of nuance and wordplay that were easy to miss in the drama of the performance . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The music drew on multiple influences &amp;mdash ; that recurring country-folk banjo theme , elements of Prokofiev and Mancini , and even a baroque interlude . The powerful dissonance in the scene of the final conflagration was riveting . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; There was lots of solo space for these outstanding players . Highlights were Kirk Knuffke&amp;rsquo ; s cornet , Warren Wolf&amp;rsquo ; s vibes and marimba and Aaron Diehl&amp;rsquo ; s piano , chamber organ and melodica . In addition to the recurring themes , Nate Radley took an amazing banjo solo which was reminiscent of a koto , adding to the kabuki sense of drama . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In these days where so much adventurous large ensemble music suffers from a lack of rehearsal or unachieved ambition , it was a real pleasure to hear this creative endeavour totally engaging on so many levels in a flawless performance . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The next opportunity to experience Ogresse is on Warren Wolf&amp;rsquo ; s home turf of SFJazz in San Francisco in March of 2020 . In the interim , Argue brings the Secret Society to New York City&amp;rsquo ; s Jazz Standard on Oct. 24. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; McLorin Salvant has a West Coast tour that brings her and the Aaron Diehl trio to North Vancouver&amp;rsquo ; s Blueshore Centre for the Performing Arts on Oct. 16 and 17 . Then she spends November in Europe before returning to New York for a recording session and performance at Carnegie Hall with the new collective Artemis . If either ( or both ) come anywhere near you , don&amp;rsquo ; t pass up the opportunity to see them . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Are you listening Mr. Pulitzer ? &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; C&amp;eacute ; cile McLorin Salvant , vocals , composer &lt;br&gt; with L&amp;rsquo ; Orchestre Ogresse &lt;br&gt; Darcy James Argue , bandleader , arranger , conductor &lt;br&gt; Alexa Tarantino , winds &lt;br&gt; Tom Christensen , winds &lt;br&gt; Kirk Knuffke , cornet &lt;br&gt; Josh Roseman , trombone , tuba &lt;br&gt; Nate Radley , guitar , banjo &lt;br&gt; Aaron Diehl , piano , melodica , organ &lt;br&gt; David Wong , bass &lt;br&gt; Samuel Torres , percussion &lt;br&gt; Warren Wolf , vibraphone , marimba &lt;br&gt; MIVOS String Quartet &lt;br&gt; Olivia De Prato , Maya Bennardo , violins &lt;br&gt; Victor Lowrie Tafoya , viola &lt;br&gt; Tyler J. Borden , cello &lt;/p&gt; Peter HumThu , 17 Oct 2019 19:36:38 **45;13536;TOOLONG ... University 's $200,000 Improv Workshop Project takes on life of its own , says Jean-Michel Pilchttp : **36;13583;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;13621;TOOLONG ... title= " Montreal-based pianist Jean-Michel Pilc " alt= " Montreal-based pianist Jean-Michel Pilc " data-portal-copyright= " Steve Sussman " **28;13657;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1284110 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span class= " doctext " &gt;When jazz pianist Jean-Michel Pilc joined &lt;/span&gt; McGill University&amp;rsquo ; s Schulich School of Music in 2015 , he found out that being a professor also meant applying for peer-reviewed grants . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I said , &amp;lsquo ; That&amp;rsquo ; s not exactly the thing that I know best how to do . But I&amp;rsquo ; ll try , ' &amp;rdquo ; recalls Pilc , 58 , who was born in France and then spent two decades playing and teaching in New York City before moving to Montreal . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Pilc formulated a proposal , enlisted the help of a professional grant writer , and applied to the Fonds de R&amp;eacute ; cherche du Qu&amp;eacute ; bec &amp;ndash ; Soci&amp;eacute ; t&amp;eacute ; et culture . The fund&amp;rsquo ; s assessors liked what they saw and awarded Pilc&amp;rsquo ; s project $200,000. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I was surprised as you are , &amp;rdquo ; Pilc says . &amp;ldquo ; I was elated and surprised and also a bit scared , because right now , I have to deliver the goods . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Outside of academia , Pilc is internationally renowned in jazz circles for his boldly unfettered and virtuosic music-making , usually in solo or trio contexts . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **34;13687;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;13723;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; However , the point of his research project &amp;mdash ; dubbed the Improv Workshop Project , or IWP &amp;mdash ; is to study how a larger ensemble of seven or eight people can &amp;ldquo ; find ways to make great music that&amp;rsquo ; s not planned in advance at all , or very little . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The IWP is entering the third of its three years and will perform in Montreal Thursday night at Le Minist&amp;egrave ; re ( 4521 Boul . Saint-Laurent ) as part of L&amp;rsquo ; Off Festival de Jazz de Montr&amp;eacute ; al . The project also frequently plays at Resonance Caf&amp;eacute ; on Tuesday nights , and its upcoming gigs there are on Oct. 29 and Nov. 12. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The project draws from a diverse group of about 50 musicians , Pilc says . Along with him , key players are fellow McGill jazz professors Kevin Dean , who usually plays trumpet in a hard-bop style , and alto saxophonist R&amp;eacute ; mi Bolduc , who is equally at home playing swinging jazz in the style of Oscar Peterson or intricately plotted , even mathematical music . McGill music students and recent alumni , naturally , are involved in the project en masse . Occasionally , special guests join in , such as New York vocalist Elisabeth Kontomanou , who will sing with the project Thursday . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In the past , saxophonist Sam Newsome and violinist Meg Okura , both U.S. musicians , have joined the IWP. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **34;13759;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;13795;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; One of the spurs for Pilc&amp;rsquo ; s project was his disinterest in jazz that strikes him as overly pre-conceived. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; My frustration when I listen to most of modern jazz is that things tend to be very controlled , &amp;rdquo ; he says . &amp;ldquo ; The compositions are very complex in general &amp;mdash ; lots of odd meters , exotic chord progressions and so forth &amp;mdash ; and people tend to write a lot before they even step foot on stage . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Even though the players might be fantastic and the music might be great , I always feels there is something that I personally am looking for in music and I don&amp;rsquo ; t find in that type of situation as a listener . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Pilc says he&amp;rsquo ; s far from the first person to plumb free music , made without written material to lean on . But he says that he wanted to avoid &amp;ldquo ; what I call free jazz , which means people make all kinds of noises and there is not much melody and rhythm or if there is , it can be a bit chaotic , everybody plays all the time and in the same time . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I wanted to go and see how by improvising completely with such a group of people , we could create something that sounds really like a composition , like something that actually could have been written beforehand . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; With willing collaborators , Pilc has met on more or less a weekly basis , recording sessions on video to scrutinize the process after the heat of the moment . Participants keep journals of their experiences , adding another dimension to the project . All aspects of the project are posted at its website , &lt;a href= " http : **27;13831;TOOLONG " &gt;im ... &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Most of the project&amp;rsquo ; s participants , while conversant in jazz playing , are less familiar with entirely improvised playing . Pilc counts himself among them . &amp;ldquo ; To be quite honest with you , until recent years , I was myself scared to do it , &amp;rdquo ; he says . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; But it&amp;rsquo ; s interesting , those people are going to want to discover something about themselves that they didn&amp;rsquo ; t know before . They&amp;rsquo ; re going to participate to the collective &amp;lsquo ; writing , &amp;rsquo ; in the moment , which in my mind is what this project is about . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I call it paperless instant composition if you want to be more specific , &amp;rdquo ; he says . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In the fall of 2018 , a year after the project was launched , it landed the regular gig at Resonance Cafe . &amp;ldquo ; We got lucky , &amp;rdquo ; Pilc says . &amp;ldquo ; That changed completely the course of the project to me , because playing live every other week changed completely the spirit . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; So far , the project has yielded several big discoveries , Pilc says . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The first is that the project &amp;ldquo ; has a personality , almost like it was a person , &amp;rdquo ; regardless of who is taking part , he says . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; There is this thing that transcends the musicians who are contributing , an interesting phenomenon of a certain mental state , that tends to instil itself , no matter who the musicians are . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Secondly , Pilc says the project has helped him rediscover the importance of the the most basic fundamentals of music . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We had to use very simple techniques and ideas so that the music would really work , &amp;rdquo ; he says . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-family : -apple-system , BlinkMacSystemFont , ' Segoe UI ' , Roboto , Oxygen-Sans , Ubuntu , Cantarell , ' Helvetica Neue ' , sans-serif ; " &gt; " T&lt;/span&gt; &lt;span style= " font-family : -apple-system , BlinkMacSystemFont , ' Segoe UI ' , Roboto , Oxygen-Sans , Ubuntu , Cantarell , ' Helvetica Neue ' , sans-serif ; " &gt;he simpler the original idea , the better the music , in the way ... &lt;/span&gt; Even if the music ends up being very complex , the original idea at the core of that music was simple . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Simple melodies , simple rhythms , simple chords , simple ideas&amp;hellip ; simple anything . Simplicity leads to clarity , I think , &amp;rdquo ; Pilc says . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; He continues : &amp;ldquo ; My goal for the last year of this project will be to go toward even more simplicity . To make music that&amp;rsquo ; s even clearer , that&amp;rsquo ; s even more transparent , that&amp;rsquo ; s less encumbered by unnecessary clutter or overplaying of you know , just notes . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I think we&amp;rsquo ; re on the right path to that . I&amp;rsquo ; m happy with where we&amp;rsquo ; re going and what we&amp;rsquo ; re exploring . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; **27;13860;TOOLONG Workshop Project at L'Off Festival&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;br&gt; Featuring Jean-Michel Pilc , Kevin Dean , and R&amp;eacute ; mi Bolduc , and Elisabeth Kontomanou &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;When : &lt;/strong&gt; Thursday , Oct. 10 , 8 p.m. &lt;br&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Where : &lt;/strong&gt; Le Minist&amp;egrave ; re , 4521 Boul . Claire Devlin ( tenor saxophone ) , Pierre Mendola ( flute ) , Ananda Suddath ( guitar ) , Mike de Masi ( bass ) , Louis-Vincent Hamel ( drums ) &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " **25;13889;TOOLONG " **32;13916;TOOLONG &lt;br&gt; &lt;a href= " http : //twitter.com/peterhum " &gt;twitter ... &lt;br&gt; &lt;a href= " http : **28;13950;TOOLONG " &gt;o ... &lt;/p&gt; Peter HumWed , 09 Oct 2019 20:50:29 **45;13980;TOOLONG ... icon Rob McConnell was like a musical father to me , Mark Ferguson sayshttp : **36;14027;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;14065;TOOLONG ... title= " Pianist Mark Ferguson . The veteran Ottawa jazz musician has played on many CDs but at last is putting out his first CD solely under his own name later this month ( Jean Levac/ Ottawa Citizen ) " alt= " Pianist Mark Ferguson . The veteran Ottawa jazz musician has played on many CDs but at last is putting out his first CD solely under his own name later this month ( Jean Levac/ Ottawa Citizen ) " data-portal-copyright= " Jean Levac " **28;14101;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 727075 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; From the late 1960s to the late 1990s , Toronto-based trombonist and bandleader Rob McConnell and his Boss Brass big band loomed large over the Canadian jazz scene . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The **25;14131;TOOLONG ensemble was , in the words of its trumpeter Guido Basso , &amp;ldquo ; the only Canadian jazz band that could cross the border and play the living daylights out of U.S. musicians . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 480 " src= " https : **34;14158;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;14194;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; After almost 30 albums , the band called it quits . McConnell recorded five more albums into the early 2000s . He died in 2010 , at the age of 75. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But his memory will live on , thanks to musicians such as Ottawa trombonist , pianist and bandleader Mark Ferguson . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; At his Saturday night performance at GigSpace , the intimate 46-seat performance space on Gladstone Avenue , Ferguson will be paying tribute to McConnell , who was something of a mentor to him . Below , Ferguson describes his relationship to McConnell and his music . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Q : Why are you paying tribute to Rob McConnell ? &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A : Rob was a major influence on me and I consider him to be one of the icons of jazz . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Q : What will your tribute entail ? &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A : Usually when people pay tribute to him , it&amp;rsquo ; s in the format of a big band concert because arranging music for the Boss Brass is the thing for which Rob is most well-known . On Saturday , we&amp;rsquo ; ll be playing compositions written by Rob as well as a couple of his favourite standards that he played often in small group settings . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Q : What were your first contacts with McConnell 's music and with him personally ? &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A : In the early 1970s when I was getting interested in jazz , the CRTC introduced Canadian content rules for radio and TV . Suddenly there were several TV variety shows produced in Toronto . I kept seeing the same musicians : Rob McConnell , Moe Koffman , Guido Basso , Jimmy Dale , Ed Bickert , Don Thompson and Terry Clarke . I was fascinated by the fact that Rob played the valve trombone and was blown away by his sound and his ability to swing . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 480 " src= " https : **34;14230;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;14266;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; When I was 19 , I attended a Phil Nimmons clinic in Fredericton , N.B. , so I could meet Rob and get some lessons with him . We developed almost a father-son type relationship . He had the reputation of being crusty and impatient with people but he was always kind to me . Meeting him inspired me to move to Toronto and study at Humber College . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Q : How has McConnell influenced your trombone playing and your composing and arranging ? &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A : Living in Toronto in the 1980s , I attended countless Boss Brass concerts and heard Rob play in jazz clubs many times with small groups . I was hired a few times to sub in the Boss Brass trombone section , which was a total thrill for me. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In the early 1990s I received a Canada Council for the Arts grant to study arranging with Rob . During the lessons , I got a lot of insight into how he approached composition and arranging . He was hard on me and didn&amp;rsquo ; t pull any punches when he thought my work was substandard . It was exactly what I needed at the time . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Q : What did you do to pull together the material for Saturday 's concert ? &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A : I had a lot of fun transcribing Rob&amp;rsquo ; s compositions for this concert . As far as I know , they&amp;rsquo ; re not available in print and I prefer learning tunes from recordings rather than just reading them anyway . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Q : On Oct. 20 , you 'll be playing at Merrickville 's Jazz Fest , but in a quartet with Ottawa saxophonist Mike Tremblay . What will that gig entail ? &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A : We&amp;rsquo ; ll be performing original material from our recently released CD , Appleface . It&amp;rsquo ; s an unusual title that makes reference to Mike&amp;rsquo ; s wife&amp;rsquo ; s horse . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Mike and I made a duo recording in 2008 that consisted of music associated with the great jazz pianist Bill Evans . For Appleface , we decided to record all originals composed by either me or Mike . We have a great friendship , and I think it affects our musical communication in a very positive way . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Mark Ferguson Trio&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;br&gt; &lt;strong&gt;When : &lt;/strong&gt; Saturday , Oct. 12 , 7:30 p.m. &lt;br&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Where : &lt;/strong&gt; GigSpace ( 953 Gladstone Ave. ) &lt;br&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Tickets : &lt;/strong&gt; $25 at &lt;a href= " http : //gigspace.ca " &gt;gigspace.ca&lt;/ ... or at the door &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Mark Ferguson / Tremblay Quartet&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;br&gt; &lt;strong&gt;When : &lt;/strong&gt; Sunday , Oct. 20 , 4 p.m. &lt;br&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Where : &lt;/strong&gt; Merrickville United Arts Centre &lt;br&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Tickets and festival passes : &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;a href= " http : **27;14302;TOOLONG " &gt;me ... &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " **25;14331;TOOLONG " **32;14358;TOOLONG &lt;br&gt; &lt;a href= " http : //twitter.com/peterhum " &gt;twitter ... &lt;br&gt; &lt;a href= " http : **28;14392;TOOLONG " &gt;o ... &lt;/p&gt; Peter HumWed , 09 Oct 2019 13:54:07 **45;14422;TOOLONG ... Photo credit : Sebastien Veilleux " data-portal-copyright= " " **28;14538;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1470698 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Although Carl Mayotte was born in the mid-1990s , it&amp;rsquo ; s music from the 1960s and 1970s that most inspires him . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I was always in search of weird music , &amp;rdquo ; the 25-year-old Quebec City-based electric bassist says . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; The &amp;rsquo ; 60s and &amp;rsquo ; 70s era was a sea of creativity and strange music . The jazz-rock scene and the prog rock albums are references for me , inspiring me with their spirituality and expression . I think that these years were the greatest moments for the musicians because virtuosity and creativity were praised by the public . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Mayotte and his quintet will unveil their update on classic jazz-rock when they launch their new album , Le Fantosme , with a concert Saturday night at Record Runner Studios in Nepean. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **34;14568;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;14604;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; To create this album , I was able to live a dream , &amp;rdquo ; Mayotte says . He adds that his latest compositions pay tribute to &amp;ldquo ; Weather Report&amp;rsquo ; s groove , Frank Zappa&amp;rsquo ; s madness , Brazilian influences from Hermeto Pascoal , virtuosity from Chick Corea and Pat Metheny&amp;rsquo ; s melody . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Mayotte has been playing bass since he was 13 and received one for Christmas . His father , a blues guitarist , was his first teacher . &amp;ldquo ; Since then , it&amp;rsquo ; s been a love story between the bass and me , &amp;rdquo ; Mayotte says . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; He progressed as a musician , playing by ear along with YouTube videos . He studied music after high school , attending Laval University . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Those years were moments of intense work and incredible discoveries , working seven to nine hours a day , and writing a lot of music , &amp;rdquo ; he says . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Then he moved to Montreal to get a Master&amp;rsquo ; s degree at McGill University . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; After three years at the same university and six years as a professional musician in the same town , I felt the need to see what was happening in other places , &amp;rdquo ; Mayotte says . &amp;ldquo ; To me , it is always important to push yourself and go beyond your safe place . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Still , he returned to Quebec City upon graduating . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Quebec City is a real gem . For the size of the place you can almost have jazz every night . The talent there is really high . Especially with the young cats that leave university and the old guys that are just incredible . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **34;14640;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;14676;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Every time I play a Quebec City show I always feel listened to , complimented and I found that sometimes it can be easier to live as a professional musician than in other bigger towns . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; As a professional musician there , I play every kind of gig &amp;mdash ; jazz gigs , country gigs , duo gigs , wedding gigs and more . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **34;14712;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;14748;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; But as busy as he is , Mayotte found time to perform &amp;mdash ; and prevail &amp;mdash ; with his top-priority quintet .
