ArtsRIP , jazz piano great Harold Mabernhttp : **34;15034;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;15070;TOOLONG ... title= " Jazz pianist Harold Mabern plays Salt Restaurant on Preston Street Aug. 15 with Toronto saxophonist Kirk MacDonald " alt= " Jazz pianist Harold Mabern plays Salt Restaurant on Preston Street Aug. 15 with Toronto saxophonist Kirk MacDonald " data-portal-copyright= " Alan Nahigian " **28;15106;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 593651 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Harold Mabern , a Memphis-raised pianist beloved for his soulfulness and power at the keyboard and his gift of gab off the bandstand , has died , his record label Smoke Sessions Records announced Thursday . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Mabern , who continued to tour , record and play in New York&amp;rsquo ; s jazz clubs with players half his age , was 83 . WBGO&amp;rsquo ; s Nate Chinen reports &lt;a href= " https : **33;15136;TOOLONG ... that Mabern died of a heart attack , and that he is survived by two children and a granddaughter , while his wife , Beatrice , died in 2010. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Word of Mabern&amp;rsquo ; s passing prompted tributes throughout the online jazz community . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;15171;TOOLONG ... &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;15206;TOOLONG ... &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;15241;TOOLONG ... &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;15276;TOOLONG ... &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Guitarist Peter Bernstein wrote on Facebook : &amp;ldquo ; Mr. Mabes I am having trouble finding words to say goodbye to you . It&amp;rsquo ; s hard to imagine the world without you . A true master of music but also of giving , you are generosity personified . All we can do is remember you and try to be better people . R.I.P . Maestro Mabern. &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;div&gt;Born on March 20 , 1936 , the musically self-taught Mabern grew up under the influence of the stellar pianist Phineas Newborn Jr . and was soon running with such peers as Frank Strozier , Booker Little and George Coleman . Mabern and Coleman were friends throughout the pianist 's lifetime , and Mabern plays on Coleman 's upcoming album , The Quartet . &lt;/div&gt; &lt;div&gt;&lt;span style= " font-family : -apple-system , BlinkMacSystemFont , ' Segoe UI ' , Roboto , Oxygen-Sans , Ubuntu , Cantarell , ' Helvetica Neue ' , sans-serif ; " &gt;&lt;br&gt; Mabern moved to Chicago in 1954 , where he played with such elite saxophonists as Johnny Griffin , Gene Ammons and Clifford Jordan . Five years later , he moved to New York , where he would join the groups of Lionel Hampton and Art Farmer and Benny Golson 's Jazztet , Miles Davis and Wes Montgomery . **35;15311;TOOLONG ... &lt;div&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 480 " src= " https : **34;15348;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;15384;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;/div&gt; &lt;div&gt;&lt;/div&gt; &lt;div&gt;Mabern released more than 20 albums as a leader . A favourite of mine is the trio album Straight Street , a Japanese recording that also features bassist Ron Carter and drummer Jack DeJohnette . Mabern appears on almost 100 albums as a sideman. &lt;/div&gt; &lt;div&gt; Mabern taught for more than three decades at New Jersey 's William Paterson University , where his students included drummers Joe Farnsworth and saxophonist Eric Alexander , two younger musicians who would join him on innumerable performances after they graduated . &lt;/div&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-family : -apple-system , BlinkMacSystemFont , ' Segoe UI ' , Roboto , Oxygen-Sans , Ubuntu , Cantarell , ' Helvetica Neue ' , sans-serif ; " &gt;Until his passing , Mabern was the quintessential New York jazzman working in the trenches , playing past midnight at Smalls Jazz Club or Smoke , two bastions of straight-ahead , swinging music . &lt;br&gt;&lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;15420;TOOLONG ... title= " Harold Mabern , hitting hard at Smalls in September 2017 " alt= " Harold Mabern , hitting hard at Smalls in September 2017 " data-portal-copyright= " " **28;15456;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1243172 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-family : -apple-system , BlinkMacSystemFont , ' Segoe UI ' , Roboto , Oxygen-Sans , Ubuntu , Cantarell , ' Helvetica Neue ' , sans-serif ; " &gt;In recent years&lt;/span&gt; &lt;span style= " font-family : -apple-system , BlinkMacSystemFont , ' Segoe UI ' , Roboto , Oxygen-Sans , Ubuntu , Cantarell , ' Helvetica Neue ' , sans-serif ; " &gt; , Mabern developed more connections with Canada 's jazz scene . He played and recorded with saxophonists Kirk MacDonald and Cory Weeds , and is featured on two of MacDonald 's most recent albums , including the Juno-winning Vista Obscura . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Here are clips of Mabern with MacDonald ( playing in Montreal ) and Weeds ( playing in Vancouver ) , throwing himself into some minor blues with his driving , swirling approach . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **34;15486;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;15522;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; &amp;nbsp ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **34;15558;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;15594;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; On Facebook , MacDonald wrote : &amp;ldquo ; I was shocked and deeply saddened to hear the devastating news of Harold Mabern&amp;rsquo ; s passing . We have lost a musical giant ! Harold&amp;rsquo ; s spirit and unconditional generosity with his gifts and wisdom was something to behold . Harold was a torchbearer who was an inspiration to his peers and a great mentor and inspiration to so many musicians of all generations around the world for decades . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I feel fortunate to have known Harold and honoured to have shared the bandstand with him numerous times over the past five years . My deepest condolences to the Mabern family and Harold&amp;rsquo ; s extended musical family . Thank you Harold for the music and inspiration . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Mabern played with MacDonald in Ottawa in the summer of 2015 . I was fortunate enough to interview him in advance of that gig , and below is the story that resulted . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; *** &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-family : -apple-system , BlinkMacSystemFont , ' Segoe UI ' , Roboto , Oxygen-Sans , Ubuntu , Cantarell , ' Helvetica Neue ' , sans-serif ; " &gt;Harold Mabern was a late starter when it came to jazz piano , but he 's more than made up for it with his longevity . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Brooklyn-based pianist , 79 , didn&amp;rsquo ; t start playing his instrument until he was 15 , growing up in Memphis , Tennessee . But within a decade , the self-taught musician was accompanying the cream of the jazz crop in New York , right up to Miles Davis and Sonny Rollins , as well as singers Sarah Vaughan and Betty Carter . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In the last decade , he&amp;rsquo ; s only grown busier , recording as a leader and sideman and touring , frequently with former students whom&amp;nbsp ; he taught at William Paterson University in New Jersey . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; His appeal extends beyond the United States , such that Toronto saxophonist Kirk MacDonald enlisted Mabern to play on his most recent record , &lt;em&gt;Vista Obscura&lt;/em&gt; . This spring , that album won a Juno Award for best jazz album of the year . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; He&amp;rsquo ; s just such a great personality , &amp;rdquo ; MacDonald says of Mabern. &amp;ldquo ; His energy is infectious . It can be overwhelming &amp;hellip ; just his love for life and music . He&amp;rsquo ; s really one of the sweetest people you would ever meet . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; MacDonald and Mabern are touring&amp;nbsp ; this week and next , playing gigs from Sydney , N.S. , to Toronto . In Ottawa , the duo plays Salt Dining Lounge on Preston Street Saturday night . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Mabern , an effusive talker and storyteller who recently returned from a two-week tour in Japan , happily discussed his life as a jazzman ; an edited transcript of our&amp;nbsp ; conversation follows . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;How did you get started playing piano ? &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; I&amp;rsquo ; m completely self-taught . No shame in my game . When I was 15 , I finally got a piano for about $50 , but I still really didn&amp;rsquo ; t know what I was doing . At the time , I think I knew about four songs . &amp;nbsp ; Unfortunately , I didn&amp;rsquo ; t have the luxury of playing Chopin Etudes. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Do you play classical music now ? &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Oh no . Most jazz piano players play classical music but I don&amp;rsquo ; t fool with it . I do love classical music . Chopin is my favourite and Ravel and Debussy , and Gustav Mahler . But maybe one day I&amp;rsquo ; ll sit down and pick some notes out and see what I can do . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;How did you progress so quickly as a jazz pianist ? &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; When I moved to Chicago , that&amp;rsquo ; s when I started putting time into it . In &amp;rsquo ; 54 , I moved to Chicago . &amp;nbsp ; Music was being played in Chicago 24/7. &amp;nbsp ; That&amp;rsquo ; s when I really learned how to play music . &amp;nbsp ; Music was on every corner . You had great pianists on every corner who could play . The camaraderie was very good . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Chicago , it&amp;rsquo ; s very diverse . You had to play all kinds of music . You had to play blues . You had to play for singers . I tell students the best way to be a great jazz pianist is to play for singers . That will teach you how to be a very good accompanist . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In Memphis , we had to play rhythm &amp;lsquo ; n&amp;rsquo ; blues . We didn&amp;rsquo ; t want to play rhythm &amp;lsquo ; n&amp;rsquo ; blues . We wanted to play bebop . And then as you look back , you say , &amp;ldquo ; Oh boy , what a pleasure , &amp;rdquo ; because as you know everybody can&amp;rsquo ; t play the blues . You can&amp;rsquo ; t teach that . It&amp;rsquo ; s something you can do or can&amp;rsquo ; t do and it has to do with the environment . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In Chicago , all the musicians could play anybody&amp;rsquo ; s music . How did that happen ? I don&amp;rsquo ; t know . We just listened . If Dizzy Gillespie came and Wynton Kelly , my hero , couldn&amp;rsquo ; t make it , I could go in and play the whole book without the music . If Bobby Timmons couldn&amp;rsquo ; t finish the gig with Art Blakey , I could play that music . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; We were playing and practising music 12 hours a day . I had a lot to do to get ready for&amp;nbsp ; New York . I&amp;rsquo ; m still getting ready for New York . You never stop learning . The day you stop being a student , you might as well pack it in. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;How did you start making a mark in New York ? &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; My first gig was with Harry &amp;ldquo ; Sweets&amp;rdquo ; Edison. &amp;nbsp ; One night ( saxophonist ) Cannonball Adderley said , &amp;ldquo ; Hey , Big Hands , you want a gig ? &amp;rdquo ; He took me down to Birdland . Tuesday night , the place was packed . Must have been 15 piano players in Birdland that night . Cannonball introduced me to &amp;ldquo ; Sweets&amp;rdquo ; and &amp;ldquo ; Sweets&amp;rdquo ; says , &amp;ldquo ; You want to play ? &amp;rdquo ; I say &amp;ldquo ; yeah . &amp;rdquo ; I&amp;rsquo ; m nervous . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; I tell young students now , the old-timers assumed if you&amp;rsquo ; re a musician , you knew everything . They didn&amp;rsquo ; t ask you what you knew . So &amp;ldquo ; Sweets&amp;rdquo ; looked at me and he said , &amp;ldquo ; Habit . Eight-bar introduction . In A-flat. &amp;rdquo ; I thought , what&amp;rsquo ; s &amp;ldquo ; Habit ? &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The song was &lt;em&gt;You 're Getting To Be A Habit To Me&lt;/em&gt; . I fumbled the first chorus . The second chorus , I had it . He hired me on the spot . That was the first song I played in New York City . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;What was it like to play with Miles Davis in the early 1960s ? &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; I can&amp;rsquo ; t believe that the six weeks I worked with Miles nobody had a tape recorder there to record ! &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It was a wonderful experience for me playing with Miles . He was very inspirational to me . He was saying ( imitates Davis&amp;rsquo ; raspy voice ) : &amp;ldquo ; You sure can swing . &amp;rdquo ; If Miles Davis was saying I can swing , that&amp;rsquo ; s all I needed to know . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;You 're coming to Canada right after a two-week tour of Japan . How was that ? &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; We call New York the Big Apple , but as far as we&amp;rsquo ; re concerned , my friends and I , we say Tokyo , Japan , kicks New York&amp;rsquo ; s butt . First of all , there&amp;rsquo ; s 14 million people there . But the thing is , the people over there are so honest . Their mindset is they&amp;rsquo ; re so honourable , they take such great pride in their work . They treat you like a super-duper human being . I always tell people , in Japan they&amp;rsquo ; ve got money for two things : tall buildings and jazz . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; I&amp;rsquo ; ve been over there 27 times . I love me some Tokyo , Japan , and when we come back to the United States , I get depressed . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;How are you enjoying playing with Kirk MacDonald ? &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Oh , love it . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; This date I did with him , that&amp;rsquo ; s probably the hardest record date that I&amp;rsquo ; ve ever done . He wrote some music , man . He&amp;rsquo ; s got one tune &amp;hellip ; I thought , &amp;ldquo ; If I can&amp;rsquo ; t hear this song , I can read it . &amp;rdquo ; I was up all night . It was only this one song , with a couple of bars I just couldn&amp;rsquo ; t get . Finally ( bassist ) Neil Swainson told me how to look at it and we were able to get two takes . I said , &amp;ldquo ; Sshh , don&amp;rsquo ; t nobody say a word because I don&amp;rsquo ; t want to have to do another . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; He&amp;rsquo ; s a very challenging player . Excellent tenor player . It&amp;rsquo ; s definitely an enjoyable moment to play with him . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;You 'll turn 80 next year . Do you expect you 'll slow down ? &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The only time I get tired is when I&amp;rsquo ; m not playing . I&amp;rsquo ; m blessed . I&amp;rsquo ; m booked up for the most part until April 2016 . As long as I feel I can work , let me work . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; *** &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Rest in peace , Harold Mabern. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " **29;15630;TOOLONG " target= " blank " rel= " noopener " **36;15661;TOOLONG &lt;br&gt; &lt;a href= " http : //twitter.com/peterhum " target= " blank " rel= " noopener " **30;15699;TOOLONG &; ... &lt;br&gt; &lt;a href= " http : **28;15731;TOOLONG " target= " blank " rel= " noopener " **30;15761;TOOLONG &; ... &lt;/p&gt; Peter HumFri , 20 Sep 2019 21:33:15 **45;15793;TOOLONG ... TuneTown takes the chord-less trio challengehttp : **31;15840;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;15873;TOOLONG ... title= " The Toronto jazz trio TuneTown , which consists of , left to right , saxophonist Kelly Jefferson , drummer Ernesto Cervini and bassist Artie Roth . The band plays GigSpace in Ottawa on Sept. 21/2019. " alt= " The Toronto jazz trio TuneTown , which consists of , left to right , saxophonist Kelly Jefferson , drummer Ernesto Cervini and bassist Artie Roth . The band plays GigSpace in Ottawa on Sept. 21/2019. " data-portal-copyright= " Jeff Kirk " **28;15909;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1467558 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Individually , saxophonist Kelly Jefferson , bassist Artie Roth and drummer Ernesto Cervini have amassed impressive credits on Toronto&amp;rsquo ; s jazz scene . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But after many years out and about , they recently joined forces as the collective trio TuneTown , which will launch its debut album this week with concerts in Toronto , Montreal and Ottawa . The band plays GigSpace , an intimate 46-seat venue on Gladstone Avenue , on Saturday night . Below , Roth discusses why the band went from a one-off gig to a full-fledged project and what distinguishes them from similar bands . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;div class= " 3U2q6dcdZCrTrR42Nxby JWNdg1hee9Rz6bIGvG1c allowTextSelection " &gt; &lt;div dir= " auto " &gt; **26;15939;TOOLONG : How and why did TuneTown form ? **27;15967;TOOLONG &lt;div&gt;&lt;/div&gt; &lt;div&gt;A : We formed TuneTown in 2016 . All of us have had long-term musical affiliations in various projects spanning as far back as 15 years , so there was already a bit of a history . &lt;/div&gt; &lt;div&gt;&lt;/div&gt; &lt;div&gt;I think there was one particular gig at the Rex . I had a month-long series of the early set on Fridays as a leader , and I was just experimenting with different configurations of musicians . I think at the time , Ernesto had recently moved back to Toronto from New York , or I think he was bouncing back and forth between the two places . On this Rex gig , we started to play , I think either a standard or one of my originals , and everything just felt really sympathetic , but not artistically safe . I felt like right off the bat , we could just go , take risks , help one another if one of those risks did n't go as planned . Basically a scenario where really adventurous creativity happens . That gig was just this happening , and we all reminisced about it whenever we saw one another . &lt;/div&gt; &lt;div&gt;&lt;/div&gt; &lt;div&gt;In 2016 , Ernesto took the plunge , and got in touch with Kelly and me and suggested , why not do that again ? But with a goal of actually making it a project so that it happens a lot . &lt;/div&gt; &lt;div&gt;&lt;/div&gt; **26;15996;TOOLONG : What 's the significance of the band 's name ? **27;16024;TOOLONG &lt;div&gt;&lt;span style= " font-family : -apple-system , BlinkMacSystemFont , ' Segoe UI ' , Roboto , Oxygen-Sans , Ubuntu , Cantarell , ' Helvetica Neue ' , sans-serif ; " &gt;&lt;br&gt; A : The band name was Ernesto 's idea . Maybe my hearing is going -- when he first told me , I thought he said Teen Town , a virtuosic electric bass piece by Jaco Pastorius . I thought , " Oh no , does that mean I have to try and play that on double bass ? Yikes ! " **25;16053;TOOLONG &lt;div&gt; &lt;p&gt;The name to me epitomizes Ernesto 's sense of humour -- a little impish , innocent , a bit off the wall if you were n't looking . Like TuneTown is some place where crazy musicians go to create music in an entirely unselfconscious way . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;/div&gt; &lt;div&gt;On an even funnier note , Kelly , who was born and raised in Regina , told us later that people in the prairie provinces call Saskatoon " Toontown . " Go figure . &lt;/div&gt; &lt;div&gt;&lt;/div&gt; **33;16080;TOOLONG style= " font-family : -apple-system , BlinkMacSystemFont , ' Segoe UI ' , Roboto , Oxygen-Sans , Ubuntu , Cantarell , ' Helvetica Neue ' , sans-serif ; " &gt;Q : What 's the appeal for all of you of this instrumentation , without a chordal instrument in the band ? **40;16115;TOOLONG &lt;div&gt;&lt;/div&gt; &lt;div&gt;A : Playing in a chord-less trio is daring . I could never resist a musical dare . &lt;/div&gt; &lt;div&gt;&lt;/div&gt; &lt;div&gt;This instrumentation is extremely challenging for musicians and audiences . The biggest challenge is not making it sound like it was originally a quartet , but the chordal instrument player did n't show up . &lt;/div&gt; &lt;div&gt;&lt;/div&gt; &lt;div&gt;When I first started playing , I remember my first sax/bass/drums trio gig and I felt totally exposed , vulnerable . To me , chordal instruments are often the glue holding all the disparate lines and rhythms in music together . So when you play in this chord-less trio configuration , you have to let go of that **25;16157;TOOLONG and embrace the absence of a chordal instrument as liberating , and as an opportunity to bring together two or three melodic lines and make that sound like a complete musical entity . &lt;/div&gt; &lt;div&gt;&lt;/div&gt; **33;16184;TOOLONG style= " font-family : -apple-system , BlinkMacSystemFont , ' Segoe UI ' , Roboto , Oxygen-Sans , Ubuntu , Cantarell , ' Helvetica Neue ' , sans-serif ; " &gt;Q : What do you think makes the trio stand out compared to other saxophone-bass-drums trios ? **40;16219;TOOLONG ... &lt;div&gt;&lt;/div&gt; &lt;div&gt;A : I think we could all reference some of the classic trios with this instrumentation . Standouts for me are the Sonny Rollins trio with Wilbur Ware on bass and Elvin Jones on drums , the ultimate Elvin Jones trio with Jimmy Garrison on bass , and Joe Farrell on sax , and the Dave Holland trio with Steve Coleman on sax and Jack DeJohnette on drums . &lt;/div&gt; &lt;div&gt;&lt;/div&gt; &lt;div&gt;It 's hard for me to put in to words how awe-inspiring these groups are , so when you suggest standing out , well that almost feels presumptuous . One thing we did was personalize and experiment with the configuration sonically , using unorthodox extended techniques on our instruments . There From Here ( the album ) also uses studio post-production and effects to our advantage thanks to the help of our producer Roman Klun . We also decided to record some little free improv segues between the songs and do this in live performance . These little interludes act as preambles to the next song or sometimes a reflective postlude to what just occurred . I ca n't think of a trio album of this instrumental configuration that has done this . &lt;/div&gt; &lt;div&gt;&lt;/div&gt; **26;16261;TOOLONG : What hopes do you have for the band and its album ? **27;16289;TOOLONG &lt;div&gt;&lt;/div&gt; &lt;div&gt;A : TuneTown has a beautiful momentum . My hope is to continue this and develop a reciprocal relationship between us and the audience . I mentioned earlier that this is a challenging configuration for audiences without the binding quality of a harmonic instrument . Further to that , we are all developing as composers and contributing to this project in that capacity as well , so I hope the journey continues to be daring and fun for us . There From Here is really our opening statement , we have a lot to say , and look forward to sharing that with the audience . &lt;/div&gt; &lt;/div&gt; &lt;div&gt;&lt;/div&gt; &lt;div&gt;https : //www.youtube.com/watch ? v=Yx ... &lt;/div&gt; &lt;div dir= " ltr " &gt;&lt;/div&gt; &lt;div dir= " ltr " **36;16318;TOOLONG ... Jazz Checkup II ( Carlos Jim ? nez , Simon Legault albums reviewed ) http : **31;16543;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;16576;TOOLONG ... title= " Montreal jazz guitarist Carlos Jimenez " alt= " Montreal jazz guitarist Carlos Jimenez " data-portal-copyright= " Amelie Fortin " **28;16612;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1456719 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Do n't Fall ( self-released ) &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;br&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Carlos Jim ? nez Quintet&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The five Montreal-based musicians on guitarist Carlos Jim&amp;eacute ; nez&amp;rsquo ; s recently released album Don&amp;rsquo ; t Fall are exceedingly well-schooled . All have either a Master&amp;rsquo ; s degree , or a bachelor&amp;rsquo ; s degree , or both , from McGill University&amp;rsquo ; s jazz program . Come to think of it , Jimenez and his bandmates are also , like so many jazz players these days , revered educators. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But there&amp;rsquo ; s very little academic or ivory tower about the music on Don&amp;rsquo ; t Fall . Granted , on Jim&amp;eacute ; nez&amp;rsquo ; s album there are choice tunes by Wayne Shorter ( Virgo ) and Hank Mobley ( East of the Village ) that attest to jazz erudition . Indeed , McGill , specifically some years ago when the guitarist , alto saxophonist Alexandre C&amp;ocirc ; t&amp;eacute ; , pianist Pierre Fran&amp;ccedil ; ois , bassist Dave Watts and drummer Dave Laing respectively attended , was known for enshrining bebop , hardbop and postbop on its curriculum . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Still , what shines through on Jimenez&amp;rsquo ; s album is how much he and his peers have internalized the jazz tradition , making its fire , flow and swing their own . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **33;16642;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;16677;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; The bulk of Don&amp;rsquo ; t Fall consists of six originals by Jimenez . The album opens with Keep It Simple , a meaty , swinging tune with a piquant , yin-yang sense of harmony . Come solo time , Jimenez is poised and incisive and then Cot&amp;eacute ; is swaggering and passionate . The title track charges harder and faster , swapping episodes of bracing swing and punchy funk before Jimenez , C&amp;ocirc ; t&amp;eacute ; and Fran&amp;ccedil ; ois show off the bopping eloquence they can muster at a bright tempo . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 100% " height= " 120 " style= " position : relative ; display : block ; width : 100% ; height : 120px ; " src= " **40;16713;TOOLONG ... allowtransparency= " true " frameborder= " 0 " &gt;&lt;/iframe&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Fifth Element is a twisty 5/4 minor blues that keeps the album&amp;rsquo ; s tension high until the delicate Shorter-penned ballad releases it . C&amp;ocirc ; t&amp;eacute ; sits this one out , allowing Jim&amp;eacute ; nez with his glowing sound and then Fran&amp;ccedil ; ois to step forward . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Still Ringing , Jim&amp;eacute ; nez&amp;rsquo ; s tribute to the late Montreal guitarist Richard Ring , is a 3/4 contrafact on This Is New , which supplies bassist Dave Watts with a wending harmonic path to negotiate before C&amp;ocirc ; t&amp;eacute ; torches the joint and Jimenez and Fran&amp;ccedil ; ois offers more burnished solos . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; While Jim&amp;eacute ; nez on his website describes Don&amp;rsquo ; t Fall as an album &amp;ldquo ; inspired by the jazz of the 1960s , &amp;rdquo ; I think there&amp;rsquo ; s an appealing touch of the 1990s to the theme of The Process , which admittedly pivots back to rangy swinging . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Samba New Life is the disc&amp;rsquo ; s bouncy penultimate number . To close Don&amp;rsquo ; t Fall , Jimenez offers a faithful rendition of saxophonist Mobley&amp;rsquo ; s tune East Of The Village . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Jim&amp;eacute ; nez and his quintet launch Don&amp;rsquo ; t Fall on Friday , Sept. 6 at 6:30 p.m. at Diese Onze in Montreal . If I could , I&amp;rsquo ; d plant myself down in a seat there and brace myself for an exciting , invigorating set . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Liminal Spaces ( Effendi Records ) &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;br&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Simon Legault&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The last time I heard from Montreal guitarist Simon Legault was a few years ago , when he put out his impressively modern quintet album called Hypnagogia Polis. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; With his new album , Liminal Spaces , Legault has shifted gears and even downsized . His playing and composing are still contemporary in sound and concept , but he&amp;rsquo ; s presenting them now in a trio format , with bassist Adrian Vedady and drummer Michel Lambert . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; While Vedady played on Legault&amp;rsquo ; s previous disc , veteran Montreal drummer Lambert is apparently a newer collaborator for Legault , who says that Liminal Spaces and its material grew out of weekly sessions with Lambert and a range of bassists. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; You might guess then that Legault wanted to see what sparks would fly from the combination of his own glowing , agile playing and Lambert&amp;rsquo ; s rugged , free-spirited drumming . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The hour-long album consists of 13 tracks , of which four are solo pieces that are thick with the kind of mystery , atmosphere and sophistication that a fan of guitar genius Ben Monder would warm to . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The album&amp;rsquo ; s trio maintain that rarefied vibe , from the surging title track that opens the disc with a combination of reverb-y spaciousness and charged , tensile playing , &amp;hellip ; . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 400 " scrolling= " no " frameborder= " no " src= " https : **26;16755;TOOLONG ? visual= ... &lt;p&gt; to the open swinging tune Inflexion&amp;hellip ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 400 " scrolling= " no " frameborder= " no " src= " https : **26;16783;TOOLONG ? visual= ... &lt;p&gt; to A Moment Ago , which begins as a plaintive rubato ballad but isn&amp;rsquo ; t afraid to increase its tension . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 400 " scrolling= " no " frameborder= " no " src= " https : **26;16811;TOOLONG ? visual= ... &lt;p&gt; Legault is a guitarist who likes to swim in deep musical waters . Given the clarity , expressiveness and flow of his playing , he&amp;rsquo ; s able to take listeners with him on his explorations . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " **25;16839;TOOLONG " **32;16866;TOOLONG &lt;br&gt; &lt;a href= " http : //twitter.com/peterhum " &gt;twitter ... &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;nbsp ; &lt;/p&gt; Peter HumWed , 04 Sep 2019 20:15:46 **45;16900;TOOLONG ... into Jazz From Detroit ( book review ) http : **33;16947;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;16982;TOOLONG ... title= " Detroit-raised jazz drumming great Elvin Jones visited Ottawa in 1999 " alt= " Detroit-raised jazz drumming great Elvin Jones visited Ottawa in 1999 " data-portal-copyright= " " **28;17018;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 244212 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Jazz From Detroit ( University of Michigan Press ) &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;br&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Mark Stryker&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Until just a few days ago , my plan was to wing my way to Detroit for this weekend&amp;rsquo ; s 40th annual Detroit Jazz Festival , where such world-class stars as Danilo Perez , Terence Blanchard , Pat Metheny and Joe Lovano would be playing , not to mention local and locally raised heavyweights such as Ron Carter , Sheila Jordan , Kenny Garrett , Rodney Whitaker , Regina Carter and Marion Hayden . To say the very least , I was stoked . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **34;17048;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;17084;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; Life , though , has a way of throwing curve balls . I tore my right Achilles&amp;rsquo ; tendon earlier this week , effectively rendering myself housebound . I hope lots of other people I know will take in the Detroit Jazz Festival &amp;mdash ; which , amazingly , is a free-admission event &amp;mdash ; and let me know how great the music was . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; My consolation will be re-reading Mark Stryker&amp;rsquo ; s recently released book Jazz From Detroit , which was my companion at the cottage last month . &amp;nbsp ; The recently retired Detroit Free Press arts writer has produced a definitive and enthralling reference work on the exceptional yet unsung jazz scene in his hometown , from the 1940s to the present day . The book was clearly a labour of love for Stryker , who , it should be said , is a saxophonist on the side . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Jazz From Detroit is chronologically ordered and bursting with context , so that a reader gains insights into not only the evolution of jazz there but also into the city&amp;rsquo ; s fortunes , from Motor City powerhouse to a metropolis hollowed by economic decline to a 2010s comeback story . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Above all , Stryker&amp;rsquo ; s book presents more than two dozen profiles of Detroit&amp;rsquo ; s gifts to jazz at large , from boppers such as Milt Jackson , Barry Harris , Sheila Jordan and Tommy Flanagan who developed their talents early in Detroit&amp;rsquo ; s vibrant musical community , to the seminal Jones brothers &amp;mdash ; cornetist and bandleader Thad , pianist Hank and drummer Elvin &amp;mdash ; to the subsequent wave of Detroit-raised greats such as trumpeter and essential mentor Marcus Belgrave and pianist Geri Allen to the current crop of the city&amp;rsquo ; s up-and-comers. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; I found every profile a breeze to read through and right on the money . Vignettes of the few musicians that I have met or interviewed ( pianist Harris , vocalist Jordan , saxophonist Garrett ) were concise but vividly drawn , and rang clearly of the truth . Stryker&amp;rsquo ; s reporting chops have allowed him to draw detailed portraits of fascinating people , and while he is clearly an admirer of jazz artistry , he does not turn away from mentioning or discussing the human foibles of a few of his subjects . Assessments of recordings dot Jazz From Detroit and they too are spot-on , with Stryker marshaling evocative language to describe artistic greatness , or writing with very clear eyes about music of lesser achievements . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; I enjoyed most of all Stryker&amp;rsquo ; s deep dive into Detroit&amp;rsquo ; s golden age of jazz from the 1940s to the 1960s , when giants such as saxophonist Yusef Lateef , guitarist Kenny Burrell , pianist Roland Hanna , drummer Louis Hayes , alto saxophonist Charles MacPherson and many others took their formative musical steps in Detroit before ascending to greater heights in New York . It&amp;rsquo ; s always great to be reminded of the depth of such talents . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 480 " src= " https : **34;17120;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;17156;TOOLONG &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **34;17192;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;17228;TOOLONG &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **34;17264;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;17300;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; Black and white photographs break up Stryker&amp;rsquo ; s words &amp;mdash ; there&amp;rsquo ; s an especially nice one of young Sheila Jordan , Barry Harris and bassist Doug Watkins &amp;mdash ; and the book would be lavish with more of them . But Stryker&amp;rsquo ; s writing is more than vivid enough on its own . Each chapter ends with Stryker&amp;rsquo ; s recommendations of several essential albums from its subject , which for me constituted worthwhile reminders to go back to savour Tommy Flanagan&amp;rsquo ; s album Overseas , Yusuf Lateef&amp;rsquo ; s Into Something and Live at Peps , and everything Geri Allen recorded . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In his closing pages , Stryker&amp;rsquo ; s elaborations on the talents of contemporary Detroiters such as bassist Marion Hayden and Ralphe Armstrong , pianist Michael Malis and saxophonist Marcus Elliot really whet my appetite for this year&amp;rsquo ; s festival . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **34;17336;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;17372;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; &amp;nbsp ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **34;17408;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;17444;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; The clip above shows Elliot&amp;rsquo ; s quartet with Malis at the piano , and drum star Karriem Riggins sitting in , playing the ferocious tune No. 3 by Detroit drummer Lawrence Williams , which has become a staple on Detroit&amp;rsquo ; s jazz scene . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It doesn&amp;rsquo ; t get any more Detroit than that , and Stryker&amp;rsquo ; s book compellingly lays out the strands of history and sound that led to that epitome of a performance . I&amp;rsquo ; ll enjoy re-reading Stryker&amp;rsquo ; s book until I can hopefully make it next year to hear his city&amp;rsquo ; s finest players on their swinging home turf . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;17480;TOOLONG ... title= " " alt= " " data-portal-copyright= " " **28;17516;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " **25;17546;TOOLONG " **32;17573;TOOLONG &lt;br&gt; &lt;a href= " http : //twitter.com/peterhum " &gt;twitter ... &lt;/p&gt; Peter HumSat , 31 Aug 2019 17:01:02 **45;17607;TOOLONG ... Carleton grad and singer-songwriter pianist , Cynthia Tauro readies for first tour across Ontariohttp : **33;17654;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;17689;TOOLONG ... title= " Cynthia Tauro. " alt= " Cynthia Tauro. " data-portal-copyright= " " **28;17725;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1455219 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; After 14 years of classical piano lessons , Cynthia Tauro homed in on jazz and pop at Carleton University . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The 24-year-old Carleton grad , now based in Toronto , is determined to forge her own path as a **25;17755;TOOLONG who takes Oscar Peterson and Carole King alike as heroes . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Below , Tauro , who plays LIVE ! on Elgin on Wednesday , Sept. 4 to kick off her fall touring schedule , discusses the path that has led to her recently released debut album , Moments . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Q : Tell me about your musical interests when you were growing up . &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A : I grew up in a very musical family . My mother is a singer , pianist and songwriter , and my uncles are both working musicians . Both of my grandparents are also multi-instrumentalists . I was very fortunate to be exposed to a wide range of music as a child . So in a way , I think music kind of fell into my lap . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; I&amp;rsquo ; ve always been interested in all sorts of genres , from musicals to funk , to jazz to classical . Before university , I&amp;rsquo ; d been classically trained for 14 years . However , it wasn&amp;rsquo ; t until university when I really zoned in and found what genres I loved and what types of music I loved playing . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Q : Why did you study music at Carleton ? What did you get from your studies ? &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A : The act of going away for university intrigued me . All of my older siblings had left home for post-secondary , so I felt like , &amp;ldquo ; Why not ? &amp;rdquo ; I received a scholarship to Carleton , and when I went for my audition and met a couple of the professors there , I fell in love with Ottawa and the school . I think I met Dr. James McGowan and Marg Stubbington on my audition day and they were so welcoming and kind ! &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Being around an incredibly diverse group of musicians at school influenced my love of jazz and Latin music . I met so many inspiring peers and teachers , including my private instructor for all four years , Mark Ferguson . Mark prepared me for the real world that comes after university , or during . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Q : How would you describe what you 're going for as a solo artist ? &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A : I think I&amp;rsquo ; m really just trying to constantly improve my craft . I practice and vocalize daily and am also trying to collaborate as much as possible . I&amp;rsquo ; d like to see how far I can push myself creatively and technically with my piano , voice and composition . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Q : What statement do you want to make with your debut album ? &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A : I really just wanted to get something out there that was a representation of where I was musically at that time . I had the privilege of working with George Koller who produced and played bass , as well as outstanding Toronto jazz musicians . So I&amp;rsquo ; d say it was a great way to break into the Toronto scene . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Q : How do jazz and pop mix in your music ? &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A : My musical world right now is mostly a product of my upbringing . You could always count on hearing jazz played in my house growing up . Whether it was putting on an Oscar Peterson record or having a jam session with my family after Sunday dinner , jazz was a constant . Pop has always been there as well , mostly because my older siblings introduced me to Top 40 and electronic music . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; I vividly remember my mother playing anything from my grandmother ( tapes of her singing on the radio ) , Earth , Wind &amp;amp ; Fire , Michel Camilo , Mozart and Sting on the car radio while driving us to school in the morning . So I always heard many different types of music in my everyday life . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Q : Who are some of your musical heroes and why ? &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A : The list goes on . From Bill Evans to Oscar Peterson to Carole King to Tom Jobim to John Mayer to Stevie Wonder . I appreciate and admire all of these artists . The way Bill Evans approaches his playing and just his touch &amp;mdash ; unbelievable . Carole King&amp;rsquo ; s songwriting also gets me . The feel , chromaticism and songwriting of Jobim. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Q : What are your goals for the near future ? &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A : There&amp;rsquo ; s so much going on ! I&amp;rsquo ; m excited about and looking forward to my first tour across Ontario and Quebec . My No. 1 goal is attracting a broader audience and exposing more people to my music , and part of that is releasing an EP in 2020. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Right now , I&amp;rsquo ; m writing and cultivating new songs , and trying to figure out which musical direction to take them in . I&amp;rsquo ; m also thrilled about networking more and collaborating with people in various parts of the world , growing more as an artist . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;hr&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Cynthia Tauro&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;br&gt; When : Wednesday , Sept. 4 , 8 p.m. &lt;br&gt; Where : LIVE ! on Elgin &lt;br&gt; Admission : $7 in advance , $10 at the door &lt;br&gt; Opening : Edra Silva &lt;/p&gt; &lt;hr&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;ALSO : &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;17782;TOOLONG ... the man behind the artwork you 're seeing everywhere in Ottawa&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;17817;TOOLONG ...
