ArtsCross-Canada Jazz Checkup I ( Paul Tynan , Tom Easley , Sam Wilson , Grahame Rhodes albums reviewed ) http : **31;17934;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;17967;TOOLONG ... title= " Nova Scotia jazz trumpeter Paul Tynan " alt= " Nova Scotia jazz trumpeter Paul Tynan " data-portal-copyright= " " **28;18003;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1410814 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;QuARTet ( Origin Records ) &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;br&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Paul Tynan&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Antigonish-based trumpeter Paul Tynan&amp;rsquo ; s latest album wastes no time in getting down to hard-swinging business . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; After a few pickup bars , Tynan&amp;rsquo ; s composition More Than Just A Single Road treats listeners to authoritative post-bop that recalls the early &amp;rsquo ; 80s music of Wynton Marsalis thanks to some of its harmonic and structural details . Tynan&amp;rsquo ; s lead-off solo highlights his own muscular yet lyrical approach to improvising , while pianist Dan Murphy&amp;rsquo ; s solo is a cavalcade of incisive single-note lines . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It&amp;rsquo ; s a solid start to an album that has much going for it . At the big-picture level , most striking are the diversity and consistency of QuARTet&amp;rsquo ; s subesequent five tracks . The unwavering strength of the album reflects well on Tynan , a professor at St&amp;nbsp ; Francis Xavier University , and his Chicago-based rhythm section &amp;mdash ; Murphy , bassist ( and Canadian expat ) Ashley Summers and drummer Chris Baker . Oh &amp;mdash ; did I mention that the group spent just one day in the studio ? &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The follow-up to More Than Just A Single Road is Tynan&amp;rsquo ; s gentle Latin tune It&amp;rsquo ; s All We Have To Offer , which on paper has a bit of Benny Golson&amp;rsquo ; s compositions to it , but winds up bringing Kenny Wheeler , one of Tynan&amp;rsquo ; s musical touchstones , to mind as it was performed . ( confession : I played this tune with Tynan during my one gig with him , which was a year ago. ) &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The disc&amp;rsquo ; s next three tracks collectively ratchet up the tension . The adventurous and aptly named tune Swirl begins with a masterful piano introduction before everyone goes out on a limb . The simpatico between Tynan and Murphy as they weave music in tandem is especially striking . What We&amp;rsquo ; ve Left Behind is a dour , rubato lament that goes deeply and unerringly into its vibe . Low Brow is a slamming , throbbing , rock tune that challenges Tynan and company to work wonders within its simple specifications . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The album comes to a rest with the forthright piece Everything I Have . Its prettiness hits twice as hard after the more tightly wound or unabashedly aggressive music that preceded it . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; None of these tracks point directly to the title of Tynan&amp;rsquo ; s album . For that , a fan would have to look at the disc&amp;rsquo ; s liner notes &amp;mdash ; or better still , the full-sized book that comes with the vinyl version of the release . Putting the art in QuARTet are the six images that Tynan took inspiration , one visual artwork per musical composition . Tynan&amp;rsquo ; s notes explain concrete connections that he made so that , for example , the A maj7 chord at the end of the form of It&amp;rsquo ; s All We Have To Offer is linked to the single bright light in artist Michael Barton&amp;rsquo ; s photo . That piano introduction to Swirl ? It was improvised after Tynan showed Paul Vienneau&amp;rsquo ; s image to pianist Murphy and said , &amp;ldquo ; Play this . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Tynan&amp;rsquo ; s liner notes add another layer to his music &amp;mdash ; it&amp;rsquo ; s always fun to lift the hood and see how the artistic process works . But at the same time , quARTet&amp;rsquo ; s music resonates powerfully entirely on its own . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;The Starting Point Vs . The Steep Decline ( self-released ) &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;br&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Easley Quartet&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; When Halifax bassist Tom Easley played the Montreal and Ottawa jazz festivals this summer with his eponymous quartet , the band&amp;rsquo ; s lineup had changed . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Drummer Mark Adam was still on board . But saxophonists Kenji Omae , visiting Ottawa from Antigonish where he and Easley teach at St. FX U , and Montrealer Joel Miller made up the group&amp;rsquo ; s front line . And yet , Easley&amp;rsquo ; s most recent album , The Starting Point Vs . The Steep Decline , is sax-free . Instead , its other two members are guitarists Geordie Haley and Kevin Brunkhorst. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; No offence to Omae and Miller , who surely were formidable in performance . But the pairing of Haley and Brunkhorst , the chair of St. FX&amp;rsquo ; s music department , is a special and potent one on Easley&amp;rsquo ; s album . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 100% " height= " 120 " style= " position : relative ; display : block ; width : 100% ; height : 120px ; " src= " **40;18033;TOOLONG ... allowtransparency= " true " frameborder= " 0 " &gt;&lt;/iframe&gt; &lt;p&gt; Both guitarists have expanded sonic palettes &amp;mdash ; they&amp;rsquo ; re post-Frisell players , some might say &amp;mdash ; and Easley&amp;rsquo ; s album profits from the soundscape quality of the music , which can be filled with swells and whooshes and much more processed guitar-playing than a Wes Montgomery or Grant Green might have deployed . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Brunkhorst and Haley also have a simpatico that allows each to spell the other off in very satisfying ways . Their balanced tandem playing brings to mind some stellar examples of two-guitar quartets in jazz , such as the Bill Frisell-Pat Metheny&amp;nbsp ; teamup on bassist Marc Johnson&amp;rsquo ; s 1998 album The Sound of Summer Running or the 1992 John Scofield album Grace Under Pressure , on which Sco and Frisell shared centre stage . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The disc of 11 Easley originals opens with The Dreaming . The patient tune&amp;rsquo ; s intro layers Easley&amp;rsquo ; s commanding arco playing on a bed of airy guitar work before its slow groove emerges . The tune&amp;rsquo ; s spaciousness and timbral qualities are an alluring invitation to Easley&amp;rsquo ; s album as a whole . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Starting Point is a twangy , well-drawn waltz with rich solos from the super-compatible guitars and Easley . The tempo rises for the sturdy shuffle Miguel&amp;rsquo ; s Big Day , which features the guitars digging even deeper into their sonic bag of tricks . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Open Sky is the album&amp;rsquo ; s epic , with another evocative soundscape intro prefiguring the tune&amp;rsquo ; s pulsating , anthemic theme . Deep Down , a crunchy rocking tune , pushes the musical reset button and shows just how wide stylistically Easley and company can go . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Duel is a swaggering tune that&amp;rsquo ; s slow , short and bashy . Old Tree changes the vibe with steely acoustic guitar handling the melodic duties . After Easley&amp;rsquo ; s rumbling intro , Going Wrong is a slinky , catchy 7/4 tune . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Researcher , a plaintive waltz , takes the tempo down . The Nacht sounds like it was spawned from another take of The Dreaming , reprising that defining piece before the disc&amp;rsquo ; s closer , The Steep Decline , a Metheny-ish ballad that features the return of acoustic guitar . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Practical considerations must have forced Easley to swap in Miller and Omae for Brunkhorst and Haley . Let&amp;rsquo ; s hope that next time the Easley Quartet heads west , its twin-guitar lineup will be back in full force . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;span class= " embed-youtube " style= " text-align:center ; display : block ; " &gt;&lt;iframe class='youtube-player ' type='text/html ' width= ' 640 ' height= ' 360 ' src='https : **34;18075;TOOLONG ... allowfullscreen='true ' style='border:0 ; ' **28;18111;TOOLONG ... &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Groundless Apprehensions ( self-released ) &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;br&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Sam Wilson&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; With her sophomore album , the young Halifax-based guitarist Sam Wilson has crafted a distinctive post-bop outing for her brass-forward quartet . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Joining Wilson are two profs from her alma mater , St. Francis Xavier University , trumpeter ( and Wilson&amp;rsquo ; s assistant producer ) Paul Tynan and drummer Tom Roach , plus trombonist Andrew Jackson and bassist Matt MacLennan. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Tunes such as the disc-opening Kenny Wheeler-ish waltz Mother&amp;rsquo ; s Muse , the punchy straight-eighths tunes A Tune For You and Eclipse , and the twisting album-closer Riverside Waves find the horns and Wilson&amp;rsquo ; s clean-toned guitar smartly deployed to give her light-hued music greater breadth . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Earl Grey is a pretty , stripped-down , back-beat-y tune that features Jackson&amp;rsquo ; s horn . The title track , and the intro to the rolling tune What She Said , make clear Wilson&amp;rsquo ; s sophistication as a solo guitarist . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 100% " height= " 120 " style= " position : relative ; display : block ; width : 100% ; height : 120px ; " src= " **40;18141;TOOLONG ... allowtransparency= " true " frameborder= " 0 " &gt;&lt;/iframe&gt; &lt;p&gt; Tynan solos with a beautiful sound and plenty of poise . Jackson has his own lyrical turns too , particularly on the closing track . Wilson is pulling together the components of her own personal approach . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **34;18183;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;18219;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt;Wilson is part of a double bill playing Thursday , Aug. 29 , at 8 p.m. at Art House Caf ? on Somerset Street West . The cover is $10. &lt;/em&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Giant Steps ( self-released ) &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;br&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Grahame Rhodes&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; PEI-based trombonist Grahame Rhodes flexes his considerable instrumental abilities on his latest album Giant Steps . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 100% " height= " 120 " style= " position : relative ; display : block ; width : 100% ; height : 120px ; " src= " **40;18255;TOOLONG ... allowtransparency= " true " frameborder= " 0 " &gt;&lt;/iframe&gt; &lt;p&gt; The veteran musician makes clear from the get-go that the technical bar is set high for himself and his A-list rhythm section &amp;mdash ; Toronto stalwarts Don Thompson on piano , Neil Swainson on bass and drummer Terry Clarke . A breakneck version of the John Coltrane-penned title track opens the disc , while two more testing Coltrane tunes , Moment&amp;rsquo ; s Notice and the disc-closing Impressions , are flag-wavers for jazz as a high-energy pursuit . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; On these tracks , Rhodes is keen to roar and show off his instrumental prowess at blazing tempos . On his solo introduction to You Don&amp;rsquo ; t What Love Is , Rhodes&amp;rsquo ; s range , chops and multiphonic abilities are on display before a rubato and very loose reading of the jazz standard&amp;rsquo ; s theme . On this track and elsewhere , Thompson shines with his melodicism and incisive , deeply intentional playing . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The disc also includes tracks that are seemingly less ambitious but at least as satisfying , if not more so . The coasting bossa Home At Last and the gentle waltzing parlour song Just A-Wearying For You dial down the intensity nicely . Rhodes is also an effective ballad crooner on two choice tunes , Here&amp;rsquo ; s To Life and The Folks Who Live On The Hill . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " **25;18297;TOOLONG " **32;18324;TOOLONG &lt;br&gt; &lt;a href= " http : //twitter.com/peterhum " &gt;twitter ... &lt;/p&gt; Peter HumThu , 29 Aug 2019 19:02:28 **45;18358;TOOLONG ... Ottawa Jazz Festival receives $250,000 provincial grant after allhttp : **37;18405;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;18444;TOOLONG ... title= " A sold-out crowd watches Chicago at the 2019 TD Ottawa Jazz Festival . " alt= " A sold-out crowd watches Chicago at the 2019 TD Ottawa Jazz Festival . " data-portal-copyright= " Lynn Saxberg " **28;18480;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1435439 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Ontario&amp;rsquo ; s Ministry of Tourism , Culture and Sport has reversed its decision to deny a grant application from the TD Ottawa Jazz Festival , and the event will receive $250,000 from the province&amp;rsquo ; s Celebrate Ontario program after all , the festival said Thursday in a release . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Days after the festival ended on July 1 , Nepean MPP Lisa MacLeod , the newly appointed tourism minister , &amp;ldquo ; contacted the festival to announce a review of the program had reversed the original decision , &amp;rdquo ; the release said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Just weeks before this year&amp;rsquo ; s festival began on June 21 , the event learned that its application for a $290,000 grant had been denied . In past years , the festival had always received funding from Celebrate Ontario , including $292,000 in 2018. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Overall , Celebrate Ontario funding was cut deeply this year . The program gave $13 million to more than 250 festivals and events across the province , compared to more than $20 million to a record 328 festivals in 2018. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In Ottawa , the jazz festival , which staged its 39th annual edition this year , was not alone in being rejected for Celebrate Ontario funding , which is meant to support events with a proven record of attracting tourists . Glowfair , Escapade , Italian Week , the Ottawa International Busker Festival and the Canadian Tulip Festival were shut out when the list of 2019 grant recipients was announced . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; However , late last week , MacLeod tweeted that the tulip festival would receive $69,000 from her ministry . She has not tweeted about other events that had been snubbed . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The tulip festival had requested $100,000 from Celebrate Ontario . After the festival&amp;rsquo ; s application was turned down , it decided to sell about 150 of its fibreglass and plaster sculptures as part of a fundraising effort meant to make up for the grant that was denied , according to a media report . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; At this year&amp;rsquo ; s jazz festival , before she introduced musicians at their concerts , executive producer Catherine O&amp;rsquo ; Grady asked jazz fans to contact the province to voice their support for the festival . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In Thursday&amp;rsquo ; s release , O&amp;rsquo ; Grady said : &amp;ldquo ; This happy ending could never have happened without the many emails and letters of support that our loyal audience sent to the ministry , to our local MPPs , and to the Premier&amp;rsquo ; s office . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Christine Leadman , the executive director of the Downtown Bank BIA , which organizes Glowfair , said Thursday that her event has not heard about a new offer of Celebrate Ontario money . It was shut out this year after receiving grants of $80,000 to $100,000 in each of the previous four years . As a result , the BIA scaled its event down from two stages to one . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; However , Leadman added that she did receive some good news from the ministry this week . While the BIA&amp;rsquo ; s grant application for roughly $15,000 for next February&amp;rsquo ; s Fire &amp;amp ; Ice Winter Festival was initially rejected , the BIA will receive $7,500 after all , Leadman said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Ottawa-area events that did receive&amp;nbsp ; &lt;span class= " dochighlight " &gt;Celebrate Ontario&lt;/span&gt; funding this year included the Ottawa International Writers Festival ( $52,000 ) ; Ottawa Children&amp;rsquo ; s Festival ( $98,000 ) ; RBC Bluesfest ( $250,000 ) ; SouthAsianFest ( $24,937 ) ; Latin Sparks Ottawa ( $33,438 ) ; CityFolk ( $250,000 ) ; and Ontario Festival of Small Halls , which brings concerts to Eastern Ontario venues ( $61,500 ) . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;hr&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;ALSO IN THE NEWS : &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;18510;TOOLONG ... remembers : Normandy residents honour Canada 's vets , WWII dead&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;18545;TOOLONG ... story-teller ' picked to create public art at new central library&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;18580;TOOLONG ... of summer : Duck , duck , loon&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;hr&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " **25;18615;TOOLONG " **32;18642;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; twitter.com/peterhum &lt;/p&gt; Peter HumThu , 25 Jul 2019 21:13:10 **45;18676;TOOLONG ... Arts2019 TD Ottawa Jazz Festival : A look back at the highs and lowshttp : **36;18723;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;18761;TOOLONG ... title= " Norah Jones performs at the Ottawa Jazz Festival , on Tuesday . " alt= " Norah Jones performs at the Ottawa Jazz Festival , on Tuesday . " data-portal-copyright= " Jean Levac " **28;18797;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1418980 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; When the TD Ottawa Jazz Festival turns 40 next year , its main stage will return to Confederation Park after two years in exile across Laurier Avenue in Marion Dewar Plaza , the festival&amp;rsquo ; s programming manager says . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I&amp;rsquo ; m relieved about that , &amp;rdquo ; Petr Cancura said as this year&amp;rsquo ; s event , which ran from June 21 to Canada Day , wound down . &amp;ldquo ; Next year is good . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In 2018 , the jazz festival&amp;rsquo ; s main stage had to relocate to the plaza to allow for sewer tunnel construction in the park . But the festival suffered because Confederation Park could hold much larger audiences than Marion Dewar Plaza could . The park&amp;rsquo ; s tree-ringed ambience was also more appealing . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The move was a major reason that 2018 festival &amp;ldquo ; lost a ton of money , &amp;rdquo ; the festival&amp;rsquo ; s executive producer Catherine O&amp;rsquo ; Grady has said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Ongoing construction meant the festival&amp;rsquo ; s main stage could not return to the park , its home from 1989 to 2017 , this year either . This spring , O&amp;rsquo ; Grady said that the National Capital Commission&amp;rsquo ; s new CEO , Tobi Nussbaum , told her it was not a &amp;ldquo ; given&amp;rdquo ; that the event would ever return to Confederation Park . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; An NCC spokesperson subsequently said that use of Confederation Park was to be reviewed , and that &amp;ldquo ; large-scale events&amp;rdquo ; there harmed the park&amp;rsquo ; s trees . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Cancura said the main stage would return to the park in 2020 with two provisos . The &amp;ldquo ; footprint&amp;rdquo ; of the festival in the park will be different , although the details have yet to be worked out , he said . Cancura added that it&amp;rsquo ; s unclear if the main stage will return to the park after 2020. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Meanwhile , Cancura said he was pleased with how this year&amp;rsquo ; s festival fared financially . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We&amp;rsquo ; ll be in the red , but it was a good year , that aside , &amp;rdquo ; Cancura said . The big headache money-wise was that Ontario&amp;rsquo ; s Ministry of Tourism , Culture and Sport denied the festival&amp;rsquo ; s application for a grant of almost $300,000 . For its 2018 edition , the festival received $292,000 from the Celebrate Ontario program , and it received money every year since the program was announced . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; If we didn&amp;rsquo ; t lose the money from Celebrate Ontario , we would have been in really good shape next year , &amp;rdquo ; Cancura said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-family : -apple-system , BlinkMacSystemFont , ' Segoe UI ' , Roboto , Oxygen-Sans , Ubuntu , Cantarell , ' Helvetica Neue ' , sans-serif ; " &gt; &lt;h2 class= " cat-head short " **40;18827;TOOLONG ... &lt;ul class= " relatedlinks " &gt; &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;18869;TOOLONG ... festivals scramble after last-minute loss of provincial **28;18904;TOOLONG &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;18934;TOOLONG ... Jazz Festival warns fans that NCC could boot it from Confederation Park next **25;18969;TOOLONG &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;18996;TOOLONG ... TD Ottawa Jazz Festival strives for gender-balanced **28;19031;TOOLONG &lt;/ul&gt; &lt;/span&gt; &lt;p&gt; This year , main stage concerts by Chicago and Nora Jones sold out , and each brought roughly 5,000 people to the plaza . The final concert there by hip-hop stars The Roots was nearly as popular , Cancura said . This year&amp;rsquo ; s festival was also bolstered by many nights of warm weather and sunny skies . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;19061;TOOLONG ... title= " A sold out crowd enjoyed the music of Chicago at the TD Ottawa Jazz Festival on Sunday . " alt= " A sold out crowd enjoyed the music of Chicago at the TD Ottawa Jazz Festival on Sunday . " data-portal-copyright= " Lynn Saxberg " **28;19097;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1417570 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; The festival&amp;rsquo ; s indoor shows in the National Arts Centre , which presented diverse concerts to audiences measured in the hundreds rather than in the thousands , were by and large well-attended artistic successes . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The festival&amp;rsquo ; s new initiative to strive for gender-balanced bookings helped to bring not only diversity but also some cutting-edge musicians and a specially commissioned concert to the NAC. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; On the festival&amp;rsquo ; s first night , an NAC Fourth Stage concert by reeds player and composer Anna Webber&amp;rsquo ; s trio was a marvel of elaborate music that was as visceral as it was cerebral . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;19127;TOOLONG ... title= " Anna Webber of Anna Webber 's Simple Trio performing at the NAC 's Fourth Stage as the 39th edition of the TD Ottawa Jazzfest kicks off at various venues in downtown Ottawa . " alt= " Anna Webber of Anna Webber 's Simple Trio performing at the NAC 's Fourth Stage as the 39th edition of the TD Ottawa Jazzfest kicks off at various venues in downtown Ottawa . " data-portal-copyright= " Wayne Cuddington " **28;19163;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1417753 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Webber , a Canadian expat living in New York , plus pianist Matt Mitchell and drummer John Hollenbeck surmounted the challenges of Webber&amp;rsquo ; s compositions , as if to affirm that any sufficiently advanced music is indistinguishable from magic . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Pianist Kris Davis , another Canadian expat making vital art on New York&amp;rsquo ; s creative music scene , played duets with saxophonist Ingrid Laubrock during another bracing concert . Both women contributed knotty , innovative compositions and knew how to leap into the improvisational unknown once the notes on the page were exhausted . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Other Canadians who distinguished themselves were the members of the = Jazz Orchestra , a big band assembled for this year&amp;rsquo ; s festival to help manifest its gender-parity ambitions at a concert in the NAC Studio . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Montreal composer , bandleader and saxophonist Christine Jensen directed a potent and gender-balanced ensemble of seasoned and emerging musicians from Montreal , Ottawa , Toronto and even New York . With a program that included compositions by Jensen , Webber , Montreal pianist Marianne Trudel , Toronto saxophonist Tara Davidson and Toronto trumpeter Rebecca Hennessy , the project was blessed with exceptional breadth and quality on its maiden voyage , not to mention a sense of occasion . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;19193;TOOLONG ... title= " Bandleader Christine Jensen leads the = Jazz Orchestra at the 2019 TD Ottawa Jazz Festival " alt= " Bandleader Christine Jensen leads the = Jazz Orchestra at the 2019 TD Ottawa Jazz Festival " data-portal-copyright= " Dan Nawrocki " **28;19229;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1420532 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Another Canadian group that wowed listeners was the funky , hard-hitting band of Toronto drummer Larnell Lewis . That group saw its concert delayed by four hours , until 10 p.m. last Saturday , because Lewis had a travel day from hell en route to Ottawa from Paris , where he had played the night before with Quincy Jones . While Lewis said he was jet-lagged , he played with mesmerizing power and focus during a set filled with his upbeat compositions . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; For last Saturday&amp;rsquo ; s concert of the up-and-coming American vibraphonist Sacha Berliner , the group was down a musician because its drummer was being held by immigration officers . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; This is going to be a bit of an experiment , but I think it will be fun , &amp;rdquo ; Berliner said . She , bassist Kanoa Mendenhall and pianist Chris McCarthy made music that had more of a chamber-music character . A highlight was Berliner&amp;rsquo ; s piece A Heroine&amp;rsquo ; s Manifesto , a politically charged piece that featured not only Berliner&amp;rsquo ; s highly advanced improvising but also bits of sampled speech that referred to notorious Trump-era events . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;19259;TOOLONG ... title= " Vocalist Cyrille Aimee at the 2019 TD Ottawa Jazz Festival " alt= " Vocalist Cyrille Aimee at the 2019 TD Ottawa Jazz Festival " data-portal-copyright= " Dan Nawrocki " **28;19295;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1420529 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; More genteel was the concert of French vocalist Cyrille Aim&amp;eacute ; e , who tackled the fantastic yet under-appreciated music of Stephen Sondheim. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; After an afternoon&amp;rsquo ; s rehearsal , Aim&amp;eacute ; e , New Orleans bassist Philip Kuehn and Hamilton-based pianist Adrean Farrugia found the splendour in Sondheim&amp;rsquo ; s music , and Aim&amp;eacute ; e could be engaging and funny with her patter , only to immediately make dry eyes moist with a love song such as Loving You . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Aim&amp;eacute ; e also brightened the festival&amp;rsquo ; s jam session at the Lord Elgin Hotel , as did the trio of guitarist Gilad Hekselman and the American saxophone stars Joel Frahm and Donny McCaslin. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; For more than two decades , the jazz festival&amp;rsquo ; s jam sessions have enjoyed a top-notch reputation for luring headliners to give casual bonus performances . This year was no exception , as house-band stalwarts Roddy Ellias on guitar , bassist John Geggie and drummer Greg Ritchie ensured that each night&amp;rsquo ; s jamming contained memorable and even you-had-to-be-there moments . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;19325;TOOLONG ... title= " Saxophonist Hayden Chisholm , bassist Matt Penman and drummer Jochen Rueckert at the 2019 TD Ottawa Jazz Festival " alt= " Saxophonist Hayden Chisholm , bassist Matt Penman and drummer Jochen Rueckert at the 2019 TD Ottawa Jazz Festival " data-portal-copyright= " Dan Nawrocki " **28;19361;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1420530 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; phum@postmedia.com &lt;br&gt; twitter.com/peterhum &lt;/p&gt; Peter HumMon , 01 Jul 2019 19:38:04 **45;19391;TOOLONG ... ArtsJazzfest review : = Jazz Orchestra at the TD Ottawa Jazz Festivalhttp : **32;19438;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;19472;TOOLONG ... title= " Bandleader Christine Jensen leads the = Jazz Orchestra at the 2019 TD Ottawa Jazz Festival " alt= " Bandleader Christine Jensen leads the = Jazz Orchestra at the 2019 TD Ottawa Jazz Festival " data-portal-copyright= " Dan Nawrocki " **28;19508;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1420532 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; = Jazz Orchestra &lt;br&gt; TD Ottawa Jazz Festival &lt;br&gt; NAC Studio &lt;br&gt; Reviewed Tuesday &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; More than 25 years ago , at the 1993 Ottawa International Jazz Festival , one of the bookings was the Kit McClure Big Band , a 16-piece all-female group that to some , in a male-dominated jazz world , smacked of novelty and tokenism. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Members of the band basically had to justify their existence . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; If this event provides women musicians with an opportunity to showcase their talents and get some exposure , then I say great , &amp;rdquo ; said Rosemary Galloway , the band&amp;rsquo ; s bassist at the time . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; What we&amp;rsquo ; re doing is at least making a place for women , &amp;rdquo ; McClure told this newspaper in an interview . &amp;ldquo ; Maybe someday the men will open their minds and their bands to women . That some day could be a long way away . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Is 2019 long enough ? These days , the jazz world&amp;rsquo ; s bookings and discourse are still male-dominated &amp;mdash ; but they are beginning to be cracks in the patriarchy. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; And at the forefront of gender-conscious jazz festivals is the TD Ottawa Jazz Festival in 2019 , which went so far as to strive this year for gender parity in its bookings . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Of those bookings , one of the most eagerly awaited was the concert of the = Jazz Orchestra , which itself strove for a gender-balanced lineup and celebrated the talents of female Canadian jazz musicians , some of whom were young enough to have not yet picked up their horns when McClure and her big band were on the festival&amp;rsquo ; s main stage in the early 1990s. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Tuesday night in the NAC Studio , at the helm of the = Jazz Orchestra was composer , bandleader , saxophonist and two-time Juno Award winner Christine Jensen . The 49-year-old Montrealer has been a role model for Canada&amp;rsquo ; s female jazz musicians since her 2000 debut disc Collage , and in recent years , her fame and accomplishments have only spread internationally . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Four of the band&amp;rsquo ; s five other saxophonists were women &amp;mdash ; New York-based Canadian expat Anna Webber , Montreal-based , Ottawa-raised Claire Devlin , Toronto&amp;rsquo ; s Allison Au and Tara Davidson , Montreal-based , Ottawa-raised Claire Devlin . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In the trumpet section , Toronto&amp;rsquo ; s Rebecca Hennessy and Emily Denison sat beside one another . At the piano was Montrealer Marianne Trudel. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; This is not to short-shrift the ad hoc band&amp;rsquo ; s other musicians &amp;mdash ; baritone saxophonist Richard Page , the all-male trombone section of Kelsley Grant , Mark Ferguson , William Carn and Colin Murray , trumpeters Nick Dyson and Jocelyn Couture , guitarist Steve Bilodeau , bassist Adrian Vedady and drummer Rich Irwin . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In all , the project &amp;mdash ; the result of brainstorming by Jensen and the festival&amp;rsquo ; s programming manager Petr Cancura &amp;mdash ; intended to celebrate the composing and playing of female musicians , without isolating them in an all-female context . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; That the band was also a special gathering for Canadian musicians from four cities ( New York , Montreal , Ottawa and Toronto ) not to mention a mix of players over two generations , was also very good thing . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Here&amp;rsquo ; s the concert&amp;rsquo ; s set list : &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; On the Stage in Cages ( Carla Bley ) &lt;br&gt; Codebreaking ( Davidson ) &lt;br&gt; Dans la for&amp;ecirc ; t de ma m&amp;eacute ; moire : II . Vent solaire ( Trudel ) &lt;br&gt; Swirl Around ( Jensen ) &lt;br&gt; Climbing On Mirrors ( Webber ) &lt;br&gt; Dig Up The Stories ( Hennessy ) &lt;br&gt; Dans la for&amp;ecirc ; t de ma m&amp;eacute ; moire : VI . Soon ( Trudel ) &lt;br&gt; Wink ( Jensen ) &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The concert&amp;rsquo ; s opener was a choice meant to honour trailblazing jazz composer Carla Bley . A kaleidoscope of a tune , it afforded prime solo space to Webber , a vigorously creative and expressive player , and exuberant Ottawa trombonist Ferguson but also allowed the band&amp;rsquo ; s brass players to make some interjections. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; On Davidson&amp;rsquo ; s swooping tune Codebreaking , she and trombonist William Carn &amp;mdash ; the two are a power couple on Toronto jazz&amp;rsquo ; s scene &amp;mdash ; stepped forward with personality-rich solos . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We&amp;rsquo ; re going to go deep , &amp;rdquo ; Jensen said , introducing the first of Trudel&amp;rsquo ; s compositions . Hennsessy and Webber imposed themselves dramatically on the pianist&amp;rsquo ; s imaginative feat . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; For the slow , rocking Swirl Around , Jensen picked up her soprano saxophone and commandingly led the charge , and guitarist Bilodeau rocked out . While that tune has plenty of grandeur when Jensen performs it with a small ensemble , it was room-fillingly intense brought to life by a big band . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Webber put down her horn to conduct her piece Climbing On Mirrors , an elaborate tour de force of interlocking parts . When it was her turn to solo , Au sliced through the piece&amp;rsquo ; s thrilling material . Before the piece concluded , there were fundamental shifts that induced goosebumps , but to go into more detail would spoil surprises . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Hennessy&amp;rsquo ; s Dig Up The Stories was a poignant glowing ballad . Typical of the project&amp;rsquo ; s sense of generosity , Hennessy turned over solo opportunities to Trudel and Davidson . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; For Trudel&amp;rsquo ; s songful anthemic Soon , Jensen once more picked up her soprano saxophone to shine during the piece&amp;rsquo ; s churning mid-section. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;h2 class= " cat-head short " **40;19538;TOOLONG ... &lt;ul class= " relatedlinks " &gt; &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;19580;TOOLONG ... Jensen leads the jazz festival 's signature gender-balanced big **25;19615;TOOLONG &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;19642;TOOLONG ... associate music with joy " - the Marianne Trudel **30;19677;TOOLONG &lt;/ul&gt; &lt;p&gt; To close the concert , Jensen&amp;rsquo ; s deluxe , swinging piece Wink brought forward two impressive soloists &amp;mdash ; Toronto-based trombonist Grant and Montreal-based , Ottawa-raised Devlin &amp;mdash ; who somehow had not yet been featured . When the piece ended , the standing ovation was immediate . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Pretty good for one day of rehearsals , &amp;rdquo ; Jensen allowed herself to say near the end of the night . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Pretty good was an understatement . The concert was more like a vital revelation of what Canadian jazz is capable of when some of its best exponents &amp;mdash ; female , male , of different generations and from different cities &amp;mdash ; band together . Let&amp;rsquo ; s hope that their team effort is more than a one-off . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " **25;19709;TOOLONG " **32;19736;TOOLONG &lt;br&gt; &lt;a href= " http : //twitter.com/peterhum " &gt;twitter ... &lt;/p&gt; Peter HumMon , 01 Jul 2019 16:17:52 **45;19770;TOOLONG ... ArtsConcert review : Terri Lyne Carrington and Social Science at the TD Ottawa Jazz Festivalhttp : **32;19817;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;19851;TOOLONG ... title= " Terri Lyne Carrington and Social Science at the 2019 TD Ottawa Jazz Festival " alt= " Terri Lyne Carrington and Social Science at the 2019 TD Ottawa Jazz Festival " data-portal-copyright= " Dan Nawrocki " **28;19887;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1420535 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Terri Lyne Carrington and Social Science &lt;br&gt; TD Ottawa Jazz Festival &lt;br&gt; TD Main Stage &lt;br&gt; Reviewed Thursday &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Resistance music collided with the rain Thursday night when Terri Lyne Carrington and Social Science played the TD Ottawa Jazz Festival . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The American drummer and her potent , uncompromising band played gritty , politically charged songs that combined rap , rock and jazz to combat racial injustice , intolerance and other ills . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The protest music , however , met with few ears . Thursday was the first rainy night at the jazz festival , and the sparse crowd had arrived to take in Carrington&amp;rsquo ; s band only thinned out further once the lightning flashed and the rain came down . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A potent force in jazz since her days as a teenaged prodigy in the early 1980s , Carrington , who has shared bandstands with Herbie Hancock , Wayne Shorter and Geri Allen . Most recently , she been a crusader on and off the bandstand for a more just society , with her Social Science project responding vocally and fiercely to the fraying of America in the time of Trump . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;19917;TOOLONG ... title= " Drummer Terri Lyne Carrington and Social Science at the 2019 TD Ottawa Jazz Festival " alt= " Drummer Terri Lyne Carrington and Social Science at the 2019 TD Ottawa Jazz Festival " data-portal-copyright= " Dan Nawrocki " **28;19953;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1420535 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; But in Ottawa Thursday night , the response to her music was subdued . Maybe a more woke audience on a less inclement evening would have sent the band the kind of energy that would have confirmed that its music was getting over . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I know that some of this music is a little heavy , some of the messages . But I hope it&amp;rsquo ; s OK , &amp;rdquo ; Carrington said at one point , sounding just a wee bit Canadian . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; This was a concert that began with a steely lament . &amp;ldquo ; This is for Donald , &amp;rdquo ; Debo Ray announced , before singing lyrics to a piece of elegiac but unbowed proportions that could only be aimed at her president . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; How much can we endure ? Time will tell , &amp;rdquo ; Ray sang . &amp;ldquo ; We&amp;rsquo ; ll suffer through then rise again . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;19983;TOOLONG ... title= " Vocalist Debo Ray of drummer Terri Lyne Carrington 's project Social Science at the 2019 TD Ottawa Jazz Festival " alt= " Vocalist Debo Ray of drummer Terri Lyne Carrington 's project Social Science at the 2019 TD Ottawa Jazz Festival " data-portal-copyright= " Dan Nawrocki " **28;20019;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1420534 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; This song and many that followed presented a new kind of blues for 2019 . Ray , always compelling , and Kassa Overall , the band&amp;rsquo ; s DJ and rapper , seemed to target one burning issue after another . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Bells , a slow , hypnotic and ultimately tragic piece by Carrington&amp;rsquo ; s keyboardist Aaron Parks , was the underpinning for what seemed to be Ray&amp;rsquo ; s response to the killings of black men by police . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; You took my love away from me , &amp;rdquo ; she sang . &amp;ldquo ; Tell me what give you the right to kill so senselessly ? &amp;rdquo ; If the song&amp;rsquo ; s words were open to interpretation , the closing sound of a siren helped to frame them . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Another piece allowed the band&amp;rsquo ; s voices to juxtapose two seemingly contradictory slogans &amp;mdash ; &amp;ldquo ; pray the hate away&amp;rdquo ; and &amp;ldquo ; pray the gay away . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In the middle of its set , Carrington&amp;rsquo ; s band covered two love songs . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; First came a tune that Carrington referred to as &amp;ldquo ; &lt;span style= " font-family : -apple-system , BlinkMacSystemFont , ' Segoe UI ' , Roboto , Oxygen-Sans , Ubuntu , Cantarell , ' Helvetica Neue ' , sans-serif ; " &gt;something that you might actually recognize . " Perhaps her read of the crowds was that they were jazz fans first and social justice warriors second . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; What followed was a rendition of the jazz standard What&amp;rsquo ; s New , ushered in gorgeously by Parks before it became the backdrop for Overall&amp;rsquo ; s spoken-word artistry . His commitment to his poetic rapping was clear , even if his voice could have been higher in the mix . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Then came the group&amp;rsquo ; s version of the Joni Mitchell tune Love , on which Ray rose to the challenge . Carrington said that earlier in the day , she received clearance to put the Mitchell tune on her band&amp;rsquo ; s forthcoming record , which is to be released in November . In concert , the tune allowed instrumentalists Parks and Stevens to stretch out . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Closing the concert of 70 or so minutes was a collaboration by Parks and Overall called Trapped in the American Dream , which offered a moody meditation on &amp;ldquo ; black and white&amp;rdquo ; and &amp;ldquo ; right and wrong . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Kudos to the jazz festival for programming Carrington , a booking utterly in sync with the event&amp;rsquo ; s aspirations to be more gender-balanced . It was frustrating , though , that a small and rain-battered crowd was one factor that helped rob the night of the dynamism and urgency that it deserved . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " **25;20049;TOOLONG " **32;20076;TOOLONG &lt;br&gt; &lt;a href= " http : //twitter.com/peterhum " &gt;twitter ... &lt;/p&gt; Peter HumFri , 28 Jun 2019 15:09:52 **45;20110;TOOLONG ... ArtsConcert review : Brad Mehlau Quintet at the TD Ottawa Jazz Festivalhttp : **32;20157;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;20191;TOOLONG ... title= " Pianist Brad Mehldau , plays the 2019 TD Ottawa Jazz Festival , " alt= " Pianist Brad Mehldau , plays the 2019 TD Ottawa Jazz Festival , " data-portal-copyright= " Michael Wilson " **28;20227;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1418497 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Brad Mehldau Quintet &lt;br&gt; TD Ottawa Jazz Festival &lt;br&gt; TD Main Stage &lt;br&gt; Reviewed Wednesday &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In Marion Dewar Plaza on Wednesday night , pianist Brad Mehldau and his bandmates returned to their roots but didn&amp;rsquo ; t relive the past . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The revered 48-year-old American pianist , a leading jazz light of his generation for half of his life , was on the main stage with old and new friends , playing the kind of hard bop tunes that they had in all likelihood cut their teeth on as teenagers . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But now , they approached the classic material not just to render it correctly as per the style of yesteryear&amp;rsquo ; s jazz and as other musicians might rigorously take as their mandate . Instead , they tackled the music with all the contemporary liberties and effusive personality of world-class musicians with nothing to prove anymore . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Mehldau , tenor saxophonist Joel Frahm , trumpeter Ambrose Akinmusire , bassist Joe Sanders and drummer Leon Parker also provided a welcome jazz incursion on the TD Main Stage , making &lt;span style= " font-family : -apple-system , BlinkMacSystemFont , ' Segoe UI ' , Roboto , Oxygen-Sans , Ubuntu , Cantarell , ' Helvetica Neue ' , sans-serif ; " &gt; the kind of acoustic instrumental music that once prevailed during the festival 's long-running Concerts Under the Stars series , but which has since been displaced by more mass-appeal sounds . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Mehldau and his new group , which was playing its eighth gig after forming earlier this month , drew a receptive , beyond-mid-sized crowd to the plaza . Sure , the audience was smaller than the plaza-filling multitude that took in Chicago and Norah Jones earlier during the festival . But Mehldau&amp;rsquo ; s quintet delivered musical delights that the others did not , including warm , glowing swinging and the overarching feeling that anything could happen . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; One thing is certain . Mehldau and his listeners were united by relief that the bad weather forecast for the evening ( severe thunderstorms with the possibility of a tornado ) never materialized . Instead , the night was perfect for enjoying jazz of impeccable lineage and abundant creativity . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The quintet&amp;rsquo ; s lengthy set consisted of : &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Blues in C ( Brad Mehldau ) &lt;br&gt; Besame Mucho ( Consuelo Vel&amp;aacute ; zquez ) &lt;br&gt; Chain Reaction ( Hank Mobley ) &lt;br&gt; Pannonica ( Thelonious Monk ) &lt;br&gt; Straight Ahead ( Kenny Dorham ) &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Encore : &lt;br&gt; Straight Street ( John Coltrane ) &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The opener set the tone for the night as the band delved into the most time-honoured tune form in jazz but found a world of possibilities in its 12 bars . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The music was roomy , patient and malleable , rooted in the jazz language but open to all kinds of spontaneous , organic feints and departures . It didn&amp;rsquo ; t take long for new harmonies , bass notes and grooves to shunt aside the usual ones . Mehldau , Akinmusire and Frahm uncorked solos that were grounded in the chosen material but wended far afield . The rhythm section&amp;rsquo ; s backdrop behind soloists morphed thanks to in-the-moment decisions , not the least of which were choices by Mehldau , Sanders or Parker not to play . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Throughout the night , highlights abounded . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; There was the way in which Mehldau and Frahm , whose musical history dates back to their high school days in Connecticut , played the blues while not playing the blues on the pianist&amp;rsquo ; s Ornette Coleman-ish blues . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; There was Akinmusire&amp;rsquo ; s hyper-motific solo on Besame Mucho , which Mehldau had recast as a bright , lilting waltz &amp;mdash ; &amp;ldquo ; a beautiful romantic song , &amp;rdquo ; he later announced &amp;mdash ; for which he rendered the melody . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Also on Besame Mucho , there was Mehldau&amp;rsquo ; s extended , swaggering cruise on the tune&amp;rsquo ; s outro , which was a charged and juicy rejoinder to anyone who thought he had been holding back in this quintet format . ( He was the uncontested star when his trio played the jazz festival&amp;rsquo ; s main stage twice in the 2000s. ) &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; There was Frahm&amp;rsquo ; s fondness for quotes , which on the Mobley-penned Minor Blues included permutations of Surrey with the Fringe on Top , Invitation and Witch Hunt &amp;mdash ; but who&amp;rsquo ; s counting ? Of course , apart from its puckish quoting , Frahm&amp;rsquo ; s playing bubbled over with ear-catching inventiveness and robust , positive feeling . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; There was Parker&amp;rsquo ; s&amp;nbsp ; body percussion / human beat box tour de force on Straight Ahead , the rhythm changes-based tune that in the hands of Mehldau&amp;rsquo ; s band was not so straight ahead , yet straight ahead . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; On any given tune , there was Parker&amp;rsquo ; s stripped down but incredibly propulsive and centred playing . A few seconds of his buoyant ride cymbal playing had as much impact as a fusillade of notes from many other drummers . It wouldn&amp;rsquo ; t be surprising if one big reason for Mehldau&amp;rsquo ; s band playing this month was the pianist&amp;rsquo ; s wish to reunite with the one-of-a-kind drummer with whom , if I&amp;rsquo ; m not mistaken , he&amp;rsquo ; d last made music with in the 1990s. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Come to think of it , there was also Parker&amp;rsquo ; s transfixing introduction from behind his kit to Straight Ahead , which made it feel for a moment like all was right in the world . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Mehldau and his peers are often involved in more high-stakes music , playing more illustrious halls and making records for posterity that jazz tastemakers evaluate as state of the art . The pianist&amp;rsquo ; s latest projects have been staggeringly ambitious , adjoining his own improvising with the music of Bach or crafting unique , hard-hitting electronic protest music to bear witness in the time of Trump . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;h2 class= " cat-head short " **40;20257;TOOLONG ... &lt;ul class= " relatedlinks " &gt; &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;20299;TOOLONG ... preview : Eclectic Brad Mehldau takes inspiration from hard bop , Bible , Trump era&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/li&gt; &lt;/ul&gt; &lt;p&gt; Wednesday night&amp;rsquo ; s concert was more low-key and admirably loose than all of that , about playing jazz because sometimes jazz is all you need . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Mehldau quintet has one more concert on its itinerary , at the Montreal International Jazz Festival tonight . The smart money is on it being super-heavy without trying to be heavy . But what will happen after ? &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; This will really be a try-and-see venture , &amp;rdquo ; Mehldau told this newspaper before his band played its mid-June week at the Village Vanguard in New York . &amp;ldquo ; If it works out and everybody enjoys it , I imagine we&amp;lsquo ; d like to continue with more gigs. &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; We can only hope that Mehldau&amp;rsquo ; s quintet doesn&amp;rsquo ; t wink out of existence after tonight . But if that&amp;rsquo ; s the case , it will have left beautiful memories . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " **25;20334;TOOLONG " **32;20361;TOOLONG &lt;br&gt; &lt;a href= " http : //twitter.com/peterhum " &gt;twitter ... &lt;br&gt; &lt;a href= " http : **28;20395;TOOLONG " &gt;o ... &lt;/p&gt; Peter HumThu , 27 Jun 2019 15:33:15 **45;20425;TOOLONG ... ArtsJazzfest preview : Eclectic Brad Mehldau takes inspiration from hard bop , Bible , Trump erahttp : **37;20472;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;20511;TOOLONG ... title= " Pianist Brad Mehldau , who plays the 2019 TD Ottawa Jazz Festival " alt= " Pianist Brad Mehldau , who plays the 2019 TD Ottawa Jazz Festival " data-portal-copyright= " " **28;20547;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1418499 " /&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 100% " height= " 100% " frameborder= " 0 " allowfullscreen= " true " src= " https : **34;20577;TOOLONG ... &gt; &lt;/iframe&gt; &lt;p&gt; Pianist Brad Mehldau is a do-just-about-anything musician who can apply his brilliance equally to swinging , bop-based jazz , Bach-inspired improvising or trippy , hard-driving , electronic music with a distinctly socio-political thrust . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 480 " src= " https : **34;20613;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;20649;TOOLONG &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **34;20685;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;20721;TOOLONG &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **34;20757;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;20793;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; Given his eclectic abilities , the news tidbit this spring that Mehldau , 48 , would debut a new , star-studded quintet in June at New York&amp;rsquo ; s legendary Village Vanguard jazz club , and soon after play selected Canadian jazz festivals with the group , prompted curiosity and speculation , above all about what kind of music he would play . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I am as curious as you are , &amp;rdquo ; said Petr Cancura , programming manager of TD Ottawa Jazz Festival , where Mehldau&amp;rsquo ; s quintet will play Wednesday night . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; It got confirmed pretty late that it ( the booking ) was in fact going to be a quintet , &amp;rdquo ; Cancura said . &amp;ldquo ; For the longest time it was going to be his usual trio . &amp;rdquo ; Mehldau played the Ottawa festival with his trio in 2002 , 2006 and 2008 , and then returned most recently to Ottawa with saxophonist Joshua Redman in 2011. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Below , Mehldau sheds light on what his new band will play in Marion Dewar Plaza , and talks about his just-released album , Finding Gabriel &amp;mdash ; an entirely different project that converts Bible-inspired notions into unique , modern protest music that embraces contemporary grooves , synth-y textures and choral singing alike . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Q : Why have you formed this new band ? &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A : I&amp;lsquo ; ve admired all these players for a number of years . ( Saxophonist ) Joel ( Frahm ) is the one I&amp;lsquo ; ve known longest , since we were 15 years old and played together in high school . ( Drummer ) Leon ( Parker ) and I played together a lot in a trio in 1990 and &amp;rsquo ; 91 , during my first years in New York City . ( Trumpeter ) Ambrose ( Akinmusire ) and ( bassist ) Joe ( Sanders ) I&amp;rsquo ; ve followed for a few years , Joe particularly in his work with pianist Gerald Clayton . Ambrose I&amp;rsquo ; ve gotten to record with twice , with ( guitarist ) Wolfgang Muthspiel , and the experience was fantastic . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;nbsp ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **33;20829;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;20864;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Q : What material will this group play ? &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A : My idea with this band is to begin more simply , and not try to reinvent the wheel in terms of composition . In that way we can have a common starting point as a band . We&amp;lsquo ; ll play some of my tunes and some standards and some material from the hard-bop era &amp;mdash ; a few early Coltrane tunes , Clifford Brown , Oliver Nelson . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Q : How will you ready the band before its first gig ? &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A : I&amp;lsquo ; ve made some arrangements and we will have one long rehearsal , and then bam &amp;mdash ; showtime. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Q : After your Canadian festival concerts , what hopes do you have for this group ? &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A : This will really be a try-and-see venture . If it works out and everybody enjoys it , I imagine we&amp;lsquo ; d like to continue with more gigs. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Q : Finding Gabriel draws upon your close reading of the Bible in recent years . Prior to that , how significant was the Bible in your life ? How religious are you ? &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A : I believe in God but do not identify with any of the monotheistic religions specifically . I&amp;lsquo ; ve also found a lot of solace and guidance from Buddhist and Vedanta teachings . The Bible is more recent for me in terms of close reading and reflection . I had never read the prophetic books in the Old Testament until two years ago and was drawn into them , along with the Gospels and the Pauline letters of the New Testament . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Q : What is it about recent times that prompted you to delve into the Bible ? &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A : It is a moral guide for modern society whether one is a believer , atheist , conservative , or liberal . It is striking to see the parallels , the same questions being asked , the same answers being given , the same mistakes being made . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Q : Were you concerned about releasing music that in some cases is so overtly political , and even anti-Trump ? &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A : The scripture from Hosea that frames the anti-Trump song , The Prophet is A Fool , is meant to invoke the phenomenon of fake news : Hosea means that even if a prophet would be among us , we would call him a fool . In just over three years , our trust has begun to evaporate in institutions that are fundamental to democracy and free speech . Trump has said that the press is an &amp;ldquo ; enemy of the people , &amp;ldquo ; in the language of fascism . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **34;20900;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;20936;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; I was concerned about a Trump supporter taking issue with that track . The important thing is , I&amp;lsquo ; m not coming down on Trump supporters . The music bears witness . I&amp;lsquo ; m bearing witness to xenophobia , racism and violence and how they have manifested in my society , a society I hold dear . I&amp;lsquo ; m inspired by the prophets , who were not afraid to judge Israel . I invite the listener to bear witness as well . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Q : Do you have an ideal listener in mind for this music ? &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A : My ideal listener , I suppose , would be one who acknowledges the danger of Trump&amp;rsquo ; s demagoguery. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Q : Is it fair to consider the album as protest music , in the lineage of jazz albums such as Max Roach 's We Insist ! , Charles Mingus 's Fables of Faubus and Charlie Haden 's Not In Our Name , to name a few ? &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A : I&amp;lsquo ; d be proud to be included with Mingus anywhere , and the others ! The only thing I would qualify that with is that Finding Gabriel is also about a personal search , very much on a subjective level , that culminates on the final title track . So it&amp;lsquo ; s trying to bridge the personal with the political , and one person finding his way in all of that . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; One of my favourite rock &amp;lsquo ; n&amp;lsquo ; roll moments is Gim me Shelter from The Rolling Stones . It&amp;lsquo ; s a protest song &amp;mdash ; maybe the only one they ever wrote . But it goes back to the first-person subject , who says &amp;ldquo ; Give me shelter&amp;rdquo ; from all of the turmoil . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Brad Mehldau Quintet&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;br&gt; TD Ottawa Jazz Festival &lt;br&gt; When : Wednesday , June 26 , 8:30 p.m. &lt;br&gt; Where : TD Main Stage , Marion Dewar Plaza &lt;br&gt; Tickets : &lt;a href= " http : //ottawajazzfestival.com " &gt;ottaw ... &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " **25;20972;TOOLONG " **32;20999;TOOLONG &lt;br&gt; &lt;a href= " http : //twitter.com/peterhum " &gt;twitter ... &lt;/p&gt; &lt;hr&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;MORE ON JAZZFEST : &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;21033;TOOLONG ... preview : Ottawa saxophonist Alison Young balances jazz exploits with rocking out in Corey Hart 's band&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;21068;TOOLONG ... preview : Christine Jensen leads the festival 's signature gender-balanced big band Tuesday&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;21103;TOOLONG ... review : Chicago comes to Ottawa with new singer , and he 's Canadian&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; Peter HumTue , 25 Jun 2019 20:13:54 **45;21138;TOOLONG ... ArtsJazzfest preview : Ottawa saxophonist Alison Young balances jazz exploits with rocking out in Corey Hart 's bandhttp : **36;21185;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;21223;TOOLONG ... title= " Ottawa-raised , Toronto-based saxophonist Alison Young , a Juno Award nominee and member of Corey Hart 's band , plays the TD Ottawa Jazz Festival on Thursday June 27 , 2019. " alt= " Ottawa-raised , Toronto-based saxophonist Alison Young , a Juno Award nominee and member of Corey Hart 's band , plays the TD Ottawa Jazz Festival on Thursday June 27 , 2019. " data-portal-copyright= " Provided by Alison Young " **28;21259;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1418480 " /&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 100% " height= " 100% " frameborder= " 0 " allowfullscreen= " true " src= " https : **34;21289;TOOLONG ... &gt; &lt;/iframe&gt; &lt;p&gt; It&amp;rsquo ; s been a banner year so far for saxophonist Alison Young , who scored a Juno nomination for her debut album , So Here We Are , and began rocking out on tour with Corey Hart . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The achievements are the latest milestones in a musical journey that includes growing up in the Glebe , playing in the Nepean All-City Jazz Band , and relocating to Toronto , where Young , 37 , studied music and has been gigging in the musical trenches since her early 20s. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In advance of her TD Ottawa Jazz Festival concert Thursday , Young discusses the steps along her journey . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Q : What were your first big musical experiences ? &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A : I started on piano around age six . I particularly loved Beethoven , and would wildly overdramatize his pieces , sacrificing technical perfection for passion . ( This habit may have stayed with me. ) &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; When I was 12 , I heard big band music for the first time , courtesy of Hopewell&amp;rsquo ; s stage band , led by Allison Woyiwada . I instantly fell in love and begged my parents to rent me a saxophone . I discovered my parents&amp;rsquo ; Count Basie and Duke Ellington records and played along with them obsessively . At the time I never imagined I&amp;rsquo ; d become a musician , but I was fascinated by the sound of the sax section and the soloists , and needed to know how to make that sound and channel that feeling . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; By the time I finished high school , I was gigging a little bit around Ottawa and playing in the Nepean All-City Jazz Band , and loving every minute of it . I couldn&amp;rsquo ; t think of anything else I wanted to do . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Q : Where did you study music and what did you get out of it ? &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A : &amp;nbsp ; I was ecstatic when I was accepted into the University of Toronto&amp;rsquo ; s jazz program , and then had a big wakeup call . Up until then I was mostly self-taught on the saxophone , and had a lot of catching up to do with regard to technique and theory . The faculty was stacked with great musicians , and Mike Murley and Kirk MacDonald were fantastic private teachers . The only thing I regret about university is that I was too terrified to get out and play more with my fellow students . I spent a lot of time in the practice rooms . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Q : How do you split your musical time and energies these days ? &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A : I do it all ! Jazz , blues , pop , R&amp;amp ; B , session work , commercials , you name it . I&amp;rsquo ; m fortunate in that I love many genres of music , and enjoy the challenge of fitting in to whatever I&amp;rsquo ; m called to play . To make a living as a gigging musician you need to stay busy , and it helps to be versatile . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The majority of my work is still as a sideman . I play regularly with a diverse bunch of projects&amp;nbsp ; ( Big Rude Jake , Heillig Manoeuvre , Alysha Brilla , Rita Chiarelli , Red Hot Ramble , Joe Sealy , Suzie McNeil , and Chelsea McBride&amp;rsquo ; s Socialist Night School among others ) and I love it . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **34;21325;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;21361;TOOLONG &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **34;21397;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;21433;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; &amp;nbsp ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; I&amp;rsquo ; m enjoying developing my voice as a leader , however , and devoting more time to composing and booking gigs of my own . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Q : What prompted you to put out your first album as a leader ? &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; I had been gigging here and there as a leader , had found a great team of musicians ( shoutout to Jeff McLeod , Eric St-Laurent , Ross MacIntyre , Sly Juhas and Chris Wallace ! ) , and had written some compositions I liked , but never felt ready to put out an album . Now I know that feeling probably never goes away . The push came when I was awarded a free day of recording at Toronto&amp;rsquo ; s Canterbury Studios and had to get the band in the studio before the end of the year . I&amp;rsquo ; m so grateful for that opportunity and deadline . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Q : Your album 's an eclectic one . Why ? &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A : Going into the studio , I wasn&amp;rsquo ; t sure whether I wanted to lean more on my jazz influences or make a funkier , bluesier sounding record . We ended up doing both , and the band swings so hard . I&amp;rsquo ; m proud of how it turned out . My priority is always to tell a story , to find the joy and soul of the song , and communicate that to the listener . I hope we achieved that with this album . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Q : What did that mean for you to have your album nominated for a Juno ? &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A : I half-jokingly said &amp;ldquo ; It&amp;rsquo ; s a confidence boost ! &amp;rdquo ; to an interviewer at the Juno Nominee announcement party , but it really has been that . It&amp;rsquo ; s an incredible honour to get recognized on a national level , especially since it&amp;rsquo ; s my first album . It means a lot to me ! I&amp;rsquo ; m hoping it will lead to more festival bookings for my band ( we&amp;rsquo ; re doing several this summer , which is exciting ) and ideally a Canadian tour in the near future . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **34;21469;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;21505;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Q : How did you come to tour with Corey Hart ? &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A : I got a phone call out of the blue last year from producer Bob Ezrin , asking if I wanted to record on Corey Hart&amp;rsquo ; s new EP . Uh , yes ! I was very young when he first rose to fame , but I remember loving Sunglasses at Night as a kid in the &amp;rsquo ; 80s , and having a cassette of The Singles . I went in to record a sax solo on the EP&amp;rsquo ; s title track , Dreaming Time Again , and to my surprise Corey was at the session , and asked me if I&amp;rsquo ; d be interested in coming on tour . Again , yes ! &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Q : How would you compare playing with Hart to your jazz exploits ? &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-family : -apple-system , BlinkMacSystemFont , ' Segoe UI ' , Roboto , Oxygen-Sans , Ubuntu , Cantarell , ' Helvetica Neue ' , sans-serif ; " &gt;A : It 's a very different experience . It 's an amazing rush , playing to a stadium full of fans who know and love the songs . I 'm so used to the comparative looseness of a jazz setting , where changing directions on a whim is par for the course , and if you lose the thread , you create a new one . This is a different challenge ! You need to play with all the rock ' n ' roll energy and intent , but also nail the iconic solos . I 'm loving this tour . Corey and the band and the crew are fantastic to work with . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Q : The Ottawa Jazz Festival is striving this year to present a lineup with gender parity . What do you think of this goal ? &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A : I think it&amp;rsquo ; s a fabulous idea to consciously present a more gender-balanced lineup in the festival . There are so many deserving artists , both established and up-and-coming , and presenting more women artists can only help inspire the next generation of young female musicians to create music and feel like they belong . I remember seeing Christine Jensen play at the Ottawa festival as a teenager and being inspired by her . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; We are still underrepresented in jazz , but lately I see more young women players coming on the scene and killing it , and I like where this is headed . Hopefully in the future it won&amp;rsquo ; t have to be a conscious decision to have a more equal balance of males and females on the festival stage , but this is a great step . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Alison Young&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;br&gt; TD Ottawa Jazz Festival &lt;br&gt; When : Thursday , June 27 , 6:30 p.m. &lt;br&gt; Where : TD Main Stage , Marion Dewar Plaza &lt;br&gt; Tickets : &lt;a href= " http : //ottawajazfestival.com " &gt;ottawa ... &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " **25;21541;TOOLONG " **32;21568;TOOLONG &lt;br&gt; &lt;a href= " http : //twitter.com/peterhum " &gt;twitter ... &lt;/p&gt; &lt;hr&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;MORE JAZZFEST : &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;21602;TOOLONG ... preview : Christine Jensen leads the festival 's signature gender-balanced big band Tuesday&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;21637;TOOLONG ... review : Chicago comes to Ottawa with new singer , and he 's Canadian&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;21672;TOOLONG ... review : New Orleans singer Erica Falls elevates Galactic fusion&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;21707;TOOLONG ... review : Judith Hill and her funky family open it on a positive note&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; Peter HumTue , 25 Jun 2019 17:09:05 **45;21742;TOOLONG ... ArtsJazzfest preview : Christine Jensen leads the festival 's signature , gender-balanced big band Tuesdayhttp : **33;21789;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;21824;TOOLONG ... title= " Montreal saxophonist/composer and bandleader Christine Jensen is in her creative prime . " alt= " Montreal saxophonist/composer and bandleader Christine Jensen is in her creative prime . " data-portal-copyright= " Randy Cole " **28;21860;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1149091 " /&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 100% " height= " 100% " frameborder= " 0 " allowfullscreen= " true " src= " https : **34;21890;TOOLONG ... &gt; &lt;/iframe&gt; &lt;p&gt; When the distinctively named = Jazz Orchestra makes its debut Tuesday at the TD Ottawa Jazz Festival , it could not have a better woman at the helm than Christine Jensen . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Montreal-based saxophonist and bandleader , 49 , has led her own jazz orchestra for more than a decade , winning a Juno Award for each of its two recordings . She and her sister , the world-class New York-based trumpeter Ingrid Jensen , have been role models for women in jazz in Canada and beyond since they made their first recordings in the 1990s. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In the NAC Studio on Tuesday night , the = Jazz Orchestra , a gender-balanced big band uniting musicians from Montreal , Ottawa and Toronto , will celebrate a subset of Canada&amp;rsquo ; s jazz sisterhood . In the band with Jensen are pianist Marianne Trudel , a fellow Montrealer , plus the Toronto-based horn players Allison Au , Tara Davidson and Rebecca Hennessy , and the New York City-based Canadian expat Anna Webber . The project is fully in sync with the jazz festival&amp;rsquo ; s goal of achieving gender parity in its bookings this year . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Below , Jensen discusses the special project , which she hopes will be more than a one-off . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Q : How did this new band come to be ? &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A : It was a bit of a brainstorm that I was having with Petr Cancura at the Ottawa Jazz Festival , about presenting more of a balance in big bands , while looking at the future of where the music is going . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; At the same time , Petr and Catherine ( O&amp;rsquo ; Grady , the jazz festival&amp;rsquo ; s executive producer ) are really working on a balanced festival program this year with gender balance . I was at the Monterey Jazz Festival last year , and it was the most amazing balance going on there , with the greatest vibe among musicians and audience . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; We started planning this winter , after I got really inspired after putting a really strong orchestra at the Oscar Peterson Jazz Festival . Its artistic director , the amazing Renee Rosnes , requested that I perform my music with a Toronto-based horn section . I was able to get four women in the horn section , plus Ingrid as soloist . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; This time , I am able to get six very strong female voices out of the 13 horns , plus the amazing wizard of spontaneity Marianne Trudel , who is adding her music with some excerpts from the suite that she wrote a few years back for the orchestra national jazz de Montreal . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Somehow the overall repertoire of this concert is , to me , &amp;ldquo ; the voice of the now , &amp;rdquo ; with compositions from Marianne , myself , Rebecca , Tara and Anna . Oh &amp;mdash ; we are adding Carla Bley&amp;rsquo ; s music , because her music is not performed enough outside of her own band . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Q : Tell me about the connections you have or are about to have with some of the orchestra 's other women . &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A : I love Marianne&amp;rsquo ; s energy . We float through different combinations of ensembles in Montreal and always have a lot of fun performing together . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Anna was once in a McGill ensemble that I directed . I also love her energy and her quest , new music meeting improvisation at an extremely high level . Allison and Tara are gems , with equally strong composition and improv skills and great sound . They worked with me on my concert at the Oscar Peterson Jazz Festival with Renee , along with Rebecca , who is also going down a really creative path . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Q : Do you hope you can get other bookings for this project down the road ? &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A : It would be amazing to keep working on this concept of presenting living composers , with an even more even voice in composition and performance . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Q : Overall , what progress is being made in jazz in terms of combating discrimination against women and advancing gender issues ? &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A : There&amp;rsquo ; s lots of pluses going on right now . These are changing times . Women still have a ways to go in breaking the glass ceiling on the main stage , and in the more elite jazz institutions . But there is a lot of work getting done with everyone becoming more conscious and finding solutions . There are more opportunities for young women to explore in a safe space with educational opportunities and jam spaces . Everyone , especially the younger generation of musicians , is so more aware . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;= Jazz Orchestra&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;br&gt; TD Ottawa Jazz Festival &lt;br&gt; **33;21926;TOOLONG : Tuesday , June 25 , 7 p.m. &lt;br&gt; **34;21961;TOOLONG : NAC Studio &lt;br&gt; **36;21997;TOOLONG : ottawajazzfestival.com &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " **25;22035;TOOLONG " **32;22062;TOOLONG &lt;br&gt; twitter.com/peterhum &lt;/p&gt; &lt;hr&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;ALSO IN THE NEWS : &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;22096;TOOLONG ... review : Chicago comes to Ottawa with new singer , and he 's Canadian&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;22131;TOOLONG ... review : New Orleans singer Erica Falls elevates Galactic fusion&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;22166;TOOLONG ... ArtsJazzfest preview : Norah Jones embraces spontaneity and strives to keep her songs alivehttp : **35;22248;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;22285;TOOLONG ... title= " LOS ANGELES , CALIFORNIA - NOVEMBER 07 : Norah Jones performs onstage at JONI 75 : A Birthday Celebration Live at Dorothy Chandler Pavilion on November 07 , 2018 in Los Angeles , California . " alt= " LOS ANGELES , CALIFORNIA - NOVEMBER 07 : Norah Jones performs onstage at JONI 75 : A Birthday Celebration Live at Dorothy Chandler Pavilion on November 07 , 2018 in Los Angeles , California . " data-portal-copyright= " Rodin Eckenroth " **28;22321;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1415893 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; With almost two months of touring North America ahead of her , Norah Jones made a small confession . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I really don&amp;rsquo ; t like rehearsing . I&amp;rsquo ; m not good at rehearsing &amp;hellip ; There&amp;rsquo ; s not a ton of rehearsing , &amp;rdquo ; the singer-songwriter said in a recent interview . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Don&amp;rsquo ; t worry &amp;mdash ; we&amp;rsquo ; ll be prepared , &amp;rdquo ; she continued with a laugh . &amp;ldquo ; As long as we all know the material , then the magic will happen . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; That tour , which began last week in Pittsburgh , Pa. and will end in early August in Anchorage , Alaska , touches down in Ottawa on Tuesday . That&amp;rsquo ; s when Jones , 40 , plays the TD Ottawa Jazz Festival&amp;rsquo ; s main stage in Marion Dewar Plaza . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; What does Jones have against rehearsing ? &amp;ldquo ; I like spontaneity and I always have , &amp;rdquo ; she said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;h2 class= " cat-head short " **40;22351;TOOLONG ... &lt;ul class= " relatedlinks " &gt; &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;22393;TOOLONG ... Norah Jones 's mellow show both a delight and challenge for Jazzfest **29;22428;TOOLONG &lt;/ul&gt; &lt;p&gt; When Jones released her easy-on-the-ears debut Come Away With Me in 2002 , it straddled genres like few recordings before it . Part pop , part folk and part jazz , the album won five Grammy Awards and has sold more than 25 million copies worldwide thanks to its fetching hit Don&amp;rsquo ; t Know Why&amp;nbsp ; and Jones&amp;rsquo ; s honeyed drawl. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But Jones&amp;rsquo ; s roots are in jazz . She studied jazz piano at the University of North Texas , and has brought world-class jazz musicians such as guitarist Bill Frisell and drummer Brian Blade into her musical world . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Jones attributed her love of spontaneity to the jazz in her musical DNA . &amp;ldquo ; I think that&amp;rsquo ; s why I&amp;rsquo ; m like that , &amp;rdquo ; she said . &amp;ldquo ; That&amp;rsquo ; s how I came up , that&amp;rsquo ; s the music I played and learned . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Even though the music I make now I would not necessarily classify it as jazz , that&amp;rsquo ; s definitely the spirit in which I like to approach things . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Jones is touring in support of her most recent release Begin Again , which is not so much an album but rather an eclectic collection of seven singles , most of which were released individually every few months over the last year or so . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; They&amp;rsquo ; re pretty separate , &amp;rdquo ; Jones said . &amp;ldquo ; They&amp;rsquo ; re not really meant to be all together .
