We just sort of scooped them up and put the first seven out . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; One reason for the string of singles was &amp;ldquo ; the way the music industry is &amp;hellip ; it definitely sort of lends itself to that , &amp;rdquo ; Jones said , likely referring to the rise of streaming services and the decline of album and music sales in general . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But she said that more importantly , she wanted to avoid the massive , two-year enterprise involved in making an album , releasing it , and touring . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Mostly it was just to stay inspired and follow my whim of what I wanted to try , without a huge cloud of a project that&amp;rsquo ; s more work , &amp;rdquo ; Jones said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Begin Again , which was released in April , pulls together songs from several recording sessions and a range of collaborators including Wilco singer-guitarist Jeff Tweedy , singer-pianist Thomas Bartlett and drummer Blade and bassist Chris Thomas . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The goal at each session , Jones said , was to get at least one good song . But the sessions were more productive than that , yielding other songs still in the can , Jones said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; It&amp;rsquo ; s easy to collaborate with people when it&amp;rsquo ; s just one or two days in the studio . You throw stuff at the wall and see what sticks . It&amp;rsquo ; s not like a huge big deal . It&amp;rsquo ; s a nice way for me to reach out to new people and collaborate and do a lot of different things without it becoming complicated . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Jones said that the single A Song With No Name appeared practically out of thin air when she got together with Tweedy. &amp;ldquo ; That was purely us sitting down with two guitars . I started playing the riff , and he started playing against the riff . I sort of improvised singing over it and that&amp;rsquo ; s what it was . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Because we were all together , there was no overdubbing possible . Whatever came out of my mouth , the melody and the lyrics at the same time , was kind of it , &amp;rdquo ; Jones said . &amp;ldquo ; Sometimes you just start something &amp;hellip ; and it works &amp;hellip ; it just kind of magically works , you know ? &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; On the other hand , the songs with Bartlett , My Heart Is Full and Uh Oh , were built layer upon layer because Bartlett is all about overdubbing. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; That&amp;rsquo ; s how he works , &amp;rdquo ; Jones said . &amp;ldquo ; He has this little room with all these crazy instruments . The original track of My Heart Is Full , I had lyrics already and I brought them in , but I didn&amp;rsquo ; t have any music or melody . He just started playing the organ and I improvised a melody with the lyrics and that&amp;rsquo ; s kind of how it happened . He just followed me. &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **34;22459;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;22495;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; With jazz musicians Blade and Thomas , Jones created Begin Again&amp;rsquo ; s earthy title track , using , for the most part , lyrics by Emily Fiskio . But Jones penned a final verse to the song that seems to take a relationship song in a new direction . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **34;22531;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;22567;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Can a nation built on blood find its way out of the mud ? Will the people at the top lose their way enough to stop ? Can we begin again ? &amp;rdquo ; was Jones&amp;rsquo ; s lyrical contribution . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; What&amp;rsquo ; s that verse about ? &amp;ldquo ; What do you think ? &amp;rdquo ; Jones responded . &amp;ldquo ; I&amp;rsquo ; m not one to explain my lyrics . I think it&amp;rsquo ; s pretty obvious , and I think it also could be open to interpretation , depending on where you live . We got a lot of problems all over the world . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Jones admitted that she has written political songs over the years . &amp;ldquo ; I&amp;rsquo ; ve written too many election songs , &amp;rdquo ; she said . &amp;ldquo ; You just kind of write what you know . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Jones said that she has been playing more piano during performances and featuring herself in the context of a smaller band . &amp;ldquo ; I like stripping it back right now . It&amp;rsquo ; s been really nice . It makes for a lot of spontaneity , &amp;rdquo ; she says . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Asked whether songs have changed in performance over the years , Jones said that &amp;ldquo ; sometimes they morph into something very different &amp;hellip ; There are old songs of mine that sometimes I don&amp;rsquo ; t even realize I&amp;rsquo ; ve changed up more than I think . It&amp;rsquo ; s just what happens over the years . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But she added that it&amp;rsquo ; s not been her intention to change a song for the sake of change . &amp;ldquo ; The intent is only just to keep the songs alive , &amp;rdquo ; she said . &amp;ldquo ; That&amp;rsquo ; s my favourite thing about touring . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Norah Jones&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;br&gt; &lt;strong&gt;TD Ottawa Jazz Festival&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;br&gt; &lt;strong&gt;When : &lt;/strong&gt; Friday , June 25 , 8:30 p.m. &lt;br&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Where : &lt;/strong&gt; TD main stage , Marion Dewar Plaza &lt;br&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Tickets : &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;a href= " http : //ottawajazzfestival.com " &gt;ottaw ... &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " **25;22603;TOOLONG " **32;22630;TOOLONG &lt;br&gt; &lt;a href= " http : //twitter.com/peterhum " &gt;twitter ... &lt;/p&gt; Peter HumSat , 22 Jun 2019 11:00:45 **45;22664;TOOLONG ... ArtsJazzfest preview : Questing pianist Kris Davis says her art is " all over the place ... but still just me " http : **37;22711;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;22750;TOOLONG ... title= " Pianist Kris Davis , who plays the 2019 TD Ottawa Jazz Festival " alt= " Pianist Kris Davis , who plays the 2019 TD Ottawa Jazz Festival " data-portal-copyright= " Caroline Mardok " **28;22786;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1416639 " /&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 100% " height= " 100% " frameborder= " 0 " allowfullscreen= " true " src= " https : **34;22816;TOOLONG ... &gt; &lt;/iframe&gt; &lt;p&gt; When Kris Davis settled in behind the piano for her concert at the 2008 Earshot Festival in Seattle , she was troubled by one question : What would her mother , who was in the audience , think of the extreme music that her group , Paradoxical Frog , was about to play ? &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; My mom&amp;rsquo ; s not a jazz fan really , but she likes to check out the music . I thought , &amp;lsquo ; Oh my gosh , she&amp;rsquo ; s really going to hate this band&amp;rsquo ; , &amp;rdquo ; Davis remembers . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Perhaps the most extreme piece that night was drummer Tyshawn Sorey&amp;rsquo ; s composition Homograph , which basically explores at length how the band can play a stark , repetitive set of just a few notes , each separated by a stretch of silence . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We played it for 30 minutes &amp;hellip ; getting super-deep into that experience and feeling like the silence was louder than the actual notes and challenging the audience to feel uncomfortable but also be drawn into the extreme experience of it , &amp;rdquo ; Davis recalls . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; What did Davis&amp;rsquo ; s mother think of the concert ? &amp;ldquo ; She thought this was the best thing she ever heard , &amp;rdquo ; Davis says . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; For the 39-year-old Calgary-born musician , her mother&amp;rsquo ; s openness to the unique music passed on a lesson . &amp;ldquo ; I don&amp;rsquo ; t want to judge or try to think that I know what the audience wants or needs , &amp;rdquo ; she says . &amp;ldquo ; It&amp;rsquo ; s rewarding for us and it seems to draw people me in. &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Davis , a cutting-edge jazz pianist and composer who plays in Ottawa Sunday with her Paradoxical Frog bandmate saxophonist Ingrid Laubrock , calls that Seattle concert a turning point . But for Davis , theres has been no shortage of milestones in an artistic career that keeps ascending and evolving . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; There&amp;rsquo ; s been a lot of phases , &amp;rdquo ; says Davis , who has been based in or close to New York City since the early 2000s. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; She was blessed to have known early on that jazz was her calling . In junior high school , she played in the jazz band and was given space to solo during a performance . &amp;ldquo ; &lt;span style= " font-family : -apple-system , BlinkMacSystemFont , ' Segoe UI ' , Roboto , Oxygen-Sans , Ubuntu , Cantarell , ' Helvetica Neue ' , sans-serif ; " &gt;I remember that concert and thinking , ' This is it , this is what I want to do , playing jazz , ' " Davis says . " That concert was kind of a pivotal moment for me and what I wanted to do for the rest of my life . " &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Davis dove into jazz with peers and teachers in Calgary and then attended the University of Toronto to study jazz . She developed a foundation in more traditional , conventional jazz &amp;mdash ; &amp;nbsp ; the music of pianists Keith Jarrett and Herbie Hancock were gateways into jazz , and like many a university jazz student , she played standard jazz repertoire and learned the rules of the bebop-based language . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But after she graduated , she gravitated to more left-leaning music that set aside typical harmonic and melodic material to embrace different improvising strategies and sonic inventions . When she attended the Banff Centre&amp;rsquo ; s famed summer jazz workshop in 2000 , Davis spent three weeks &amp;ldquo ; playing improvised music and really not understanding what was going on , &amp;rdquo ; she says . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Even if she was confused , Davis was undeterred . &amp;ldquo ; That&amp;rsquo ; s always the thing with me , &amp;rdquo ; she says . &amp;ldquo ; It was like , I&amp;rsquo ; m intrigued , I don&amp;rsquo ; t understand this , let&amp;rsquo ; s go deeper into it . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Going deeper meant moving to New York . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I did think if I stayed in Toronto , I&amp;rsquo ; d be , like , a jazz pianist and play standards &amp;hellip ; I was going to get lost in the fold because there was lots of people who could do that . It would have been fine . I was good at it . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; But I just wanted to do that ( more unconventional music ) at another level , and play with people , this is all they were doing . And find out what that was about . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It didn&amp;rsquo ; t long for Davis to begin to demystify the aesthetic that had intrigued her . She hung out with saxophonist Tony Malaby , who had been a faculty member at the Banff Centre , discussing ways to be play more texturally and structurally in free-form jazz situations . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; She studied 20th-century compositions by Gy&amp;ouml ; rgy Ligeti , Morton Feldman and others for insights . &amp;ldquo ; How do you create harmony without stepping on someone&amp;rsquo ; s toes or always directing ( the music ) ? &amp;rdquo ; Davis wondered . &amp;ldquo ; Going into the contemporary classical composers kind of answered that question for me. &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; She began recording in 2003 , and has been prolific , releasing almost 20 albums as a leader and playing on almost 20 more . She has made more bracing music for solo piano , small ensembles , duos with renowned collaborators such as guitarist Bill Frisell , saxophonist Tim Berne and pianist Craig Taborn , and a unique octet that includes three bass clarinetists and a contrabass clarinetist. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **34;22852;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;22888;TOOLONG &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **34;22924;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;22960;TOOLONG &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **33;22996;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;23031;TOOLONG &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **34;23067;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;23103;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; In 2011 , Ben Ratliff , then the influential jazz critic for the New York Times , singled out Davis as one of four &amp;ldquo ; new young pilots at the keyboard , &amp;rdquo ; and called her style &amp;ldquo ; dry and blunt and authoritative , and still changing . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In the last few years , Davis has begun working with the revered jazz drummer Terri Lyne Carrington , who is 15 years her senior and who travels in more mainstream musical circles . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Davis says that Carrington , who plays on the TD Ottawa Jazz Festival&amp;rsquo ; s main stage on Thursday , emailed her out of the blue to say she was a fan who hoped to make music with her . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;rdquo ; I said , &amp;lsquo ; Of course&amp;rsquo ; , &amp;rdquo ; Davis says . She and Carrington have made a lot of music since then , in different and even more mainstream contexts . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I can say it&amp;rsquo ; s all over the place , &amp;rdquo ; Davis says . &amp;ldquo ; But the great thing is that I&amp;rsquo ; m still just me and I&amp;rsquo ; m doing what I do . And the fact that people are interested in having that in things that are thought of as more traditional and straight-ahead , I&amp;rsquo ; m absolutely loving that . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Kris Davis &amp; Ingrid Laubrock&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;br&gt; &lt;strong&gt;TD Ottawa Jazz Festival&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;br&gt; &lt;strong&gt;When : &lt;/strong&gt; Sunday , June 22 , 6 p.m. &lt;br&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Where : &lt;/strong&gt; NAC Fourth Stage &lt;br&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Tickets : &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;a href= " http : //ottawajazzfestival.com " &gt;ottaw ... &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " **25;23139;TOOLONG " **32;23166;TOOLONG &lt;br&gt; &lt;a href= " http : //twitter.com/peterhum " &gt;twitter ... &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;nbsp ; &lt;/p&gt; Peter HumFri , 21 Jun 2019 20:26:28 **45;23200;TOOLONG ... ArtsJazzfest preview : Saxophonist and composer Anna Webber finds fun in complexityhttp : **30;23247;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;23279;TOOLONG ... title= " Anna Webber 's Simple Trio plays the 2019 TD Ottawa Jazz Festival . " alt= " Anna Webber 's Simple Trio plays the 2019 TD Ottawa Jazz Festival . " data-portal-copyright= " " **28;23315;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1416172 " /&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 100% " height= " 100% " frameborder= " 0 " allowfullscreen= " true " src= " https : **34;23345;TOOLONG ... &gt; &lt;/iframe&gt; &lt;p&gt; Fans of smart , ambitious , creative music should have the New York City-based Canadian expat Anna Webber on their radar . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Following her studies at McGill University , the Manhattan School of Music and the Jazz Institute Berlin , the B.C.-raised saxophonist , flutist and composer is making ever-increasing waves with her hypermodern music . Among her collaborators are Dave Douglas , John Hollenbeck and Matt Mitchell , all leading artists on jazz&amp;rsquo ; s cutting edge . Webber&amp;rsquo ; s most recent milestone was the release in February of Clockwise , her septet album on Pi Recordings , a record label revered for its heady , trendsetting music . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Webber , who is in her mid-30s , plays twice at the 2019 TD Ottawa Jazz Festival &amp;mdash ; on Friday with her archly named Simple Trio , which includes Hollenbeck and Mitchell , and then next Tuesday with the = Jazz Orchestra that will celebrate the writing and playing of female musicians at the forefront of Canada&amp;rsquo ; s jazz scene . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Webber&amp;rsquo ; s keen grasp of her artistic practice shines in the edited conversation below . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Q : How and why did you form the Simple Trio ? &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A : In 2011 , I moved to Berlin to do a master&amp;rsquo ; s degree at the Jazz Institute Berlin , which is where John Hollenbeck was teaching . I got to know him through studying composition with him . I met Matt when I moved back to New York from Berlin . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; I formed a trio with these two musicians because they are both fantastic improvisers and players with a similar artistic esthetic to me , and they are both excellent at playing challenging music but making it seem relaxed and natural . The first album that we recorded is called Simple , which is a little tongue in cheek , as that music is kind of anything but . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; I&amp;rsquo ; m not consciously writing different music for Matt and John than I would for my other projects . But I do know that I can more or less put anything in front of them and it will be played accurately immediately . So that certainly informs at least the level of complexity in the music that I feel comfortable bringing to this band . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; With this band it&amp;rsquo ; s less about the particular instrumentation but more about the particular musicians . These guys are very fun to compose for and even more fun to improvise with . I feel that the band has developed over these years into something really special . There&amp;rsquo ; s a deep level of musical trust and empathy . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; At this point , the Simple Trio has been more or less my primary working band since 2013 . It feels like it has longevity ! We have two albums out ( Simple and Binary , both on Skirl Records ) , and we&amp;rsquo ; re working on a third . We play a couple short tours a year on average , and some one-offs . John lives in Montreal whereas Matt and I are both in New York , so anything more than that would be fairly complicated . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; I can pretty directly trace my development as a composer through the music I&amp;rsquo ; ve been writing for this group , and I&amp;rsquo ; m very proud of the current collection of music . This music , which we&amp;rsquo ; ll be playing at the Ottawa Jazz Festival , is new and unreleased . It&amp;rsquo ; s a series of pieces called Idiom , all based on saxophone and flute extended techniques . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Q : How would you describe the sound that you&amp;rsquo ; re going for as a composer ? &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A : I like listening to music that is cohesive , through-composed , and develops in non-obvious ways . This is also the kind of music that I like writing . I like exploring different forms and structures and ways to shape improvisation . I like using numbers to develop pitch material . I try to make the entirety of every piece come from one simple idea or cell , which can be musical or non-musical . I then develop this cell in as many ways as I can and through this process let the piece show me what it wants to be . My personal taste falls somewhere between avant-garde jazz , new music , and math rock . So I guess the sound that I&amp;rsquo ; m going for as a composer is a blend of all of that . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Q : Tell me about some of your other recent musical achievements ( Clockwork , for example ) and ambitions . What did you set out to achieve and why ? &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A : Clockwise is a septet album that I released in February on Pi Recordings . The band is myself , Jeremy Viner on tenor and clarinet , Christopher Hoffman on cello , Jacob Garchik on trombone , Matt Mitchell on piano , Chris Tordini on bass , and Ches Smith on drums . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; I&amp;rsquo ; m super-happy with how the album turned out , and releasing it on Pi was a bit of a dream come true . The music on that album is all very loosely based on specific 20th-century classical percussion works &amp;mdash ; works for solo percussion or percussion ensemble . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; I used that as a starting place because it&amp;rsquo ; s music that I&amp;rsquo ; ve loved for a long time , and also because I think percussion music has answers to a lot of questions that interest me &amp;mdash ; How can timbre be the primary driving force of a piece of music ? What makes a piece coherent when what we traditionally call melody and harmony is subtracted ? &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Clockwise band is going to be playing the Saalfelden Festival in Austria this summer , and I&amp;rsquo ; m also planning on touring with the band a couple of times in 2020. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; On top of that , I am in the middle of writing the final Idiom piece , which is for a 12-piece large ensemble , which will feature a combination of improvisers and new music musicians . We&amp;rsquo ; ll be premiering that at John Zorn&amp;rsquo ; s series at National Sawdust in January next year . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Q : The Ottawa Jazz Festival this year set out achieve gender parity with its bookings . What do you think of this goal ? &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A : Obviously , I think that&amp;rsquo ; s great , and I applaud jazz festivals and presenters who are shooting for gender parity in booking . There are still fewer women than men in the field , so I know it can seem like an uphill battle , but one way of achieving parity overall is to normalize it institutionally . I also think it is important that the weight of discussion ( including in interviews such as this ) doesn&amp;rsquo ; t fall entirely on the backs of women . It&amp;rsquo ; s easy to ask these questions of women , but the topic is not just relevant to us . We are all in this together , and achieving parity will make things better for everyone . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Q : As a jazz student and musician , what discrimination and sexism have you experienced ? What do think of initiatives that are meant to address sexism in jazz ? &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A : Obviously all women have experienced sexism , be it in the jazz world or elsewhere . I&amp;rsquo ; d rather not talk about my specific experiences in a public way . I&amp;rsquo ; d rather talk about my music ! I think that the ultimate goal of any sort of initiative in this direction is that one day soon we can focus on the things that are actually important&amp;nbsp ; &amp;mdash ; the art ! &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Anna Webber 's Simple Trio&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;br&gt; When : Friday , June 21 , 6 p.m. &lt;br&gt; Where : NAC Fourth Stage &lt;br&gt; Tickets : &lt;a href= " http : //ottawajazzfestival.com " &gt;ottaw ... &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " **25;23381;TOOLONG " **32;23408;TOOLONG &lt;br&gt; &lt;a href= " http : //twitter.com/peterhum " &gt;twitter ... &lt;/p&gt; &lt;hr&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;ALSO IN THE NEWS : &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;23442;TOOLONG ... the spotlight : When everyone has a cell phone , police use of force under scrutiny&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " http : **34;23477;TOOLONG ... gets update on LeBreton , signs off on new site for Afghanistan war monument&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " http : **34;23513;TOOLONG ... might ship off its swans . But in Stratford , swans are king&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;hr&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;nbsp ; &lt;/p&gt; Peter HumFri , 21 Jun 2019 20:26:12 **45;23549;TOOLONG ... ArtsTD Ottawa Jazz Festival concert rediscovers prolific NFB composer Eldon Rathburnhttp : **32;23596;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;23630;TOOLONG ... title= " The late Canadian Eldon Rathburn is shown here in a 2007 photo . " alt= " The late Canadian Eldon Rathburn is shown here in a 2007 photo . " data-portal-copyright= " Bruno Schlumberger " **28;23666;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1416486 " /&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 100% " height= " 100% " frameborder= " 0 " allowfullscreen= " true " src= " https : **34;23696;TOOLONG ... &gt; &lt;/iframe&gt; &lt;p&gt; Jazz fans familiar with the genius of Duke Ellington or John Coltrane could be forgiven for seeing a listing for the jazz concert in Ottawa on Sunday called The Romance of Improvisation in Canada : the Genius of Eldon Rathburn and thinking &amp;ldquo ; Who&amp;rsquo ; s he ? &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Rathburn , who was born in Queenstown , NB , was one of Canada&amp;rsquo ; s most prolific composers , having written more than 100 concert works and scored more than 250 films . A full-time composer at the National Film Board from 1947 to 1976 , Rathburn lived in Vanier for the first decade of his career when the NFB was located in Ottawa , and moved here after he retired . He died in Ottawa in 2008 at the age of 92. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Sunday&amp;rsquo ; s concert at the TD Ottawa Jazz Festival adapts some of Rathburn&amp;rsquo ; s music into the jazz realm and stars a powerful quintet that includes Ottawa saxophonist Petr Cancura , Toronto trumpeter Kevin Turcotte and three Montrealers &amp;mdash ; pianist Marianne Trudel , bassist Adrian Vedady and drummer Jim Doxas . The rediscovery of Rathburn&amp;rsquo ; s music , sparked by Carleton University music professor James Wright&amp;rsquo ; s new book They Shot , He Scored : The Life and Music of Eldon Rathburn , will continue this year at other Ottawa events . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Ottawa drummer Allyson Rogers and Ottawa saxophonist Adrian Matte generated the quintet&amp;rsquo ; s jazz materials and produced its recently released album on Justin Time Records . Below , Rogers explains the appeal of a fresh jazz take on Rathburn. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Q : What was Eldon Rathburn 's connection to jazz ? &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;A : &lt;/strong&gt; Rathburn was a musical polyglot and a voracious listener . This made him a phenomenal composer for the NFB . Rathburn kept meticulous listening logs , and jazz greats such as Duke Ellington , John Coltrane and Gunther Schuller show up alongside Charles Ives , Paul Hindemith and Schoenberg. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Although Rathburn&amp;rsquo ; s greatest sources of inspiration were modernist composers , he wrote a handful of jazz scores in the 1950s that are full of great melodies and creative instrumentation . The most notable are The Romance of Transportation in Canada ( 1952 ) , from which the name of this project is derived , The Structure of Unions ( 1955 ) , Fish Spoilage Control ( 1956 ) , and Short and Suite ( 1959 ) . Even though he uses classical language and notation , it&amp;rsquo ; s clear that he understands the intricacies of harmonic movement and motivic development in jazz . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Q : What prompted your Rathburn project ? &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;A : &lt;/strong&gt; I spent a lot of time at the NFB watching films and digging in the archives when I was working with James Wright on his book . The music for Fish Spoilage Control and The Romance of Transportation really stood out . Fortunately the handwritten scores were also in the archives . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Adrian Matte and I initially thought there might be material here we could arrange and play ourselves . However , after looking at the scores more carefully , we quickly realized the potential for a much larger project . The music is challenging and we wanted to find the right musicians to do it justice . We shifted away from casting ourselves as performers , and stepped into the role of arrangers , producers and project managers . The enthusiasm and support we received at each step , especially from the NFB and the musicians , kept our motivation high . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Q : How did you make jazz arrangements from Rathburn 's work ? &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;A : &lt;/strong&gt; Creating the arrangements was very time-consuming and truly collaborative . Adrian and I listened to the films over and over again , with and without the visuals . The NFB provided us with copies of the handwritten parts , so we had to enter everything into Finale ( music notation software ) to work with the material digitally . Fish Spoilage Control and The Romance of Transportation each contain a dozen short sections of music ( cues ) that range from approximately 30 seconds to a minute and a half . We identified the cues we felt had the strongest thematic material and then worked side by side , moving sections around , trying out ideas , listening back and making adjustments . Essentially , we arranged the material into more standard song forms that could be used as the basis for improvisation . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Q : How did you cast the project 's musicians ? &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;A : &lt;/strong&gt; Petr Cancura was on board from the beginning . We asked him to put together the right quintet . Funding from the Ontario Arts Council and the City of Ottawa , as well as the participation of the NFB , made it possible to hire a dream team of jazz musicians who took the music to another level in the studio . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Q : Tell me about the recording . &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;A : &lt;/strong&gt; We spent five days recording and editing in the Chester Beachell studio at the NFB in Montreal where Rathburn had worked for most of his career . It was a fantastic experience in a historic studio . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The album is very eclectic and reflects the wide range of jazz and popular dance styles in the films . We sometimes pushed the material even further . The series of free improvisations ( Ox Driver&amp;rsquo ; s Blues , parts I , II , and III ) are all based on a blues motif from a 30-second film cue in Romance . It worked really well and gives a sense of unity to these tracks . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Q : How will the Rathburn project continue after Sunday ? &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;A : &lt;/strong&gt; We worked with 10 film cues for the album , which is only half of the material in Fish Spoilage and Romance . There are also a few other jazz scores by Rathburn that we haven&amp;rsquo ; t looked at yet . Since the musicians are asking for more , Adrian and I are planning another album . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Now that the book is out , we&amp;rsquo ; re working on events in several cities to celebrate both Rathburn projects . The Ottawa book launch will be part of the Ottawa International Chamber Music Festival on Aug. 5 at Dominion-Chalmers . Wright will give a talk and three of Rathburn&amp;rsquo ; s chamber works will be performed . The quintet will perform at the National Arts Centre on Nov. 7 . This will be a multimedia event and a chance to see some of the films . A larger event in Montreal this fall is also in the works . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;The Romance of Improvisation in Canada : The Genius of Eldon Rathburn&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;br&gt; &lt;strong&gt;TD Ottawa Jazz Festival&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;br&gt; &lt;strong&gt;When : &lt;/strong&gt; Sunday , June 23 , 8 p.m. &lt;br&gt; **34;23732;TOOLONG : NAC Fourth Stage &lt;br&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Tickets : &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;a href= " http : //ottawajazzfestival.com " &gt;ottaw ... &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " **25;23768;TOOLONG " **32;23795;TOOLONG &lt;br&gt; &lt;a href= " http : //twitter.com/peterhum " &gt;twitter ... &lt;/p&gt; &lt;hr&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;ALSO : &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;23829;TOOLONG ... Citizen 's music writers share their 2019 TD Ottaw Jazz Festival picks&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;23864;TOOLONG ... preview : Questing pianist Kris Davis says her art is " all over the place ... but still just me " &lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;23899;TOOLONG ... preview : Saxophonist and composer Anna Webber finds fun in complexity&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; Peter HumFri , 21 Jun 2019 20:25:43 **45;23934;TOOLONG ... ArtsIt takes courage to leave the musical comfort zone , Gilad Hekselman sayshttp : **36;23981;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;24019;TOOLONG ... title= " Guitarist Gilad Hekselman plays the 2019 TD Ottawa Jazz Festival on June 21 " alt= " Guitarist Gilad Hekselman plays the 2019 TD Ottawa Jazz Festival on June 21 " data-portal-copyright= " " **28;24055;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1414920 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Gilad Hekselman is blunt about what it&amp;rsquo ; s taken to become as accomplished as he is musically . In a word , it&amp;rsquo ; s obsession . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; It&amp;rsquo ; s basically just being obsessive and completely dedicated for years , years and years , without thinking about a career or anything like that , &amp;rdquo ; says the 36-year-old Israeli-born , New York City-based guitarist . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Fortunately , Hekselman has seen considerable returns for his efforts . He&amp;rsquo ; s widely regarded as one of the top jazz guitarists of his generation , such that guitar elder Pat Metheny sings his praises . This month , Hekselman played with his trio in Asia before the group&amp;rsquo ; s upcoming Canadian concerts , one of which is a show Friday night in the NAC&amp;rsquo ; s Fourth Stage . Next month , touring in Europe will beckon . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **34;24085;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;24121;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; It&amp;rsquo ; s a good thing that Hekselman&amp;rsquo ; s dedication to music began when he was very young . Growing up near Tel Aviv , he was first drawn to music after he rented a video of the 1988 Michael Jackson movie Moonwalker. &amp;ldquo ; I was kind of in a state of shock . It was the coolest thing I&amp;rsquo ; d ever seen , &amp;rdquo ; he remembers . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Hekselman played piano and then drums , which didn&amp;rsquo ; t sit so well with his neighbours , before settling on guitar when he was nine years old . &amp;ldquo ; ( I was ) just spending hours and hours with the instrument because I loved it a lot , &amp;rdquo ; Hekselman recalls . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; By the time he was 12 , Hekselman was a pro , playing guitar in the youthful band of a national TV talk show . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I used to go and spend days in the recording studio every week , &amp;rdquo ; he says . &amp;ldquo ; That was a big part of my life , but also a big part of my musical development , because all the musicians in the band were slightly older than me &amp;mdash ; still kids , but at that age , it felt like significantly older . They showed me stuff , some of my first exposures to anything that kind of led to jazz . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Jazz didn&amp;rsquo ; t enter into the picture for the guitarist until he attended the prestigious Thelma Yellin High School&amp;nbsp ; of the&amp;nbsp ; Arts . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; At that point , I was kind of like a sponge . I still try to be , but when I went to that high school , I was 14 , and I saw a lot of other kids and it sounded cool , &amp;rdquo ; says Hekselman. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I remember listening to the music and pretty early on getting excited about the fact that you can actually communicate in a way that&amp;rsquo ; s similar to a language in this music , &amp;rdquo ; he adds . &amp;ldquo ; That&amp;rsquo ; s something I didn&amp;rsquo ; t have so much in rock . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **33;24157;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;24192;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; While in high school , Hekselman was told that if he wanted to continue pursuing jazz , the right thing to do was to move to New York . He went there for a few months on an artist&amp;rsquo ; s visa when he was 18 and had the time of his life , hanging out at clubs and jam sessions. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; After returning to Israel for a year and a half , Hekselman moved for good to New York , where he now has a wife , who is a music teacher , and a young son . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Many an aspiring 20-something has come to New York with jazz dreams . How did Hekselman rise to where he is now ? &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; He took lessons from more established musicians and stresses that &lt;span style= " font-family : -apple-system , BlinkMacSystemFont , ' Segoe UI ' , Roboto , Oxygen-Sans , Ubuntu , Cantarell , ' Helvetica Neue ' , sans-serif ; " &gt;he was able to improve by challenging himself , acknowledging his weaknesses and working to eradicate them . Or as he says more succinctly , by " getting my ass kicked . " &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; By that , he means &amp;ldquo ; basically being on gigs with musicians that are much better than me , including my own band at times . Trying to play with the best musicians I can , and if the gig goes great , great , and if I think I was lacking , then being pretty particular about what I was lacking in. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; The tendency &amp;mdash ; and I used to do this too &amp;mdash ; is to go , &amp;lsquo ; Oh man , I really suck , I didn&amp;rsquo ; t sound good . &amp;rsquo ; But that&amp;rsquo ; s lazy . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; That feeling has a treasure in it , which is how you can grow . It&amp;rsquo ; s your artist&amp;rsquo ; s sense that is teaching you . I can see how I can be better , but I&amp;rsquo ; m not quite there yet , so let&amp;rsquo ; s work on it . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; These days , Hekselman plays with the elite of the modern jazz mainstream , including the bands of peers such as drummer Ari Hoenig and saxophonist Ben Wendel . He has recorded six albums as a leader and favours playing in quartet and trio settings . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **34;24228;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;24264;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; &amp;nbsp ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **34;24300;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;24336;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; Hekselman says he&amp;rsquo ; s been playing in a trio since he was 16 because he is &amp;ldquo ; a massive fan of piano trios . A big part of my playing is basically me attempting to sound like a piano player . For me , that&amp;rsquo ; s the perfect instrumentation to do that . I try to imagine what some of my heroes would play &amp;mdash ; Bill Evans , Brad Mehldau , Ahmad Jamal , Keith Jarrett. &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Hekselman&amp;rsquo ; s bandmates , Montreal-raised bassist Rick Rosato and drummer Jonathan Pinson , &amp;ldquo ; are great musicians . ( But ) it&amp;rsquo ; s more their attitude to music that really speaks to me , &amp;rdquo ; Hekselman says . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We have a rule in this band where it should never be the same , &amp;rdquo ; he continues . &amp;ldquo ; Whenever we feel too comfortable with something , we have to change it up . That kind of attitude really keeps everybody on their toes and really keeps us interested in the music . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Anything can change in real time , Hekselman says , from the beat of the tune to its order of solos , from its dynamics and arc to its arrangement . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; It takes courage to not know what&amp;rsquo ; s going to happen every night , &amp;rdquo ; Hekselman says . &amp;ldquo ; But that feeling , it has adrenalin , it has life in it . I think it makes the music exciting &amp;mdash ; especially to play , but also to listen to . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **34;24372;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;24408;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Gilad Hekselman Trio&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;br&gt; &lt;strong&gt;When : &lt;/strong&gt; June 21 , 8 p.m. &lt;br&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Where : &lt;/strong&gt; NAC Fourth Stage : &lt;br&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Tickets : &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;a href= " http : //ottawajazzfestival.com " &gt;ottaw ... &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt;The Gilad Hekselsman Trio also plays : &lt;/em&gt; &lt;br&gt; &lt;em&gt;June 20 at the &lt;span class= " st " &gt;Le Festival Qu ? bec Jazz en Juin&lt;/span&gt; in Quebec City&lt;/em&gt; &lt;br&gt; &lt;em&gt;June 22 at the Rochester Jazz Festival in Rochester , NY&lt;/em&gt; &lt;br&gt; &lt;em&gt;July 3 at the Upstairs Jazz Bar and Grill in Montreal&lt;/em&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " **25;24444;TOOLONG " **32;24471;TOOLONG &lt;br&gt; &lt;a href= " http : //twitter.com/peterhum " &gt;twitter ... &lt;/p&gt; Peter HumWed , 19 Jun 2019 13:46:23 **45;24505;TOOLONG ... ArtsThe Citizen 's music writers share their 2019 TD Ottawa Jazz Festival pickshttp : **35;24552;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;24589;TOOLONG ... title= " Saxophonist Melissa Aldana , who plays the 2019 TD Ottawa Jazz Festival on Wednesday , June 26. " alt= " Saxophonist Melissa Aldana , who plays the 2019 TD Ottawa Jazz Festival on Wednesday , June 26. " data-portal-copyright= " " **28;24625;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1414820 " /&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 100% " height= " 100% " frameborder= " 0 " allowfullscreen= " true " src= " https : **34;24655;TOOLONG ... &gt; &lt;/iframe&gt; &lt;p&gt; In the last few years , in step with the backlash against a p&amp;mdash ; &amp;ndash ; grabbing U.S. president and the rise of the MeToo Movement , it became more apparent than ever that jazz had problems with patriarchy and sexism. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Big-name jazz professors left their jobs after allegations of sexual misconduct surfaced . Prominent jazz pianists Robert Glasper and Ethan Iverson were vilified after their conversation , printed in March 2017 on Iverson&amp;rsquo ; s well-read blog , delved into a discussion of why women listeners prefer grooves over complex improvised melodies. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Women &amp;ldquo ; don&amp;rsquo ; t love a whole lot of soloing . When you hit that one groove and stay there , it&amp;rsquo ; s like a musical clitoris , &amp;rdquo ; Glasper notoriously said . &amp;ldquo ; You&amp;rsquo ; re there , you stay on that groove , and the women&amp;rsquo ; s eyes close and they start to sway , going into a trance . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Glasper&amp;rsquo ; s comments came as a shock to exactly zero people who&amp;rsquo ; ve spent any time in the jazz world , &amp;rdquo ; responded U.S. author and jazz journalist Michelle Mercer in a column that she wrote for NPR. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; This scandal reveals that the issue of women in jazz goes deeper , into a gendered construction of the music itself , &amp;rdquo ; Mercer continued . &amp;ldquo ; We need an intelligent public discussion about gendered notions of jazz , and this hot mess might as well be the impetus for that discussion . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Since then , the jazz world has seen more music made with a feminist slant , more all-female bands , and even the recent creation of an Institute of Jazz and Gender Justice at Boston&amp;rsquo ; s Berklee College of Music &amp;mdash ; a respected institution that had been plagued by &amp;ldquo ; a culture of blatant sexual harassment , &amp;rdquo ; according to a November 2017 Boston Globe investigation . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It may well be that this year&amp;rsquo ; s TD Ottawa Jazz Festival , which starts Friday and runs until Canada Day , advances the cause of women in jazz more boldly than any of its Canadian and U.S. counterparts , thanks to its new commitment to strive for gender parity in its bookings . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; As usual , the festival continues to be basically two events in one &amp;mdash ; an outdoor festival based in Marion Dewar Plaza with much mass-appeal , cross-over music in the mix , plus an indoor festival based in the National Arts Centre presenting more uncompromisingly jazz-based music . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But this year , the bookings prominently featuring women &amp;mdash ; vocalists and instrumentalists alike &amp;mdash ; make up roughly half of the festival&amp;rsquo ; s bookings , from Norah Jones , Jane Siberry , Judith Hill , Cuban vocalist Omara Portuondo and former Prince guitarist Donna Grantis in the first category to the bonafide jazz stars Patricia Barber , Cyrille Aim&amp;eacute ; e , Kris Davis , Mary Halvorson , Melissa Aldana , Anna Webber and Terri Lyne Carrington , plus rising stars Nubya Garcia and Sasha Berliner . Montreal-based saxophonist and composer Christine Jensen , a two-time Juno Award winner , will lead the festival&amp;rsquo ; s first gender-balanced big band that will include Canadian jazz stars Marianne Trudel , Allison Au , Tara Davidson and Rebecca Hennessy . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Ottawa may involve the best assemblage of women jazz musicians ever ! &amp;rdquo ; Mercer commented when pointed to the lineup . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Other commenters lauded the festival not only for trying to equalize the presence of men and women on its stages , but simply for the excellence of the musicians , gender aside , and the lineup&amp;rsquo ; s generational shift . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; While past jazz festivals in Ottawa and elsewhere could get caught up in nostalgically booking older musicians ( and attracting grey-haired listeners ) , this year&amp;rsquo ; s Ottawa festival also showcases more jazz musicians in their 30s , 40s and early 50s , including many of the women named above and also piano star Brad Mehldau , saxophonist Donny McCaslin and guitarist Gilad Hekselman. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The crush of talent seems all the more obvious given that this year&amp;rsquo ; s festival is shorter than usual , starting on a Friday rather than a Thursday and with its Monday without performances for the first time . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Below , music writers Peter Hum and Lynn Saxberg zero in on the concerts they&amp;rsquo ; re most excited about . Coincidentally , women figure as much as men in their picks . &lt;/p&gt; **29;24691;TOOLONG Hum 's **31;24722;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Anna Webber 's Simple Trio&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;br&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Friday , June 21 , 6 p.m. , NAC Fourth Stage&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The B.C.-born , New York-based saxophonist , flutist and composer has grown to become one of the most invigorating musicians on jazz&amp;rsquo ; s cutting edge . Be warned that Webber&amp;rsquo ; s &amp;ldquo ; Simple Trio&amp;rdquo ; is named archly , as her compositions are fiendishly tricky and innovative . It&amp;rsquo ; s a good thing pianist Matt Mitchell and drummer John Hollenbeck ( an American who&amp;rsquo ; s the current chair of McGill University&amp;rsquo ; s jazz department ) are world-class avant-gardists who revel in interpreting Webber&amp;rsquo ; s intriguing , challenging material . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **34;24755;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;24791;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Gilad Hekselman Trio&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;br&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Friday , June 21 , 8 p.m. , NAC Fourth Stage&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; No less than jazz guitar hero Pat Metheny has singled out New York City-based guitarist Hekselman as a rising star . One listen to the 36-year-old native of Israel helps to explain why . Hekselman is a lucid , lyrical player at the fore of jazz&amp;rsquo ; s modern mainstream , blessed with a mastery of tone and a knack for making fresh , personal music feel accessible and even ineffably right . At the NAC&amp;rsquo ; s Fourth Stage , Hekselman will perform with Montreal-raised bassist Rick Rosato and drummer Jonathan Pinson , a supportive pair that accompanied the guitarist earlier this month in China and Japan . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **33;24827;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;24862;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Kris Davis &amp; Ingrid Laubrock&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;br&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Sunday , June 23 , 6 p.m. , NAC Fourth Stage&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In 2010 , Ben Ratliff , then the jazz critic for the New York Times , wrote : &amp;ldquo ; Over the last couple of years in New York , one method for deciding where to hear jazz on a given night has been to track down the pianist Kris Davis . &amp;rdquo ; In the decade since , the Calgary-raised musician has demonstrated astonishing breadth and imagination as a vital figure on jazz and creative music&amp;rsquo ; s leftmost flank . She and German saxophonist Laubrock are long-time , perfectly paired collaborators who explore bracing music that intertwines knotty compositions and unfettered improvising . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Jakob Bro / Thomas Morgan / Joey Baron&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;br&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Tuesday , June 25 , 6 p.m. , NAC Fourth Stage&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; For the last decade , the award-winning Danish guitarist Bro , 41 , has been collaborating with the cream of the American jazz crop to make thoughtful and even dreamy music in line with ECM Records&amp;rsquo ; s evocative aesthetic . At the NAC Fourth Stage , Bro will lead a trio that includes bassist Thomas Morgan and drummer Joey Baron , two U.S. musicians with whom he has released two records on ECM in recent years . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **33;24898;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;24933;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;= Jazz Orchestra &amp; Christine Jensen&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;br&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Tuesday , June 25 , 8 p.m. , NAC Studio&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A large ensemble formed to wave the flag for gender parity at this year&amp;rsquo ; s festival , this group also pulls together some of the best jazz musicians of their generation in Montreal , Toronto and Ottawa . Saxophonist Christine Jensen , the two-time Juno Award winner from Montreal , Toronto-based horn players , Rebecca Hennessy , Tara Davidson and Allison Au , plus pianist Marianne Trudel will shine most brightly as this command performance will feature their compositions as well as their playing . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Melissa Aldana Quartet&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;br&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Wednesday , June 26 , 6 p.m. , NAC Fourth Stage&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; After winning the 2013 Thelonious Monk International Jazz Saxophone Competition at the age of 24 , the Chilean-born musician with a thrilling , virtuosic modern style saw her star rise dramatically . In Ottawa next Wednesday , Aldana kicks off a slate of especially promising concerts . Leading a quartet that includes the equally young and compelling pianist Sam Harris , Aldana will likely showcase some of the surging , heady music from her new album , Visions , whose compositions were inspired by the life and works of the late Mexican artist Frida Kahlo . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **34;24969;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;25005;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Illegal Crowns&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;br&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Wednesday , June 26 , 8 p.m. , NAC Fourth Stage&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The collective quartet called Illegal Crowns consists of four highly regarded and bristling improvisors : drummer Tomas Fujiwara , guitarist Mary Halvorson , cornet player Taylor Ho Bynum and pianist Benoit Delbecq . While the first three musicians , who are all New York City-based , are able to play together regularly , the quartet with French pianist Delbecq is a more sporadic project . Expect creative sparks to fly when the four strong and outward-bound personalities are reunited . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Brad Mehldau Quintet&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;br&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Wednesday , June 26 , 8:30 p.m. , TD main stage , Marion Dewar Plaza&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; There&amp;rsquo ; s seemingly little that Mehldau , the most influential pianist in jazz over the last two decades hasn&amp;rsquo ; t tried . He&amp;rsquo ; s led a breathtaking trio since the mid-1990s , dazzled as a virtuosic soloist who can delve into Bach one day and Radiohead the next , and played equally moving duets with classic soprano Renee Fleming and mandolinist Chris Thile . The 48-year-old will bring a new all-star quintet to Ottawa for what should be the most jazz-rich of the festival&amp;rsquo ; s main-stage concerts . Expect horn players Ambrose Akinmusire ( trumpet ) and Joel Frahm ( tenor saxophone ) to thrill , while drummer Leon Parker is renowned for his uniquely buoyant , minimalist swinging . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Hayden Chisholm Trio&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;br&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Wednesday , June 26 , 7 p.m. , NAC Studio&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;br&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Thursday , June 27 , 6 p.m. , NAC Fourth Stage&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Raised in New Zealand and based in Serbia , alto saxophonist Chisholm is an infrequent visitor to North America . That&amp;rsquo ; s a shame , because the 44-year-old is an engaging musician with a softened , appealing tone , great melodic agility and a quirky penchants for microtones and the Indian drone-producing shruti box . He will perform twice at the festival , on Wednesday with a larger ensemble that will include Ottawa-area string players , and then on Thursday with his long-time collaborators Matt Penman on bass and Jochen Rueckert on drums . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **34;25041;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;25077;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Terri Lyne Carrington and Social Science&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;br&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Thursday , June 27 , 8:30 p.m. , TD main stage , Marion Dewar Plaza&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A prodigious talent who received a full scholarship to Berklee College of Music when she was 11 , drummer Carrington has blossomed into not just a leader of formidable ensembles but also a champion of gender issues , having recently created an Institute for Gender Justice at her alma mater . In Ottawa , the 53-year-old will present her forward-thinking and politically progressive project Social Science , which includes the incandescent thirty-something musicians Aaron Parks ( keyboards ) and Matthew Stevens ( guitar ) and Kassa Overall ( DJ ) along with vocalist Debo Ray and multi-instrumentalist Morgan Guerin. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **34;25113;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;25149;TOOLONG **28;25185;TOOLONG Saxberg 's **31;25215;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Judith Hill&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;br&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Friday , June 21 , 8:30 p.m. , TD Main Stage&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The powerhouse singer was one of the backup vocalists profiled in the documentary 20 Feet From Stardom , recognizing her work with the likes of Prince , Stevie Wonder , Elton John , Michael Jackson and more . She stepped into the spotlight with her Prince-produced 2015 debut album , which demonstrated her skill as a songwriter , too . Last year&amp;rsquo ; s follow-up , Golden Child , is another showcase of her ability to weave synth pop , gospel , rock and soul into one funky brew . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Fun fact : Her dad is pioneering funk bassist Robert Lee Hill ( and is in her band ) , while her mom , Michiko Hill , is an accomplished keyboardist . The couple also run a studio in L.A. Growing up , Judith always had singers in her room because her bedroom was the vocal booth . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Galactic featuring Erica Falls&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;br&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Saturday , June 22 , 8:30 p.m. , TD Main Stage&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; New Orleans funk-and-jazz jam band Galactic blast off in a new direction with their 10th and latest album , Already Ready Already . Produced by band members Robert Mercurio and Ben Ellman , the record finds them joining forces with an array of emerging singers , including vocalist Erica Falls , who&amp;rsquo ; s heralded as the queen of New Orleans neo-soul . She&amp;rsquo ; s also touring with the band , providing a refreshing change from the band&amp;rsquo ; s usual male-dominated groove-making. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **34;25248;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;25284;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; Fun fact : Galactic recently purchased Tipitina&amp;rsquo ; s , the legendary New Orleans nightclub where they&amp;rsquo ; ve been playing regular gigs for 25 years , including an annual all-nighter on the eve of Mardi Gras. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Jane Siberry&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;br&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Saturday , June 22 , 10:30 p.m. , OLG Stage , Confederation Park&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Canada&amp;rsquo ; s free-ranging songbird returns to one of her favourite nests , Ottawa , a city where she has found inspiration and plenty of support from the folk-music community over the years . She&amp;rsquo ; ll be accompanied by a small but mighty ensemble consisting of Ottawa-born , New York-based music director Peter Kiesewalter on piano , Julian Armour on bass and Whitney LaGrange on violin . It&amp;rsquo ; s hard to predict what she&amp;rsquo ; ll play but we know that Siberry&amp;rsquo ; s arty mid-&amp;rsquo ; 80s hit , Mimi On The Beach , has been resurfacing in recent concerts , and we hear there are new tunes to unveil , too . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Fun fact : Siberry once revealed that she enjoys a simple baked potato before performing , and won&amp;rsquo ; t go on stage without lipstick and her hair done up . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; **36;25320;TOOLONG &lt;br&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Sunday , June 23 , 8:30 p.m. , TD Main Stage&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Chicago has been going strong for half a century , proud of the fact that they&amp;rsquo ; ve never missed a show in 52 years . They formed in Chicago , of course , in 1967 as the Chicago Transit Authority , with the intention of writing politically charged rock &amp;lsquo ; n&amp;rsquo ; roll songs powered by a horn section . Instead they shortened the name and hit the charts with songs like If You Leave Me Now , Saturday in the Park , Hard to Say I&amp;rsquo ; m Sorry and 25 or 6 to 4 , becoming staples of the &amp;rsquo ; 70s&amp;rsquo ; wave of soft rock . Three original members are still in the band , and they still tour with several truckloads of gear , making it the biggest production of the jazz festival&amp;rsquo ; s main stage this year . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Fun fact : In the early years of touring , the budget only allowed for two hotel rooms , one for sleeping and one for partying . Which was fine because &amp;ldquo ; we didn&amp;rsquo ; t need much sleep back in those days , &amp;rdquo ; recalls trumpeter Lee Loughnane. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; **40;25358;TOOLONG &lt;br&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Thursday , June 27 , 10:30 p.m. , OLG Stage , Confederation Park&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; DakhaBrakha was created 15 years ago by an avant-garde theatre director in Kyiv to liven up his shows , but probably not to dominate them , which is what happens whenever they play . With a name that means give/take in old Ukrainian , the quartet specializes in the folk music of their homeland , reinventing it for a modern age with an unapologetic feminist message . In traditionally-inspired costumes , Marko Halanevych , Olena Tsybulska , Nina Garenetska and Iryna Kovalenko rev up the rhythms with instruments from around the world , including tabla , djembe , accordion , bass , cello , didgeridoo and more . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **33;25400;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;25435;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; Fun fact : DakhaBrakha&amp;rsquo ; s first show at Bonnaroo in 2014 was such a hit that Rolling Stone declared them the breakout act of the festival , coining the new musical subgenre : &amp;ldquo ; Ukrainian folkdrone Bjorkpunk. &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Omara Portuondo with NAC Orchestra&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;br&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Friday , June 28 , 8:30 p.m. , TD Main Stage&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The legendary Cuban singer , who&amp;rsquo ; s well into her 80s , is renowned as the diva ( and the only female voice ) of the Buena Vista Social Club . International fame came with the &amp;nbsp ; Grammy-winning Buena Vista album and an Oscar-nominated documentary , but she had been a superstar at home for decades , equally comfortable with all the Afro-Cuban styles , from boleros to rumba . On this tour , a final , worldwide journey she calls her Last Kiss tour , she&amp;rsquo ; ll be performing songs from Cuba&amp;rsquo ; s golden era with the lush and expert backing of the NAC Orchestra . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **34;25471;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;25507;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; Fun fact : Portuondo plays the good witch who lives in the Louisiana swamps in the Spanish version of Disney&amp;rsquo ; s animated The Princess and the Frog . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Cha Wa&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;br&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Friday , June 28 , 10:30 p.m. , OLG Stage , Confederation Park&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Two of the musical traditions that make New Orleans&amp;rsquo ; street culture so rich find a common groove in Cha Wa . The New Orleans band combines the big , brassy marching band sound with Mardi Gras Indian culture to come up with a funk-laced , sousaphone-soaked twist on the party music of the Big Easy . While some of the songs have a social-justice message , you can dance to any of them . Their latest album , Spyboy , is produced by fellow jazzfest player , Ben Ellman of the band Galactic. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Fun fact : Singer J&amp;rsquo ; Wan Boudreaux is the grandson of Big Chief Monk Boudreaux of the Golden Eagles Mardi Gras Indian tribe . For more than 100 years , Indian tribes have paraded through New Orleans on Mardi Gras , decked out in elaborate costumes they craft themselves . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;The Roots&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;br&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Saturday , June 29 , 8:30 p.m. , TD Main Stage&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; After wowing the jazzfest crowd for the 35th anniversary edition of the festival four years ago , the legendary Philadelphia hiphop crew The Roots are back to strut more of their stuff on the main stage . Last time Jimmy Fallon&amp;rsquo ; s house band tailored their show to a jazz audience , with plenty of jazz-infused hiphop and a Herbie Hancock cover , along with crowd-pleasing surprises by the likes of Guns N Roses , Led Zeppelin and Kool and the Gang . And who knows ? Maybe they&amp;rsquo ; ll bring some of those delightful classroom instruments they use for the viral videos produced with Fallon and his superstar musical guests . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Fun fact : You don&amp;rsquo ; t have to go far for the after-party . Roots&amp;rsquo ; DJ Questlove plays a DJ set on the OLG Stage in Confederation Park as part of the After Dark Series . It starts at 10:30 p.m. after the main set ( June 29 ) . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Nubya **31;25543;TOOLONG &lt;strong&gt;Saturday , June 29 , 4 p.m. , NAC Fourth Stage&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The saxophonist is one of a generation of young musicians in London finding their way into jazz from the club scene , a wave that Rolling Stone magazine describes as a new British invasion . Raised by Caribbean parents , Garcia discovered the tenor sax at the tender age of 10 , after learning piano , violin and recorder . She made her recording debut in 2017 with the fearless 5ive EP and followed that last year with the electronic-infused When We Are . Her Afro-tinged jazz doesn&amp;rsquo ; t go wild on technique but , as an NPR reviewer said , she has an &amp;ldquo ; imploring and soulful style on tenor , with enough controlled brawn to evoke a touchstone like Sonny Rollins. &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **34;25576;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;25612;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; Fun fact : Garcia was one of the few jazz artists to showcase at SXSW in Austin last year . A month later , she was in the crowd at Coachella to see Beyonce. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; *** &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;TD Ottawa Jazz Festival&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;br&gt; &lt;strong&gt;When : &lt;/strong&gt; June 21 to July 1 &lt;br&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Where : &lt;/strong&gt; Marion Dewar Plaza , National Arts Centre , Confederation Park , Queen St. Fare , Lord Elgin Hotel , Mercury Lounge &lt;br&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Info and tickets : &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;a href= " http : //ottawajazzfestival.com " &gt;ottaw ... &lt;/p&gt; &lt;hr&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;LISTEN TO THE 10/3 PODCAST&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe src= " **40;25648;TOOLONG ... Peter HumWed , 19 Jun 2019 11:45:02 **45;25690;TOOLONG ... ArtsMusicAaron Parks album reviewedhttp : **32;25737;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;25771;TOOLONG ... title= " Pianist Aaron Parks ' Little Big band consists of , left to right , Parks , bassist David Ginyard , guitarist Greg Tuohey and drummer Tommy Crane " alt= " Pianist Aaron Parks ' Little Big band consists of , left to right , Parks , bassist David Ginyard , guitarist Greg Tuohey and drummer Tommy Crane " data-portal-copyright= " Deneka Peniston " **28;25807;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1412992 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Little Big ( Ropeadope Select ) &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;br&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Aaron Parks&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Over the years , I&amp;rsquo ; ve gone out of my way to be nearby when Aaron Parks plays piano . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; I first heard him , quite fortuitously , in the winter of 2003 , when he played a trio set at the big International Association for Jazz Education get-together in Toronto . Parks &amp;mdash ; who played then with bassist Matt Brewer and drummer Obed Calvaire , if memory serves &amp;mdash ; was scarcely more than 19 . But he had an awful lot going on . After all , he was already playing in the band of trumpeter Terence Blanchard , who was also in town . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Since then , I&amp;rsquo ; ve caught Parks at a host of festival gigs &amp;mdash ; with Blanchard in Ottawa ( 2005 ) , with the all-star quartet James Farm in Montreal ( 2009 ) , Ottawa ( 2010 ) and Newport , Rhode Island ( 2011 ) , and then back in Ottawa in 2013 with Kurt Rosenwinkel . I heard Parks play free-flowing solo piano in a church at the 2013 Quebec City Jazz Festival . Most recently , I heard Parks in two small New York clubs &amp;mdash ; at Smalls in 2017 , when he backed saxophonist Remi LeBoeuf , and this spring at Rockwood Music Hall , where he played an opening set with his latest project , Little Big . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The point of this recap is that for a long time &amp;mdash ; Parks is 35 now &amp;mdash ; I&amp;rsquo ; ve had big ears for his special lyricism and creativity , which are audible in every situation , whether he&amp;rsquo ; s applying his gifts to jazz standards or presenting the strains of his original music that has become so influential . While Parks has dazzled on a range of recordings ( I&amp;rsquo ; ll single out his 2017 piano trio album on ECM , &lt;em&gt;Find The Way&lt;/em&gt; ) , it was his 2008 release &lt;em&gt;Invisible Cinema&lt;/em&gt; , Parks&amp;rsquo ; s big-label debut at the age of 24 , that made his generation of jazz lovers and peers go &amp;ldquo ; wow , &amp;rdquo ; with its seminal , standard-setting blend of jazz with indie rock , electronica and more . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Which brings me to this week , when Parks plays tonight ( Friday ) and Saturday night at Montreal&amp;rsquo ; s cozy Diese Onze club with his Little Big group . You can bet that it tempts me to make the road trip . ( Note : Parks will be in Ottawa later this month , on the 27th , in the band of drummer Terri Lyne Carrington when it plays the TD Ottawa Jazz Festival. ) &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Parks&amp;rsquo ; s &lt;em&gt; Little Big&lt;/em&gt; album was released last fall , and I popped it onto &lt;a href= " https : **33;25837;TOOLONG ... list of 2018 's best jazz albums&lt;/a&gt; without hesitation . I never did get around to writing in detail about it , but given Parks&amp;rsquo ; s upcoming Montreal shows , plus a few more recent fan-friendly developments , now&amp;rsquo ; s the time . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Despite a hiatus of more than a decade , &lt;em&gt;Little Big&lt;/em&gt; picks up where &lt;em&gt;Invisible Cinema&lt;/em&gt; left off , exploring how a guitar-heavy , hard-grooving , indie-rock sensibility can intersect with the sophistications and improvisations of jazz . However , Parks has put a new band together , drawing on the distinctive musical voices of guitarist Greg Tuohey , bedrock bassist David &amp;ldquo ; DJ&amp;rdquo ; Ginyard Jr . and drummer Tommy Crane . In the evolution from &lt;em&gt;Invisible Cinema&lt;/em&gt; to &lt;em&gt;Little Big&lt;/em&gt; , Parks&amp;rsquo ; s music burrows deeper into the sheer sonic side of things , with not only the pianist&amp;rsquo ; s arsenal of keyboards but also Tuohey&amp;rsquo ; s electronic manipulations and Crane&amp;rsquo ; s earthy clattering contributing to a larger sound that embraces the best of multiple genres. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Here&amp;rsquo ; s a live version of &lt;em&gt;Kid , &lt;/em&gt; the first of &lt;em&gt;Little Big&lt;/em&gt; &amp;lsquo ; s 15 tracks . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **34;25872;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;25908;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; On the album , it&amp;rsquo ; s a perfect opener , demonstrating the breadth of Parks&amp;rsquo ; s vision and its marriage of accessible , earworm-worthy elements and melodic exoticism . I love that pristine piano and gritty distortion co-exist so successfully on &lt;em&gt;Little Big&lt;/em&gt; , and that the songs and the soloing are so well fused . The big-picture vibe and the detail-level note choices are always aligned so that listeners , however they access music , will likely be hooked . It feels to me that the material and concept are so compelling that at its best , the musicians are making the music &amp;mdash ; they&amp;rsquo ; re channeling it . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Over its many tracks , &lt;em&gt;Little Big&lt;/em&gt; changes gears and switches up its sonic palette . There are other rousing tunes like &lt;em&gt;Kid&lt;/em&gt; &amp;nbsp ; such as the percolating tune &lt;em&gt;Digital Society&lt;/em&gt; that will set heads bobbing. &lt;em&gt;Rising Mind&lt;/em&gt; &amp;nbsp ; has a different kind of urgency , paired with harmonies and a melody that have a special flow . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The slower follow-up &lt;em&gt; Small Planet&lt;/em&gt; was probably my chill-out tune of 2018 : &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 100% " height= " 120 " style= " position : relative ; display : block ; width : 100% ; height : 120px ; " src= " **40;25944;TOOLONG ... allowtransparency= " true " frameborder= " 0 " &gt;&lt;/iframe&gt; &lt;p&gt; Other tunes such as **28;25986;TOOLONG and **26;26016;TOOLONG cast distinctive hypnotic spells . **29;26044;TOOLONG is burbling , reverberant and immersive. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; **26;26075;TOOLONG , which Parks recorded on &lt;a href= " https : **33;26103;TOOLONG ... 2013 piano trio album that he gave away on the internet&lt;/a&gt; , reappears on &lt;em&gt;Little Big&lt;/em&gt; in a more expansive form , with poignant solos by Ginyard and Parks at the piano embedded in the song&amp;rsquo ; s much larger dramatic arc . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Two brief and fetching solo piano pieces &amp;mdash ; the jaunty **26;26138;TOOLONG and more rubato **27;26166;TOOLONG &amp;mdash ; are musical resets on the album . I&amp;rsquo ; ll also say that they bring to mind Keith Jarrett&amp;rsquo ; s heartstring-tugging evocations of Americana , which is not to say that they&amp;rsquo ; re derivative , especially since I&amp;rsquo ; m someone who frequently hears beautiful piano music of all kinds and thinks , &amp;ldquo ; Oh right , Keith Jarrett got there first . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 100% " height= " 120 " style= " position : relative ; display : block ; width : 100% ; height : 120px ; " src= " **40;26195;TOOLONG ... allowtransparency= " true " frameborder= " 0 " &gt;&lt;/iframe&gt; &lt;p&gt; The album&amp;rsquo ; s penultimate tune &lt;em&gt;Good Morning&lt;/em&gt; is a bright-keyed , sun-dappled , drone-y , unabashedly upbeat treat that pops and refreshes all the more given the minor-key moods that &lt;em&gt;Little Big&lt;/em&gt; delves into so effectively . Then , &lt;em&gt;Doors Open&lt;/em&gt; concludes the album concisely and definitively with hymnal cadences and a swell of grand , swaggering feeling . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A note to musicians who want more insight into Parks&amp;rsquo ; s magic : Last month , he generously made available , for free , not only the sheet music for the &lt;em&gt;Little Big&lt;/em&gt; album , but the stems ( sub-groups of audio tracks ) as well , ripe for remixing and more . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; The idea is to make this raw material available to anyone who may be inclined to play around with it , &amp;rdquo ; Parks wrote in a mass email . &amp;ldquo ; That could be as simple as muting the piano track and taking my place in the band , or as substantial as a total remix/re-imagination . I&amp;rsquo ; m super-curious to hear what people might come up with ! You can find the stems &lt;a href= " https : **33;26237;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " **31;26272;TOOLONG ... . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; If you make something with our stems and want to share it non-commercially ( posting on SoundCloud , YouTube , or Instagram , for example ) , I&amp;rsquo ; m all for that ! That&amp;rsquo ; s totally the idea . Just please give us credit . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; So , take Parks up on his largesse if you can &amp;mdash ; piggyback your creativity on his . But don&amp;rsquo ; t forget to buy his music too ( &lt;a href= " https : **33;26305;TOOLONG ... might be the best way to give him his proper share ) , and try to catch him live . I know I will . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " **25;26340;TOOLONG " **32;26367;TOOLONG &lt;br&gt; &lt;a href= " http : //twitter.com/peterhum " &gt;twitter ... &lt;br&gt; &lt;a href= " http : **28;26401;TOOLONG " &gt;o ... &lt;/p&gt; Peter HumFri , 14 Jun 2019 15:38:57 **45;26431;TOOLONG ... Downes , a British jazz musician of dizzying stylistic breadth , plays in West Quebec on Saturdayhttp : **32;26478;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;26512;TOOLONG ... title= " British jazz pianist / organist Kit Downes . Handout photo . " alt= " British jazz pianist / organist Kit Downes . Handout photo . " data-portal-copyright= " Alex Bonney " **28;26548;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1411139 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; To make his 2018 album **29;26578;TOOLONG , British musician Kit Downes stepped back from the pianos and Hammond organs that he usually plays to visit three churches and explore how their chamber organs would inspire him . Out came a mix of uniquely composed and improvised pieces that were nuanced and patient , always highly evocative and even haunting. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **34;26609;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;26645;TOOLONG &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **34;26681;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;26717;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; But if you peruse YouTube , you&amp;rsquo ; ll see clips of Downes playing in different contexts , and almost unrecognizably so . There&amp;rsquo ; s Downes at the piano , swinging hard and playing rhapsodically , with the band of U.S.-born drummer Jeff Williams . There&amp;rsquo ; s Downes&amp;rsquo ; trio called ENEMY , playing more enigmatically and edgily . In another video , Downes is at the organ in the trio called Troyka , making more rocking , visceral music . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **34;26753;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;26789;TOOLONG &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **34;26825;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;26861;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; To a listener trying to grasp the big picture , Downes&amp;rsquo ; musical breadth is practically dizzying &amp;mdash ; certainly enough to flag the prospect of his solo piano concert Saturday night as part of the Pontiac Enchant&amp;eacute ; concert series at Venturing Hills Farms , an intimate , rural performance space about 40 minutes from Ottawa . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But if the 33-year-old , who has won the BBC Jazz Award and been nominated for the United Kingdom&amp;rsquo ; s Mercury Music Award , lives up to his reputation , the concert is likely to be as beautiful as it is unpredictable . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Below , Downes , who will also play this month with ENEMY at jazz festivals in Rochester , New York , and Toronto , delves into the aesthetic that allows him to flourish across a wide spectrum . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Q : This month , you 're playing solo concerts , with ENEMY , duos with bassist Petter Eldh and Jeff Williams ' group . Tell me about the different satisfactions that come from these different situations . &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;A : &lt;/strong&gt; In all of these situations I feel really free musically .
