I feel very lucky for this . I have been playing with Petter , James , Jeff and Sam for many years and they are good friends as well as important people to me musically . I have learnt a lot from playing with them and continue to do so every time we have a concert together . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; These lineups are more on the jazz side of the playing I do . But I would say the same things about playing with folk fiddler Aidan O&amp;rsquo ; Rourke , turntablist Shiva Feshareki , cellist Lucy Railton &amp;mdash ; all good friends and inspiring musicians . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **34;26897;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;26933;TOOLONG &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **34;26969;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;27005;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Q : You mentioned your " jazz side , " which implies a " non-jazz side . " How important are musical genres to you ? Do you still think of yourself as a jazz musician and what you do as jazz ? &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;A : &lt;/strong&gt; I think of myself as a jazz musician to a degree , as that was the first music I really fell in love with , and I owe a lot , everything really , to it for that . It taught me so many things and really made we want to be a musician . So I am very conscious of that . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; However , I love lots of types of music and am always trying to develop my playing and process in new ways , to find different things . Also , aesthetically , it feels a little jarring to call all my music one genre &amp;mdash ; but I imagine 99 per cent of musicians aren&amp;rsquo ; t that fond of being called one thing either . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It&amp;rsquo ; s an important question for any jazz musician , and in part , the way you deal with that question can be quite defining for you as a musician . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Q : What 's the allure of playing not just piano , but also organ , keyboards and even church organ ? &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;A : &lt;/strong&gt; I feel the same about these instruments as I do about genre . Maybe it&amp;rsquo ; s part of being one of the first generations to grow up with the internet , and to have access to so much music so quickly , but I think it helped me when I started having the confidence to pick up new instruments , or old childhood instruments . By being a little broader in my practice , I think it helped me to work out what I was trying to say . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Q : What is constant about your playing regardless of the context ? What are you always striving for ? &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;A : &lt;/strong&gt; I have always been interested in improvisers that have a kind of melodic economy &amp;mdash ; a very direct and understated way of expressing their ideas . Also , players that are constantly trying to make sounds , compositions , lines , harmony that haven&amp;rsquo ; t been heard before . Pianists like Thelonious Monk , Jason Moran , Craig Taborn and Keith Jarrett , for example . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Q : What initially drew you to music ? And to jazz ? &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;A : &lt;/strong&gt; I first sung in church . That&amp;rsquo ; s where I first heard the organ and started playing it . I am not religious , and wasn&amp;rsquo ; t then either , but something about the atmosphere of Norwich Cathedral , the silence and the reflection of the services I would sing at for five years , had a profound effect on me . I got into improvising on the organ at first , not in any particular style , just enjoying the sounds . Then my mum thought I would like jazz because of that , so she bought me Night Train . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Q : Is there a British jazz aesthetic or sound , compared to how jazz sounds in other countries ? &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;A : &lt;/strong&gt; Many UK musicians were important to me growing up , particularly John Taylor and Django Bates . Though I was and still am very interested by a lot of music from Norway , Germany , the Netherlands , and all over Europe . All that music really connects with what happens over here , as we are European too ! &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; I would say all the people I really like sound like themselves . They are undoubtedly influenced by where they are from , but as outward-looking and curious people , they are influenced by many things from all over the world . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; So I don&amp;rsquo ; t really believe in the &amp;ldquo ; sound of a country&amp;rdquo ; thing . Though I do , of course , believe in musical family &amp;mdash ; musicians hanging out and influencing each other . This is not bound by nationality , thankfully . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Kit Downes&lt;br&gt; Pontiac Enchant ? Concert Series&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;br&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Where : &lt;/strong&gt; Venturing Hills Farm , &amp;nbsp ; 440 Chemin Cr&amp;eacute ; gheur , &amp;nbsp ; Luskville , Que. &lt;br&gt; &lt;strong&gt;When : &lt;/strong&gt; Saturday , June 15 , 7:30 p.m. &lt;br&gt; **36;27041;TOOLONG : $30 at &lt;a href= " http : //pontiacenchante.ca " &gt;pontiacen ... &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " **25;27079;TOOLONG " **32;27106;TOOLONG &lt;br&gt; &lt;a href= " http : //twitter.com/peterhum " &gt;twitter ... &lt;/p&gt; Peter HumWed , 12 Jun 2019 23:49:51 **45;27140;TOOLONG ... ArtsOttawa Jazz Festival warns fans that NCC could boot it from Confederation Park next yearhttp : **36;27187;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;27225;TOOLONG ... title= " The opening night of the 2011 Ottawa Jazz Festival in Confederation Park " alt= " The opening night of the 2011 Ottawa Jazz Festival in Confederation Park " data-portal-copyright= " Tony Caldwell " **28;27261;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1388372 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; The TD Ottawa Jazz Festival has heard loud and clear from festival-goers that they are against the possibility of the National Capital Commission kicking the event out of its long-standing site , Confederation Park , next year , the festival&amp;rsquo ; s executive producer said Monday . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; It would be heartbreaking to not have a major music event in that park , &amp;rdquo ; Catherine O&amp;rsquo ; Grady said in an interview . &amp;ldquo ; I just can&amp;rsquo ; t imagine us not being there . The thought of it makes me so sad . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; O&amp;rsquo ; Grady alerted festival-goers via email on Friday that she had recently met with Tobi Nussbaum , the NCC&amp;rsquo ; s new CEO , to brief him on her event&amp;rsquo ; s plans for a 40th-anniversary edition in Confederation Park in 2020 . According to O&amp;rsquo ; Grady , Nussbaum told her it was not a &amp;ldquo ; given&amp;rdquo ; that the festival would return there , as the NCC&amp;rsquo ; s use of the park is under review . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; When the festival stages its 39th annual edition in late June this year , its main outdoor concerts will be in Marion Dewar Plaza outside Ottawa City Hall , returning to the location it used in 2018 . The festival moved to the plaza because of sewer tunnel construction in the park . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; O&amp;rsquo ; Grady said that as a result of her email , more than 800 festival-goers signed a petition last weekend in support of the festival returning to Confederation Park . &amp;ldquo ; That&amp;rsquo ; s an overwhelming positive response , &amp;rdquo ; O&amp;rsquo ; Grady said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Now I have to take that back to my board and work on strategy going forward . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Last year in the plaza , the festival drew smaller crowds for its main stage shows than it did in past years at Confederation Park . From 1989 to 2017 , the festival made full use of the park , which holds several thousand more people compared to the plaza . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In her email , O&amp;rsquo ; Grady called Confederation Park &amp;ldquo ; a beautiful setting by the canal and among the trees in a safe , urban environment that is easily accessible by bus , bike , and on foot . &amp;rdquo ; She also lauded the sound quality in the park and its proximity to the festival&amp;rsquo ; s indoor venues at the National Arts Centre . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; O&amp;rsquo ; Grady said Nussbaum did not categorically say that the jazz festival would not return to the park . But she did say that negotiating the return of the festival to the park &amp;ldquo ; is going to be a bit of a battle , is my understanding . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Dominique Huras , an NCC strategic communications advisor , said the &lt;span lang= " en-CA " &gt;NCC is planning a review of its Core Area Sector Plan , which includes Confederation Park , and that the review would involve widespread consultations . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; &lt;span lang= " en-CA " &gt;Over the years , Confederation Park has been a victim of its own success , " Huras noted . The park has deteriorated " as a result of its overuse by special events combined with activities that are not always suitable for the park 's features , " she continued . In particular , the park 's trees , some of which are rare and endangered , are suffering from soil compaction because of the many large-scale events that take place in the park , Huras said . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;hr&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;ALSO IN THE NEWS : &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;27291;TOOLONG ... ArtsLocal NewsAudiophile label Newvelle 's offer on Record Store Day a treat for jazz fanshttp : **36;27504;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;27542;TOOLONG ... title= " Drummer Jack DeJohnette at the 2017 Ottawa Jazz Festival " alt= " Drummer Jack DeJohnette at the 2017 Ottawa Jazz Festival " data-portal-copyright= " Dan Nawrocki " **28;27578;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1027900 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; I can&amp;rsquo ; t think of a better way for a jazz fan to mark Record Store Day &amp;mdash ; Saturday , April 13 &amp;mdash ; than to treat him or herself to some vinyl from this weekend&amp;rsquo ; s unprecedented sale that Newvelle Records . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Until now , the exclusive Paris-based label , which launched in 2016 , has sold its top-quality recordings by such leading musicians as Jack DeJohnette , John Patitucci , Lionel Loueke and many others through annual subscriptions of $400 ( all figures in U.S. dollars ) . Its customers receive six 180-gram LPs each year and Newvelle does not make its music available on CD or as downloads , never mind through streaming services . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Admittedly , that subscription fee can be a barrier . But this weekend , NewVelle for the first time is selling single copies of some of their releases in their back catalog for $60 each , with free shipping . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Among the albums for sale are recordings by DeJohnette , Patittuci , Loueke , tenor saxophonist Noah Preminger , pianists Don Friedman , Leo Genovese and Jon Cowherd , bassists Rufus Reid and Chris Tordini , and trumpeter Jason Palmer . The full list of albums on offer is &lt;a href= " https : **33;27608;TOOLONG ... . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; I can vouch for Preminger&amp;rsquo ; s album Some Other Time , a typically ear-catching release that features the saxophonist with guitarist Ben Monder , bassist Larry Grenadier and drummer Billy Hart , tackling such pieces as Duke Ellington&amp;rsquo ; s Melancholia , Try A Little Tenderness , and the Leonard Bernstein-penned title track . Also excellent is Cowherd&amp;rsquo ; s album Gateway , which presents his originals as performed in a quartet that includes guitarist Steve Cardenas , bassist Tony Scherr and his most steadfast collaborator , drummer Brian Blade . That disc reveals how Cowherd&amp;rsquo ; s musical vision manifests itself outside of Blade&amp;rsquo ; s Fellowship Band , the context in which it&amp;rsquo ; s most often heard . Finally , I can recommend Reid&amp;rsquo ; s album Terrestial Dance , a refined but swinging listen that combines Reid&amp;rsquo ; s trio with pianist Steve Allee and drummer Duduka Da Fonseca and the Sirius Quartet . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **34;27643;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;27679;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; https : //www.youtube.com/watch ? timecontinue=2 ... &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **34;27715;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;27751;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; Last year , Newvelle sent me review copies of these albums , which continue to impress me with their warm , vivid sound and inspired performances . It&amp;rsquo ; s hard to go back to the mass market vinyl in my collection , even if the music contained therein is fantastic . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; As well , Newvelle&amp;rsquo ; s packaging is gorgeous and artful , while the liner notes have been shifted to its &lt;a href= " https : **27;27787;TOOLONG " &gt;w ... . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; While there&amp;rsquo ; s no putting mp3s heard through earbuds , or worse , streaming jazz that pays the smallest of pittances to musicians , back in the bottle , there&amp;rsquo ; s some consolation in a label such as Newvelle caring so clearly about the music and the artists who make it . In just a few days , and for just a few days , it will easier to show your support for the business and its cause . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " **25;27816;TOOLONG " **32;27843;TOOLONG &lt;br&gt; &lt;a href= " http : //twitter.com/petehrum " &gt;twitter ... &lt;/p&gt; Peter HumThu , 11 Apr 2019 11:02:18 **45;27877;TOOLONG ... Blake CD reviewedhttp : **32;27924;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;27958;TOOLONG ... title= " Seamus Blake playing at the 2015 TD Ottawa Jazz Festival at Confederation Park in Ottawa Tuesday June 23 , 2015 " alt= " Seamus Blake playing at the 2015 TD Ottawa Jazz Festival at Confederation Park in Ottawa Tuesday June 23 , 2015 " data-portal-copyright= " Tony Caldwell " **28;27994;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1376958 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Guardians of the Heart Machine ( Whirlwind Recordings ) &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;br&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Seamus Blake&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; By now , it should come as no surprise &amp;mdash ; especially to Canadian jazz fans who claim his as one of their own&amp;nbsp ; &amp;mdash ; when saxophonist Seamus Blake puts out another great record . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The 48-year-old has been impressing us for half his life now , since his distinguished debut disc The Call came out in 1994 on Criss Cross Jazz . Subsequent albums on that Dutch label , plus the 5Passion and Smalls Live and Jazz Eyes label , have reaffirmed Blake&amp;rsquo ; s top-tier abilities as an improviser and his knack for writing fresh and earworm-worthy tunes . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Released in mid-March , Blake&amp;rsquo ; s latest album Guardians of the Heart Machine extends this streak . Blake is as commanding and fluent as you&amp;rsquo ; ve come to expect , and its cache of the saxophonist&amp;rsquo ; s originals , whether they&amp;rsquo ; re hard-hitting and groove-forward or more plaintive and even vulnerable , are catchy enough to demand repeated listens . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; What is new about Guardians of the Heart Machine is Blake&amp;rsquo ; s new band , which consists of the young French-born players Tony Tixier at the piano , bassist Florent Nisse and drummer Gautier Garrigue , who are in their mid-30s . While they&amp;rsquo ; re far from jazz-world household names , these French musicians give Blake and his music all the support needed and then some , all the while injecting the proceedings with a new-generation sensibility . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In the press release for the album , Blake said he developed the music with these new colleagues in mind . &amp;ldquo ; My idea was to bridge what I consider elements of European and American styles , writing music I like to play , but also with a European sensibility , , including classical harmony and certain types of groove . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I envisioned the strengths of each of the band members , having already toured with them , &amp;rdquo ; he continued . &amp;ldquo ; They&amp;rsquo ; re versatile and adept , with the foundation , openness and energy to head in different directions ; so their creative contributions became part of the spirit of the record . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The proof is in the listening . Here&amp;rsquo ; s the rousing title track , which opens the album : &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **34;28024;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;28060;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; And here&amp;rsquo ; s the second tune Vaporbabe , which despite its plaintive vibe feature some of Blake&amp;rsquo ; s signature saxophone heroism : &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **34;28096;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;28132;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; Sneaky D coasts along on a chattering contemporary groove and has an infectiously poppy vibe . On I&amp;rsquo ; m Okay , Blake covers a gorgeous ballad by keyboardist Eddie del Barrio that Stan Getz recorded on his swan-song release People Time . Blake&amp;rsquo ; s version has the tenderness and gravitas to withstand comparison to Getz&amp;rsquo ; s sublime work . Here&amp;rsquo ; s a live version from the quartet&amp;rsquo ; s 2017 tour , in advance of its recording session : &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **33;28168;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;28203;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; Lanota , which follows , changes the channel to offer music that&amp;rsquo ; s more tense , pulsating and unfettered . Tixier&amp;rsquo ; s solo in particular shows off what he , Nisse and Garrigue can do when they&amp;rsquo ; re left to their own devices and the musical borders are pushed back . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Wandering Aengus is a simple and unabashedly pretty tune . On it , the purity of Blake&amp;rsquo ; s sound and his direction emotional appeal shine through , while Tixier takes a swirling , winning solo with lots of poise . More bubbly is the samba-ish Betty In Rio , which fuses the harmonic inspiration of Benny Golson&amp;rsquo ; s bopping hit Along Came Betty with the rhythmic effervescence of Brazil . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Pianist Tixier&amp;rsquo ; s contribution Blues for the Real Human Beings is a slow , loping tune that&amp;rsquo ; s lyrical and uplifting . Blake closes his album with a vocal lament , putting down his saxophone to sing his dour , chastening tune The Blasted Heath . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; I&amp;rsquo ; ll probably always think of Blake as the formidable arriviste that he was in the early &amp;rsquo ; 90s . I first heard and met him then , and he blew my mind . But Guardians of the Heart Machine is forcing me , and in the most enjoyable way , to hear him also as a mentor and leading light for today&amp;rsquo ; s wave of up-and-comers. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " **25;28239;TOOLONG " **32;28266;TOOLONG &lt;br&gt; &lt;a href= " http : //twitter.com/peterhum " &gt;twitter ... &lt;br&gt; &lt;a href= " http : **28;28300;TOOLONG " &gt;o ... &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;nbsp ; &lt;/p&gt; Peter HumFri , 05 Apr 2019 19:24:53 **45;28330;TOOLONG ... Roberts and Lage Lund CD reviewedhttp : **32;28377;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;28411;TOOLONG ... title= " Guitarist Lage Lund and pianist Bryn Roberts recently released a sparkling duo album , Hide the Moon and the Stars " alt= " Guitarist Lage Lund and pianist Bryn Roberts recently released a sparkling duo album , Hide the Moon and the Stars " data-portal-copyright= " James Bizon " **28;28447;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1372749 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Bryn Roberts and Lage Lund ( BMR Music ) &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;br&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Hide The Moon and the Stars&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; If you want to know what compatibility sounds like , give a listen to the very enjoyable album Hide The Moon and the Stars , from pianist Bryn Roberts and guitarist Lage Lund . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Released last fall , the duo&amp;rsquo ; s second album revisits the special simpatico between the Portland-based Roberts and New York-based Lund , who forged their enviable musical bond more than a decade ago when Roberts was based in Brooklyn and the pair ran with the same fast company . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Some say that in jazz , piano and guitar are supposed to have a tough time melding , given the likelihood of notes clashing with so many chords being plucked out of the air . But if this pairing involves an intrinsic challenge , Roberts and Lund certainly figured out how to overcome it . Throughout , the album teems with lush , refined interactions and staunch cooperation . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Indeed , over its 10 tracks &amp;mdash ; four penned by Lund and six by Roberts &amp;mdash ; the album , in as few words as possible , is also unwaveringly pretty . Not ever in a treacly way , mind you , and in pieces such as Roberts&amp;rsquo ; s slinky groover Cheers For The Call and especially the pianist&amp;rsquo ; s tune-cum-soundscape Alternative Facts , there are hints of disquietude . Still , Roberts and Lund are so committed to lyrical playing that even when the album departs from the overtly gorgeous , the music nonetheless has a singing quality . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The album begins and ends with pieces by Lund that have an appealing folk-y vibe . Both the opener California and the closer Elsa and Anna resonate with the strains of Americana . Given that Lund is a native of Norway while Roberts is a Canadian , you might posit that they&amp;rsquo ; re not speaking their musical mother tongues &amp;mdash ; but then , jazz must make its adepts multilingual . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Lund also composed the plaintive waltz Brent Rogers ( a kind of play on his collaborator&amp;rsquo ; s name ) , whose chords continue to surprise after multiple listenings , plus the haunting title track : &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **33;28477;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;28512;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; Roberts&amp;rsquo ; s other tunes include the lovely waltz Amaryllis&amp;hellip ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **34;28548;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;28584;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; &amp;hellip ; from which you can tell that Roberts has a keen appreciation of what makes the great , enduring jazz standards tick and can make sparkling personal statements from that wisdom . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Speaking of great , enduring jazz standards , the album&amp;rsquo ; s sole cover is a rendition of You Go To My Head , which everyone from Louis Armstrong to Clifford Baker to Chet Baker to Billie Holiday has made his or hers . With their jaunty version , Lund and Roberts place their own substantial imprint on the classic love song . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **34;28620;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;28656;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; Rounding out the album are two small but poised solo pieces by Roberts that act as windups to the pieces that follow them . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; More often than not , more than 2,800 miles separate Lund and Roberts . But as Hide the Moon and the Stars suggests , music lovers should be very glad when they close that geographical gap and sublimely join forces . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;h2 class= " cat-head short " **40;28692;TOOLONG ... &lt;ul class= " relatedlinks " &gt; &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;28734;TOOLONG ... Lund 's Terrible Animals CD **29;28769;TOOLONG &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;28800;TOOLONG ... long list of 2018 's best Canadian **25;28835;TOOLONG &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;28862;TOOLONG ... comings and goings of pianist Bryn **28;28897;TOOLONG &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;28927;TOOLONG ... Roberts at GigSpace ( 2014 concert review ) &lt;/a&gt;&lt;/li&gt; &lt;/ul&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " **25;28962;TOOLONG " **32;28989;TOOLONG &lt;br&gt; &lt;a href= " http : //twitter.com/peterhum " &gt;twitter ... &lt;br&gt; &lt;a href= " http : **28;29023;TOOLONG " &gt;o ... &lt;/p&gt; Peter HumMon , 01 Apr 2019 18:36:01 **45;29053;TOOLONG ... Lund CD reviewedhttp : **32;29100;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;29134;TOOLONG ... title= " New York-based jazz guitarist Lage Lund " alt= " New York-based jazz guitarist Lage Lund " data-portal-copyright= " Jimmy &amp; Dena Katz " **28;29170;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1358313 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Terrible Animals ( Criss Cross Jazz ) &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;br&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Lage Lund&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The cover for Terrible Animals , the latest album from New York-based guitarist Lage Lund , has a decidedly retro feel to it . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Jazz aficionados will recognize the cover&amp;rsquo ; s red border and casual sketch of Lund as a nod to the look of selected Criss Cross Jazz album covers from the early 1980s , i.e. , a time when Lund , who is 40 now , was still a pre-schooler in Norway , years away from picking up his first guitar . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;nbsp ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **34;29200;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;29236;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; That said , the album&amp;rsquo ; s renditions of 10 original compositions by Lund are anything but old-fashioned . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; They have a contemporary cast to them , and are frequently as appealingly lyrical and singable as they are intensely grooving . Furthermore , beyond the rich and vivid in-studio playing by Lund , pianist Sullivan Fortner , bassist Larry Grenadier and drummer Tyshawn Sorey &amp;mdash ; an improvisatory dream team if ever there was one &amp;mdash ; the guitarist after the session augmented the music with overdubbed counter-lines and textures that make his compositions more expansive , swirling , quirky and even mysterious . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Indeed , given the album&amp;rsquo ; s title and the black or surreal humour I detect in the video clip above , I&amp;rsquo ; m tempted to say that with his effects pedals and prepared guitar , Lund has added bleats , warbles , whooshes and assorted evocative sounds that make his music more ominous and at times disturbingly dreamy . ( Is his tune Haitian Ballad really about frogs feasting on bugs ? ) &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; I would have loved Terrible Animals even without those post-session enhancements that make it so distinctive without obstructing its live-off-the-floor content . Fortner , Grenadier and Sorey make up a rhythm section that strikes me as surprising , given their playing histories and associations , yet incredibly potent and even inspired . Fortner and Sorey gush with virtuosic creativity throughout Lund&amp;rsquo ; s winning material , while Grenadier heroically anchors the music and steps forward with commanding solos as needed . Throughout , Lund&amp;rsquo ; s guitarwork &amp;mdash ; or rather , the album&amp;rsquo ; s primary layer of guitar &amp;mdash ; glows with sinuous melodicism , delivering profundity and adventure . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The album&amp;rsquo ; s opener , Hard Eights , alternates tense minor-key grinding with the release of a quasi jazz-shuffle . Aquanauts is a fantastic burbling piece with a truly eye-widening solo by Sorey that runs roughshod over its 5/4 grid . ( Here&amp;rsquo ; s a trio performance of the piece with bassist Matt Brewer and drummer Justin Faulkner throwing themselves into the music. ) &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; https : //www.youtube.com/watch ? v=IJBMhN5wooY &a; ... &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; ( By the way , on Tuesday night at the Jazz Standard in New York , it will be Faulkner , along with Fortner and Grenadier , diving again into Lund&amp;rsquo ; s music to celebrate the recent release of Terrible Animals. ) &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Suppressions is a churning , incident-filled piece that is several generations removed from one of its ancestors , John Coltrane&amp;rsquo ; s Impressions . Haitian Ballad is a plaintive , airy composition with a little bit of menace to it . Ray Ray is a slow , lilting piece with special gravitas . Octoberry is short and enigmatic , with a clanky sonic signature and a questing solo by Grenadier . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Brasilia is a jaunty piece with a sparkling , poised solo by Fortner that could not have been improved upon had he composed it in advance . The title track , which is short , dour and rumbling , is sandwiched between two sunnier tunes , Take It Eas and the disc-closing We Are There Yet . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In a short e-mail exchange , I asked Lund if he thought of Terrible Animals as more of a studio creation than a live-off-the-floor recording , or perhaps as some kind of hybrid . &amp;ldquo ; It&amp;rsquo ; s a hybrid , I suppose , &amp;rdquo ; he replied , before offering an analogy that was more incisive . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; You could say that basically all my previous records were documentaries whereas this one is based on a true story , &amp;rdquo ; Lund wrote . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; I like the sound of that almost as much as like the sound of Terrible Animals , an album that creates its own unique truth , thanks to a formidable , free-spirited team effort and Lund&amp;rsquo ; s brave imagination . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " **25;29272;TOOLONG " **32;29299;TOOLONG &lt;br&gt; &lt;a href= " http : //twitter.com/peterhum " &gt;twitter ... &lt;br&gt; &lt;a href= " http : **28;29333;TOOLONG " &gt;o ... &lt;/p&gt; Peter HumTue , 26 Mar 2019 13:52:33 **45;29363;TOOLONG ... the piano , Gentiane MG transcends emotions and thoughts to plumb the unknownhttp : **33;29410;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;29445;TOOLONG ... title= " Montreal jazz pianist Gentiane MG , who plays the NAC Fourth Stage on March 28 , 2019. " alt= " Montreal jazz pianist Gentiane MG , who plays the NAC Fourth Stage on March 28 , 2019. " data-portal-copyright= " " **28;29481;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1369967 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Montreal jazz pianist Gentiane Michaud-Gagnon ( or Gentiane MG as she abbreviates her name ) ignited her career with a bang less than two years ago and shows no sign of slowing her momentum . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The 27-year-old released Eternal Cycle , her first album as a leader , in the summer of 2017 . That album of original compositions for her trio was picked by CBC as one of the year&amp;rsquo ; s best Canadian jazz releases . For 2018-19 , Michaud-Gagnon was named Radio-Canada&amp;rsquo ; s jazz discovery . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; On Thursday , she brings her trio to the NAC Fourth Stage , in advance of the upcoming release of her sophomore album Wonderland , to be released in early May . Below , Michaud-Gagnon , a composer and player who&amp;rsquo ; s unafraid of exploring complex musical and psychological realms , discusses how she&amp;rsquo ; s progressed as an artist . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Q : You recorded your second album with your trio in January . How does this record differ from your trio 's debut disc ? &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A : My second album is in a way a development of the first one , while being quite different . There are two pieces in Eternal Cycle that I think initiated the creative process for these new 13 pieces . The writing is more developed , the pieces are contrasting and each has a different rhythmic concept . I wanted to explore different forms . There are some short interludes , some through-composed pieces , as well as a suite in three parts . I also searched for new ways of creating improvised sections in order to lead the trio and myself to new territories . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Q : How would you describe the emotional content of your music ? &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A : Music is a language that goes way beyond the reality of words . I think each person has its very particular way of perceiving the message of one given piece of music . My short answer would be that I tend to be attracted by darkness when it comes to art in general , although there is a lot of nuance to that idea . Darkness can&amp;rsquo ; t exist without brightness . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; When I compose , I think a lot about the idea of inaccessible feelings , of contradictions , of emotions that I can&amp;rsquo ; t control . Each piece has a real meaning to me based on where I was in my life at the moment I composed it . How emotions can be translated to music is still a concept that I find mystical and that I don&amp;rsquo ; t quite understand rationally . I just know that music is the only way for me to transcend a whole world of emotions , thoughts , and to dig further into the unknown . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Q : How significant are the titles of your compositions to you ? What should a listener take from such evocative titles as Forgiveness , Repentance , Sanctuary , Erased Self , Empty Canvas and Heavy Weight ? &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A : Titles are hard to find and are very important to me . Often , while I am in the process of writing , I start conceptualizing what the piece means to me . Then , I try finding a phrase or an idea based in reality that describes it . Once the concept is more defined , I search for the right word , or the right few words . That last step is the hardest . Most of the time , I have the title before the piece is finished , although sometimes it requires more research . Other times I get lucky and the title comes first and starts the whole creative process . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; When it comes to albums , I try to find the title as early on as possible so I can write pieces that are all part of the bigger concept . Eternal Cycle was expressing deep emotions that sometimes go away but that always come back . Wonderland represents hope . It&amp;rsquo ; s a place where things can be different , a place where beauty still exists and where the cycle might be breaking . Forgiveness , Sanctuary , Erased Self , Empty Canvas and Heavy Weight represent many of the necessary steps toward that Wonderland . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **34;29511;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;29547;TOOLONG &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **34;29583;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;29619;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Q : Beyond your trio , you 're also playing with some musicians a generation older than yourself , such as Yannick Rieu and Frank Lozano . What do these experiences give you ? What do you have in common with and how do you differ from jazz players who might be twice your age ? &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A : I feel blessed and grateful for the opportunity to share and to learn from these incredible musicians . Playing with such musicians has made me grow enormously . They have more experience , they&amp;rsquo ; ve played with more people than I have , and they have more knowledge . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; What we have in common is something that I think we&amp;rsquo ; re all trying to reach . Something that goes beyond reality and that touches spirituality . A state in which only music really means anything and where us as mortal human beings doesn&amp;rsquo ; t matter so much , at least while we play . I think it&amp;rsquo ; s in those moments especially that age doesn&amp;rsquo ; t mean anything anymore . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Montreal has a very rich jazz community in that sense . Older musicians share with younger musicians , and I am always amazed to see how age is transcended by the fact that we share something very meaningful to us all and this alone is enough to let go of our differences . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **34;29655;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;29691;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Q : What artistic and career goals do you have for the rest of 2019 ? &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A : First of all , we will launch my new CD Wonderland on May 6 . I already have concerts booked around Quebec for the rest of the year and we&amp;rsquo ; re working on a Canadian tour for the trio in the fall . I love traveling . I am currently working on some new music for a duo with saxophonist Frank Lozano , and regularly playing around Montreal with different projects . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; My short-term artistic goals are always evolving . I have spent the better part of the last year working toward the release of my upcoming album , and am now finally able to re-focus my energy towards working on my personal musical development . But in the end , I think it&amp;rsquo ; s all serving the same purpose : to keep growing as a performer and composer and to continue sharing with other musicians . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Gentiane MG Trio&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;br&gt; &lt;strong&gt;When : &lt;/strong&gt; Thursday , March 28 , 8:30 p.m. &lt;br&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Where : &lt;/strong&gt; NAC Fourth Stage &lt;br&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Tickets : &lt;/strong&gt; $19 at &lt;a href= " http : //nac-cna.ca " &gt;nac-cna.ca&lt;/a &; ... or the NAC Fourth Stage &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " **25;29727;TOOLONG " **32;29754;TOOLONG &lt;br&gt; &lt;a href= " http : //twitter.com/peterhum " &gt;twitter ... &lt;/p&gt; Peter HumThu , 21 Mar 2019 19:06:10 **45;29788;TOOLONG ...
